# Implementation notes — how to actually fetch each site

> ## 🔴 READ FIRST (2026-08-30): CLASSIFICATION, LOCATION, DEDUP AND TRACKER WRITES ARE NOW CODE
>
> They live in **`bin/`** and are **authoritative over any prose in this file.**
> Import them; do not re-derive them into a scratchpad.
>
> 🔴 **Run from the project root** — this bootstrap and every documented command are root-relative.
>
> ```python
> import sys; sys.path.insert(0, "bin")
> import jobscan_match, jobscan_location, jobscan_dedup, jobscan_tracker
> ```
>
> `jobscan_tracker.SEEN_JOBS` / `.INDEED_SEEN` resolve themselves off the module's own location, so
> they stay correct even if a group changes directory. Never hardcode a tracker path.
>
> | Module | Replaces |
> |---|---|
> | `jobscan_match.py` | every hand-rolled classifier / exclusion regex. **The bar is now mechanical with NO exclusions** — see the rewritten `rules.md` §1. |
> | `jobscan_location.py` | the location-field traps catalogued below (ISO2-vs-state collisions, ISO3 prefixes, `Remote, MT`, trailing bare `US`, Centene `Remote-MO`, coordinate pairs, semicolon multi-site) |
> | `jobscan_dedup.py` | `dedup_keys` / `primary_id` / `base_id`. **Dedup is URL-based now**, not stable-ID — the operator ruled 2026-08-30 that a repost under a new URL must be reported. |
> | `jobscan_tracker.py` | the per-run `tracker_write.py`, with the `.bak` + post-write assertions built in |
>
> `bin/test_jobscan.py` is the regression suite (**231 cases** after the 2026-08-31 r3 location
> and req-ID work — it was 167 at the start of that run, and the figures 126 and 149 recorded
> elsewhere in this file are stale). **When a run finds a new trap, add the case there FIRST,
> then fix the module** — on r3 the 25 new location cases were confirmed FAILING before a line
> of the module changed, which is what proves a case is testing something.
>
> It also carries **three structural guards** that are not case-table entries:
> `check_core_items_are_net_anchored` (a widened bar must not outrun the enumeration net),
> `check_iso2_sets_are_disjoint` (a US state abbreviation in `NON_US_ISO2` is a silent US
> drop), and `check_retitle_survives_the_reqid_fold` (the req-ID fold must not swallow a
> retitle). The prose below is
> retained as the *why* — it documents the incidents each rule came from — but where prose
> and code disagree, the code wins and the prose is stale.
>
> 🔴🔴 **THE SUITE PASSING IS NOT EVIDENCE THAT A CODE PATH WORKS — ONLY THAT THE CASES
> PRESENT PASS.** *(2026-08-31.)* `jobscan_location.is_us()`'s structured `country_code`
> branch accepted **only** `US` / `USA` / `UNITED STATES` and returned **non-US** for
> everything else — including Workday CXS's actual value, `"United States of America"`.
> That silently drops **every US posting on every CXS tenant** for any caller obeying the
> "an authoritative country field wins" rule below. **The then-104-case suite passed clean
> throughout, because it contained ZERO cases exercising `country_code` at all.** Fixed
> 2026-08-31: letters-only comparison, plus the US territories PR/GU/VI/AS/MP, which the
> *text* path already read as US via `US_STATE_ABBR` — the two paths were contradicting
> each other on the same posting. **Standing lesson: when you ship a module, audit which of
> its parameters have NO fixture at all. An unexercised branch is where this class of defect
> lives, and it fails silently by construction — a dropped posting leaves no trace.**
>
> Context: this project previously re-derived ~8,000 lines of Python per run from these notes
> and threw it away, which is why the same defects recurred ("eight defects found in the shared
> helper", 2026-08-30). That is what `bin/` exists to stop.

> **📐 Maintenance rule (added 2026-08-20 r2).** This file hit 163KB because two things accumulated inside it: dated scan narrative, and stacked "method corrections" sections. Both are now controlled — keep them that way.
> 1. **No scan narrative in this file.** Dated write-ups go in `scan-history.md`. This file holds *method only* — if a sentence says what a scan found rather than how to fetch something, it belongs elsewhere.
> 2. **Fold corrections down; don't stack them.** When a scan adds a corrections section, the *previous* one gets merged into the per-ATS recipe or per-company entry it corrects, and deleted. **At most one corrections section should exist at a time.** *Status 2026-08-30: **the rule is satisfied — exactly one corrections section exists (`2026-08-30`).** The `2026-08-29` section was folded down and deleted this pass. **The fold was VERIFIED, not assumed** — each of its 14 pointer-carrying items was grepped for its own distinctive token before deletion. 🟢 **Result: 14 of 14 resolved fully — the FIRST CLEAN FOLD ON RECORD**, against 4-of-10 on 2026-08-29 and 6-of-10 the pass before. **The cause is identifiable and repeatable: the 2026-08-29 section was written as a pure index by an author who homed each claim BEFORE writing its pointer.** That is the practice to keep — the index form is not just cheap to fold, it is what made the fold trustworthy. ⚠️ **This does NOT retire the verify-every-pointer rule** — one clean pass is a sample, and the two before it failed at 40% and 60%. Three 2026-08-29 items were **superseded** by the 2026-08-30 run rather than merely folded (Wendy's body-size gate, the iCIMS facet-containment finding, the Under Armour stop row) and were corrected at their homes rather than carried forward. Historical status 2026-08-29: 4 of 10 resolved; the unhomed ones were the Quest sitemap death, Marriott small-keywords-first, UHG's `strict=False`, Airbnb's `total_rows`, the Under Armour stop row, and most of the blocked-signature updates. Historical status 2026-08-25:*
> * The `2026-08-24 r2` section was folded down and deleted this pass. It had been written as a **pure index** — every item carried a "→ *X row*" pointer and its header said outright "the authoritative text is now in the recipe/row named, not here" — so folding it meant verifying the pointers and deleting the index, not re-homing prose. **That is the cheapest possible fold, and it is worth writing the next corrections section the same way: full text at the home, a one-line pointer in the section.** Two r2 claims were superseded by the 2026-08-25 run and corrected at their homes rather than carried forward: **eBay's null trap is NOT "cleared by r2"** (it recurred — it is per-run), and **Dick's 338 reconciliation is closed** (the 338 is stale; a wider 22-keyword list pools 99 with every keyword exact). Next pass: if a new date opens a section, fold `2026-08-25` down the same way.*
> 3. **A correction that contradicts a recipe must edit that recipe, not just sit above it.** Two recipes below (Etsy's `data-count`, Airbnb's "Showing X-Y of Z") stated the *opposite* of what a later scan proved, and the correction only existed in the narrative — so moving the narrative out would have left the wrong instruction standing alone. Fix the recipe in place.

Direct instructions for pulling postings per company, so a scan doesn't have to re-derive fetch techniques from scratch each time. Treat these as the default approach; override/pivot if a site's mechanism has changed (ATS migrations, new bot protection, etc.) and update this file when it does. `companies.md` stays the source of truth for scan *results*; this file is the source of truth for scan *method*.

## Dispatch groups — balance by FETCH COST, not company count

*Added 2026-08-21 after a scan took 40 minutes of wall clock that should have taken ~20.*

**The mistake to avoid:** splitting 94 companies into six groups of ~16 each. Company count and fetch cost are unrelated. Measured 2026-08-21, six groups of 16/16/16/16/16/14:

| Group | Wall | Tool calls | Contents |
|---|---|---|---|
| G6 | **7 min** | 32 | 14 small Greenhouse/Lever/Ashby boards |
| G1 | 15 min | 50 | Allstate, Cigna, J&J, Capital One |
| G5 | 15 min | 47 | Workday CXS insurance/enterprise |
| G2 | 22 min | 64 | UHG, CVS, Humana, Centene |
| G3 | 22 min | 66 | Adobe, Comcast, eBay (Apple, then in G3, since removed) |
| **G4** | **40 min** | **106** | retail/QSR keyword-sweep sites |

**120 minutes of agent work compressed into 40 — about 50% parallel efficiency.** G4 did 3× G6's tool calls for two more companies. Perfect balance would put the critical path at ~20 min.

**Cost is driven by pagination and keyword-sweep breadth, not company count.** The expensive sites are the ones that still need multi-keyword enumeration: Marsh (1,003 unique, 45 pages), Hilton (603, 11 queries), Marriott (511, ~55 requests), Home Depot (154, 7 keywords + pagination), McDonald's (144, 4 keywords × path pagination), Under Armour (4 keywords × `startrow`), CVS (693, facet + 8 keywords). Small Greenhouse/Lever/Ashby/Rippling boards are one API call each and cost almost nothing.

### Standing split (97 companies, 6 groups, ceiling ~22 min)

- **G1 — big Workday + gaming/betting + Marriott** *(~14 min)*: Allstate · Geico · Cigna · Progressive · J&J · Capital One · Mozilla · Fanatics Corp · Fanatics Betting · BetMGM · Prize Picks · Underdog Fantasy · Scopely · StockX · GOAT · SeatGeek · **Marriott**
  - **✅ THE REBALANCE IS DONE AND MEASURED (2026-08-25 r2). Marriott moved G4a → G1; do not move it back.** Apple's removal had left G1 the cheapest group, and Marriott was G4a's largest cost. Result: **G4a ~23 min → 8.9 min, G1 ~10 → ~14 min, critical path ~17 min.** Do not re-add Apple.
  - **Marriott is 62% of G1's request volume** (~520 of ~840) and roughly half its fetch wall. Everything else in G1 is cheap and fully concurrent — the six Workday tenants cost one Capital-One's-worth of wall between them (Capital One 94 pages and J&J 92 overlap), and the nine Greenhouse boards are effectively free.
- **G2 — healthcare majors** *(~22 min)*: Humana · CVS · Centene · United Health · VSP · Cotiviti · Talkiatry · Hinge Health · Teladoc · Cast and Crew · Lyra Health · Quest Diagnostics · Omada Health · Coalition · Alpaca · Human Interest
- **G3 — big tech** *(~15 min)*: Adobe · Microsoft · Pinterest · Zillow · Airbnb · Shopify · Etsy · Comcast · eBay · NBC Universal · Sysco · American Express · Gopuff · Tailscale · Vanta
- **G4a — retail/QSR keyword-sweep sites** *(**8.9 min**, 7 companies)*: Marsh · Hilton · McDonald's · Home Depot · Wendy's · iHerb · HCA Healthcare *(blocked)*
  - ⚠️ **`iHerb *(blocked)*` was STALE and was corrected here 2026-09-04 r2.** It has been unblocked for 5+ consecutive runs via the native `POST careers.iherb.com/api/Jobs/Search` (re-verified 56/56 that run), while `companies.md` recorded it correctly throughout. **A stale block annotation in this line propagates into every dispatch brief written from this file** — a group told a board is blocked treats a clean fetch as a surprise rather than as the expected result. Check this line against `companies.md` whenever either changes.
  - **🔴 G4a IS NO LONGER THE CRITICAL PATH, and two standing levers are now DEAD.** **Hilton is no longer a sensible donor** — dropping its keyword union for full pagination took it to 45 requests / ~14 s (see the Oracle RC recipe). **And G4a's remaining expensive site is Wendy's, whose cost is RATE-LIMIT WALL TIME at a hard ≤2-worker cap (160 s), not request count — so moving it to another group would not reduce the ceiling at all.** Everything else in the group finishes in under 60 s combined.
- **G4b — retail light + all small boards** *(~13 min, 26 companies)*: Ross Stores · Under Armour · Kohl's · Dick's Sporting Goods · PetSmart · Ulta Beauty · Post Holdings · PSA (Collectors) · Life360 · Versapay · Pantheon · Foodsmart · Jellyvision · DNC · Celigo · Alt · Gemini · Digible · EnergyHub · Cribl · Parachute Health · Aurora Solar · **AccuWeather** · **Collectiv** · **InStride Health** · **Webflow**
  - **🔴 AccuWeather was added to the roster on 2026-08-24 but was MISSING FROM THIS SPLIT until 2026-08-25 r2 — so it was silently going unfetched on every full scan in between.** Assigned to G4b (Greenhouse, one call). **Cheap standing check: the six group lists must sum to `companies.md`'s roster count.** 🔴 **The current sum is stated in ONE place only — the newest roster-add bullet below.** This line used to carry its own copy of the total, and it went stale at **96** while the split itself was correct at 97, so the check that exists to catch an unfetched company was asserting the wrong target. Never re-add a count here.
  - **Collectiv added 2026-08-26** — Breezy HR, one call, effectively free. Assigned here on the same reasoning as AccuWeather. **This is the check that catches a roster-add that never reaches a dispatch group; run it whenever `companies.md` gains a row.**
  - **InStride Health added 2026-08-27** — Greenhouse, one call, 16 postings, effectively free. Assigned here on the same reasoning.
  - **Webflow added 2026-08-28** — Greenhouse (token `webflow`), one call, 27 postings, effectively free. Assigned here on the same reasoning. **Split now sums to 97: 17 + 16 + 15 + 7 + 26 + 16.**
- **G5 — Workday CXS insurance/enterprise + ADP** *(~15 min)*: Travelers · The Hartford · Nationwide · Pfizer · Stryker · Wintrust · Alteryx · Empower · ICF · American Public Education · Overhaul · Parallels · Direct Travel · Ryze · Logic20/20 · Easy Dynamics

Group sizes are deliberately lopsided — **7 companies in G4a versus 26 in G4b.** That is correct, not a mistake to "fix." G4b, the largest group, ran its boards in **36.6 s** of actual fetch against ~200 s summed sequential (~5.5×), and its whole wall is just Ulta's own 36.3 s. *(Measured 2026-08-25 r2 at 23 boards; Collectiv, InStride Health and Webflow have been added since, each one Greenhouse/Breezy API call and effectively free, so the wall is unchanged.)*

**✅ MEASURED 2026-08-25 r2 — the rebalance worked and the critical path MOVED. It is now G2, not G4a.**

| Group | Companies | Wall 2026-08-24 | Wall 2026-08-25 r2 | Calls |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| G4a | 8 → **7** | ~23 min | **8.9 min** | 27 |
| G4b | 22 → **23** | ~16 min | ~13 min | 33 |
| G5 | 16 | ~23 min | ~13 min | 21 |
| G1 | 16 → **17** | ~10 min | ~14 min | 31 |
| G3 | 15 | ~19 min | ~14 min | 20 |
| **G2** | 16 | ~15 min | **~17 min** | 24 |

**Next lever, if the ceiling needs to come down again: G2.** Its cost is CVS (86 s fetch wall, ~200 pages, of which 137 are the `power bi` keyword) plus UHG's two 5.3 MB sitemaps and Lyra's 37 sequential HTML pages. **G3 is now cheap enough to be a donor** (~35 s of actual fetch across 15 boards), which is the reverse of its historical position.

**✅ MEASURED — the split works. Critical path 40 min → 18.4 min (2026-08-21 r2) → ~23 min (2026-08-24, after deliberately buying accuracy with time).**

| Group | Companies | Wall 2026-08-21 r2 | Calls | Wall 2026-08-24 | Calls |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| G1 | 17 | 14.5 min | 51 | ~10 min | ~22 |
| G2 | 16 | 13.9 min | 52 | ~15 min | ~29 |
| G4b | 22 | 11.0 min | 45 | ~16 min | ~24 |
| G3 | 15 | 13.6 min | 50 | ~19 min | ~30 |
| G4a | **8** | **18.4 min** | 54 | ~23 min | ~40 |
| G5 | 16 | 10.7 min | 30 | ~23 min | ~35 |

On 2026-08-21 r2, total agent work 82 min compressed into 18.4 — ~4.5× parallel efficiency, against ~50% on the pre-split run. The 22-company G4b finishing second-fastest is the clearest possible confirmation that **company count is not fetch cost.** The standing split has been used unchanged since; the previously-suggested "move Hilton to G4b" lever has **not** been applied.

**The two standing levers have now BEEN APPLIED AND MEASURED — do not re-propose them:**
- **🟢 G5 — RUN THE NINE CXS TENANTS CONCURRENTLY, NOT SEQUENTIALLY. Measured: 48.8 s wall against 125.4 s summed sequential (2.6×), taking G5's total fetch wall from ~23 min to ~51 s.** The group had risen 10.7 → 23 min purely because it moved all nine onto full empty-`searchText` pagination (~150 POSTs; **Stryker alone is 59 pages**) — an accuracy-for-time trade that concurrency now buys back outright. **The wall is now just Stryker's own 48.8 s, so the "split Stryker out" lever is DROPPED** — under concurrency it runs alongside the other eight rather than after them. **Keep full pagination.**
- **🟢 G4a — MARSH IS ON FULL BOARD PAGINATION AS PRIMARY. Measured: 100 POSTs against the sweep's ~713 (7.1× cheaper) AND more accurate** (1,954 unique against a page-1 `total` of 1,954 — exact 100%, versus the sweep's 99.5%). G4a's remaining sweep costs: Marriott ~294 · Hilton ~116 · McDonald's 46 · Wendy's ~56 · Home Depot 21 · iHerb+HCA 7.
- **Known cost drivers to budget for, not to "fix":** CVS's `power bi` keyword is a broad full-text term returning **2,701** on its own and expanding the pool 690 → **3,808** (5.5×) — same class as Marsh's `bi` and Hilton's `developer`. (Apple, formerly the other big cost driver here, was removed from the roster 2026-08-25.)
- **Optional and NOT recommended:** Home Depot's bare `analyst` keyword takes its pool **207 → 560** for **zero** additional matches (the extra ~350 are retail/store/finance bare-function Analyst titles). Recorded so the gap is not later misread as a coverage hole.
- If the ceiling still needs to come down after those, move **CVS** out of G2 (facet + keyword sweep) and **Hilton** or **Marriott** out of G4a. Don't rebalance blindly — re-measure first, since costs move as sites change.

**Scratchpad prefixes** follow the group name (`g1_`, `g4a_`, `g4b_`, …) plus the date, and every group sets `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` to avoid the shared `__pycache__` collision seen on 2026-08-21.

## Dispatch-group deliverable contract

*Added 2026-08-31. **This exists because the dispatch brief had no checked-in home.*** The
note above says "keep the dispatch brief in sync with it, in both directions" — but the
brief was rewritten from scratch in the orchestrator's prompt every run, so there was
nothing to sync *with*. The measurable consequence: on 2026-08-31 the six groups returned
six different readings of `counts.analyst` — **G5 returned `0` while its own post-bar dump
held 11 analyst records; G1 returned 55** — which made a per-tier census impossible to
build without publishing wrong numbers. **This section is the contract. The brief cites it;
it does not restate it.** Per-group company lists and fetch recipes stay where they are.

Each group writes one JSON file, `g{N}_{date}_results.json`:

```json
{"group":"G1","companies":[{
  "company":"Allstate","status":"ok|blocked|error",
  "board_total":465,"unique_enumerated":465,
  "failure_signature":null,
  "counts":{
    "core":         {"found":0,"non_us_dropped":0,"ambiguous_flagged":0,"seen_suppressed":0,"new":0},
    "analyst":      {"found":0,"non_us_dropped":0,"ambiguous_flagged":0,"seen_suppressed":0,"new":0},
    "lead_manager": {"found":0,"non_us_dropped":0,"ambiguous_flagged":0,"seen_suppressed":0,"new":0},
    "borderline":   {"found":0,"non_us_dropped":0,"ambiguous_flagged":0,"seen_suppressed":0,"judged_away":0,"new":0},
    "excluded":     {"early_career":0}
  },
  "items":[{"company":"…","title":"…","location":"…","url":"https://…",
            "tier":"core|analyst|lead_manager|borderline",
            "base_tier":"core|analyst",
            "location_verdict":"US|AMBIGUOUS",
            "judgement":"one line, borderline only"}],
  "notes":"durable method/state findings"
}]}
```

🔴 **Field definitions are in `rules.md` §4, "The count contract", and are authoritative
there.** The one that gets misread: **`found` is counted on the FULLY ENUMERATED board,
BEFORE any location or dedup filtering.** It is not "what I reported", and it is not "what
survived dedup".

#### 🔴 THE `items[].tier` ENUM IS PART OF THE CONTRACT — pinned 2026-09-02 r2

*(Added because it was NOT pinned, and it promptly became the FIFTH field to drift.)* The
value is exactly one of **`core` · `analyst` · `lead_manager` · `borderline`** — the same
four keys used in `counts`. **Never `borderline_candidate`.**

🔴 **`classify()` RETURNS `borderline_candidate`, AND THE CONTRACT DOES NOT ACCEPT IT.** This
is the whole trap: the natural implementation is `item["tier"] = classify(title)`, and that
emits a string the contract has never used. On 2026-09-02 r2, G2 did exactly this while the
other five groups normalised; a compiler matching a single spelling would have **silently
dropped all five of G2's borderline rows** — four CVS postings and a Humana one, every one of
which reached the report. **Map it once, at the point of classification:**

```python
tier = jobscan_match.classify(title)
if tier == "borderline_candidate":
    tier = "borderline"          # contract spelling; counts and items must agree
```

🔵 **This is the FOURTH instance of one failure and the pattern is now unmistakable**:
`counts.analyst` (2026-08-31 r1), `counts.excluded` (r3), `excluded_audit` (2026-09-02),
`items[].tier` (2026-09-02 r2) — **each time in the field the contract gave a shape but not an
enum.** A JSON example showing `"tier":"core|analyst|lead_manager|borderline"` reads as
documentation to a human and as a *placeholder* to an implementer. **Prose that says "return a
list" is not a schema, and an example value is not an enum.**

⚠️ **The orchestrator's compiler normalises known aliases and PRINTS the drift** rather than
absorbing it silently — a normalisation nobody is told about is how a contract quietly stops
being one. It must never normalise a value it cannot name.

#### 🔴 THE `excluded_audit` SHAPE IS PART OF THE CONTRACT — pinned 2026-09-02

*(Added because it was NOT pinned, and the field promptly drifted four ways in one run.)*
`rules.md` §4 mandates the audit list but this block only ever said "plus a flat list of the
qualifying titles". On 2026-09-02 the six groups returned **four different key names for the
withheld tier** (`would_be_tier` · `would_tier` · `tier` · `tier_withheld`), **two for the
family** (`family` · `exclusion_family`), **G1 nested it per-company while four others put it
top-level, and G4a wrote it in BOTH places.** A compilation reading only the documented
top-level field returned **41 rows against a true 53**, with the US-eligibility column empty on
every row — i.e. it would have under-reported the ratified cost of the exclusions by 23% and
claimed **0** US-eligible where the truth was **40**.

🔵 **This is the third instance of one failure**: 2026-08-31 r1 spread `counts.analyst` six
ways, r3 spread `counts.excluded` six ways, and 2026-09-02 spread `excluded_audit` four ways —
**each time in the field the contract had not scoped.** An unpinned field in a six-way fan-out
does not stay consistent. Pin it, exactly:

```json
"excluded_audit":[{"company":"…","title":"…","url":"https://…",
                   "would_be_tier":"core|analyst",
                   "family":"early_career",
                   "location":"…","location_verdict":"US|non-US|AMBIGUOUS"}]
```

🔴 **`family` is `early_career` only, as of 2026-09-02** — `manager` was removed from the
exclusions and is now **routed to the `lead_manager` tier** instead of withheld, so it can
never appear in this list. A group returning `"family":"manager"` is running pre-2026-09-02
policy. See `rules.md` §1.

- **Top-level on the group object, exactly once.** Never per-company, never both.
- **`would_be_tier`** is the tier the title *would* have reached with the `is_excluded()`
  early-return bypassed. Core and analyst only — borderline-only withholds are counted in
  `counts.excluded` but do not enter the audit list.
- **`location_verdict` is mandatory and must come from the scoped detail resolver**, not a
  text read of a facility name — see the 2026-09-01 r2 correction that required this.
- The orchestrator **dedups the audit list by URL** and must tolerate none of the above being
  honoured; a compiler that reads one key name silently undercounts.

🔴 **`lead_manager` IS A FOURTH TIER, ADDED 2026-09-02** — a title that clears the core or
analyst bar and carries `lead` or `manager` is reported there instead. It is a **routing
overlay, not a filter**, so it carries the same five buckets and obeys the same identity as
every other tier. Two things follow that groups get wrong if they treat it as a slice of
Core:

- **`counts.core` and `counts.analyst` must NOT also count a routed title.** Each title
  lands in exactly one tier's `found`, or the identity double-counts and the orchestrator
  halts.
- **Every `lead_manager` item must carry `base_tier`** (`core` or `analyst`) — use
  `jobscan_match.base_tier()`, never re-derive it. Without it, "how many core-quality matches
  did this board produce" is unrecoverable, because the answer is now split across two
  sections. **Borderline is NOT routed**; a lead/manager title that only reaches the
  borderline net stays borderline.

🔵 **The identity below MUST hold per tier, per company, and the orchestrator asserts it and
halts on a mismatch:**

```
found == new + non_us_dropped + ambiguous_flagged + seen_suppressed + judged_away
```

**Do not emit a census that has not been asserted.** "A healthy health check is not a proof"
is a lesson this file already records twice (Travelers' `seo_url`; the `is_us()`
country-descriptor branch that passed 104 tests with zero fixtures) — an unchecked count is
the same failure wearing a different hat.

Also standing, and unchanged by this contract: **groups never write either tracker**;
read-only dedup against `seen_jobs.json` is expected and is what `seen_suppressed` counts.

### 🔴 DEDUP NOW HAS TWO STEPS — ruled 2026-08-31 r3

URL-based dedup is still the rule, but `bin/jobscan_dedup.py` now carries a **narrow, opt-in
req-ID fold** for individually proven drift shapes (`DRIFT_SHAPES`: adobe, pantheon, ulta,
progressive). Groups must use both, and must **not** suppress a retitle:

```python
index  = jobscan_dedup.build_index(jobs)
ridx   = jobscan_dedup.build_reqid_index(jobs)

if jobscan_dedup.is_seen(url, index):
    suppress()                                   # seen_suppressed
else:
    stored = jobscan_dedup.seen_by_reqid(url, ridx)
    if stored and jobs[stored].get("title") == served_title:
        suppress()                               # seen_suppressed -- pure URL drift
    elif stored:
        report(flag="retitle", stored_url=stored)  # SAME req, CHANGED title -- must report
    else:
        report(new=True)
```

🔴 **A req-ID hit is NOT automatically a suppress.** Three of the 13 collapse groups in the
tracker are retitles, and one (Progressive `18060418`) **crossed a classification boundary** —
`classify()` returns `None` on the old title and `analyst` on the new. Full rule and the
evidence requirement for registering a new shape: `rules.md` §3.

### 🔴 REPORT FORMAT CHANGED — ruled 2026-08-31 r3

The report carries the tables and the prose findings. **It does NOT carry the tier census
or the exclusion counts** — those go to `scan-history.md`. 🔵 **Groups still return every field
in the contract above, and the orchestrator still asserts the identity and halts on a
mismatch** — only the display was dropped. Report the **audit list** of withheld core/analyst
titles instead of the count. See `rules.md` §4.

🔴 **IT IS EIGHT TABLES AS OF 2026-09-02, NOT SIX** — `Leadership & Management` was added to both the
company-list and Indeed halves, sitting after Data Analyst and before Borderline. **Every
table is still emitted even when empty**, carrying the `*(none)*` placeholder; that rule is
what stops a tier being silently absent and it now applies to eight. Full order in
`rules.md` §4.

---

## Global / common rules (apply to every company)

- **🔴 NON-US IS A HARD EXCLUSION (ruled 2026-08-25) — BUT IT IS A CRITERIA RULE AND MUST NEVER TOUCH THE FETCH.** A posting outside the US is not reported and not persisted. **This is decided CLIENT-SIDE, on the posting's location field, after the whole board has been enumerated.**
  - 🔴 **Do NOT add a location filter, facet or `locationId` param to any fetch, and do NOT trim a keyword sweep on location grounds.** Several ATSes filter location unreliably (the Cigna `jobFamilyGroup` facet is the standing cautionary example of a server-side filter that silently undercounted for weeks). A fetch-side location filter would drop US postings whose location metadata is malformed or missing — failing toward false negatives, invisibly. Same coverage-vs-criteria warning as the borderline bar.
  - **Ambiguous or missing location → report it, don't drop it.** Unlike every other exclusion, this one keys on a metadata field rather than the title, so a parse failure looks exactly like a foreign posting. Where the location genuinely can't be determined, surface it with the uncertainty flagged.
  - **A US-remote listing enumerating many locations is US** — e.g. CVS's `NY - Work from home (40 locations)`. Judge the posting's location field, not the employer's global footprint.
  - ⚠️ **Expect this to cut reported volume at the global enterprises** — UHG, J&J, Marsh, Empower, Cigna and Capital One post to India, Ireland, Spain and the Philippines as well as the US. On 2026-08-25 it took the company-list yield from 10 core to 1.
  - ⛔ **A low US count is NOT a reason to touch the roster.** Every roster company posts US jobs; they also post offshore. **Never propose pruning, deprioritising or skipping a company on non-US volume, low recent yield or fetch cost**, and never extrapolate a per-scan yield rate from one run — new postings per company per day are in the low single digits. The operator ruled on this explicitly; see `rules.md` §4, "closed-and-rejected".

- **🔴 LOCATION-FIELD TRAPS — the non-US rule keys on a metadata field, so a PARSE FAILURE LOOKS EXACTLY LIKE A FOREIGN POSTING.** *(Consolidated 2026-08-25 r2; every one of these fails silently.)* This is why `rules.md` §1 requires an ambiguous location to be **reported with the uncertainty flagged, never dropped.**
  - **🔴🔴 STANDING ASSERTION FOR EVERY HTML-SCRAPED BOARD: A NONZERO EMPTY-LOCATION COUNT IS A PARSER DEFECT, NOT A BOARD PROPERTY — HALT AND FIX THE MATCHER** *(generalised 2026-09-03; it was previously written as a Radancy/TalentBrew-only rule)*. It earned the promotion by catching a defect on a **non-Radancy** board: Airbnb's location is **not in an element with a `location` class — there isn't one** — it sits in a trailing `col-span-3` div after the title anchor, and a matcher keying on a class name returned **172/172 empty locations.** ⚠️ **The failure direction is what makes this mandatory rather than nice-to-have: an empty location resolves AMBIGUOUS, not error**, so a wholly broken location parser produces a board that is merely "all flagged" — which `rules.md` §2 says to report rather than drop. The defect therefore survives every count check, every identity assertion and the report itself. **Assert `empty_locations == 0` on every HTML-scraped board before believing any location verdict from it.**
  - **Never build a US test on a `, XX` state-abbreviation regex.** It fails in both directions: `Bangalore, IN` → Indiana and `München, DE` → Delaware are **false US positives** (McDonald's location strings end in an ISO country code), while `Bolingbrook, Illinois` (Ulta), `Phoenix, Arizona` (PetSmart), `Lakeville, Minnesota` (Post Holdings), `Kohl's Corporate Offices (0900)` and Dick's `Customer Support Center` are **real US postings the regex cannot read at all** — five roster companies whose postings a naive filter would silently drop.
  - **🔴 Travelers: `primary_location` is a COORDINATE PAIR** (`[-72.6851, 41.7637]`), not a place name. Build the location from `primary_city` / `primary_state` / `primary_country`. ⚠️ `location`, `city`, `state`, `country`, `job_id` and `job_url` all **exist as keys but are always `null`**, so a naive read returns `None` rather than erroring.
  - **🔴 Direct Travel (ADP myjobs): `requisitionLocations` IS POPULATED ON THE LIST RESPONSE, and carries a first-class structured country** *(corrected 2026-08-31 r3 — the previous text said there was no location data on either endpoint, and that is no longer true)*. Read `requisitionLocations[].address.country.codeValue` (`"USA"`); the record also carries `longName`, `cityName`, `postalCode`, `countrySubdivisionLevel1` and a pre-joined `nameCode.longName` (`"Schaumburg, IL"`). **Prefer the structured country field and never round-trip it through the text heuristics.** Only `workLocations` and `postingLocations` are still empty arrays on every record. **`locationCode` on the per-req detail still works and is kept as the fallback** (`"VUSA"` = Virtual USA) — *(detail response is `{"jobRequisitions": [ … ]}`, a **list**, so a top-level field read returns `None`)*. ⚠️ **This field appearing is a reminder to re-check "no data here" claims rather than inheriting them** — the old note would have sent every Direct Travel candidate down the detail-fetch path for data now available in one call.
  - **🔴 ADP WORKFORCE NOW — GENERALISED 2026-08-29, this is the carrier rule, not a Parallels quirk. LOCATION LIVES AT `requisitionLocations[].nameCode.shortName`, AND `address` CARRIES NO COUNTRY FIELD AT ALL** (`countrySubdivisionLevel1.codeValue` is empty on every record). **An `address`-only read returns AMBIGUOUS for the whole board.**
    - **`shortName`'s LAST comma-separated token is an ISO COUNTRY code; the middle token, when present, is the subdivision.** Overhaul serves `Santo Andre, São Paulo, BR` (city, subdivision, country — note the leading space, strip it). **Parallels' `Remote, MT` has NO subdivision token, so `MT` is the COUNTRY: MALTA, not Montana.** A `, XX` state regex returns Montana — a false US positive.
    - Confirmed against sibling records on the same Parallels board: `Remote, CA` = Canada (the board carries a separate literal `Remote, US`), `Remote, DE` = Germany, `Remote, GB` = UK. **This is the exact mirror image of the `, XX` state-abbreviation trap above and fails the same way** — so a resolver that "fixes" the `, XX` trap by trusting a two-letter suffix inherits it. Read the employer's own convention off its other records before assuming either reading.
  - **🔴🔴 A COUNTRY NAME THAT IS ALSO A US PLACE NAME MUST LOSE TO A STRUCTURAL US SIGNAL** *(found 2026-08-30 independently by THREE dispatch groups — the same root cause each time, which is why it is written here once)*. This is the **generalisation** of the Ross `Dublin, CA` bullet below, and it fails the same way: a **silent false negative in the one exclusion that runs BEFORE the bar.** Live cases this run: **`New Mexico`** (matched `mexico` — **17 real CVS/UHG postings** scored non-US), **`Greece, NY`**, **`Lake Wales, FL`** (matched `wales`), **`Ontario, OR`** and **`Ontario, CA`**, **`Peru, IL`**, **`Panama City, FL`** — plus **18 Home Depot records whose authoritative `primary_country` was literally `US`.**
    - **🔴 RULE: an authoritative country field, or a trailing US-state token, WINS over the hint list.** Test the structural signal first; consult the hint list only when neither is present.
    - **⚠️ This does NOT reopen the `Bangalore, IN` → Indiana trap.** A **non-colliding** hint token (`bangalore`, `ireland`, `bengaluru`) still wins outright — only tokens that collide with a US place name are demoted. Keep both rules; they operate on disjoint token sets.
  - **🔴🔴 A MULTI-LOCATION STRING THAT NAMES A US LOCATION IS US-ELIGIBLE, WHATEVER ELSE IT NAMES** *(found 2026-08-30)*. `Remote, USA; Remote, Canada` and `United States (Remote) , Canada (Remote)` both scored **non-US**, because the hint list searched the whole string and **returned on first hit**. Live at Life360 (10 records), Pantheon (1), Mozilla (2), Scopely (1) — and worst at **J&J**, where `Reporting & Analytics Lead` (R-093082-1) had a **resolved `United States of America` country descriptor OVERRIDDEN** by a `Bangalore`/`Limerick` hit in the same field. **⚠️ A `;`-split alone does NOT catch all forms** — the second example separates on `,` and parenthesises the country. **Scan the whole string for a US signal before accepting any non-US hit; first-hit-wins is the bug.**
  - **✅ FIXED 2026-08-31 r3 — THE `_COMMA_STATE` / `_LEADING_CC` REGION PRODUCED FOUR FALSE-VERDICT MECHANISMS.** *(Found, ruled and fixed the same day. Retained in full because the mechanisms are the reason the fix looks the way it does — and because mechanism D is the project's clearest example of a silent drop.)* This is the one exclusion that runs **before** the bar, so a wrong verdict here decides whether a posting is ever judged.
    - **🔴 (D) THE SERIOUS ONE — A SILENT US DROP. `AR` and `CO` were in `NON_US_ISO2`, which `_LEADING_CC` tests BEFORE `US_STATE_ABBR`.** On the state-first shape CVS's whole board uses, `CO - Denver` resolved **non-US** (Colorado read as Colombia) and `AR - Little Rock` likewise (Arkansas as Argentina) — never reaching the collision handling the module already had. `AR` was in **both** sets and the wrong one won; `CO` was in neither collision set. **Two stored postings carried it: Comcast `CO - Virtual`, CVS `CO - Work from home`.** 🔵 Fixed by data, not logic: both moved to `AMBIGUOUS_STATE_COUNTRY`, which defers to the hint list via the existing `pending_us_state` path. **The invariant is now asserted every run by `test_jobscan.py::check_iso2_sets_are_disjoint` — never put a US state abbreviation in `NON_US_ISO2` again.**
    - ⚠️ **A–C below produce visible noise; D deleted postings with no trace.** When triaging this region, fix the drop direction first.
    - **Validated over 12,557 real location strings** (tracker + every r3 board dump), not just the suite: **27 US postings recovered**, 26 genuinely-foreign corrected, 50 mirror-gap fixes, and **AMBIGUOUS FELL 4,153 → 4,103** so the flag channel did not flood. Suite 167 → 231 cases.
    - 🔴 **THE REPLAY CAUGHT A REGRESSION THE SUITE DID NOT.** The first version of the fix deferred the undecidable `City, <name>, COLLIDING-ISO2` shape to `FOREIGN_HINTS`, which matched the bare city name `nottingham` and scored **`Nottingham, Baltimore County, MD` as non-US**. Bare city names in that list are the documented Ross `Dublin, CA` trap. A US administrative middle token (`… County`/`Parish`/`Borough`, or a full state name) now settles it **before** the hint list. Measured: **83 of the 90 real strings reaching that branch are US**, so US must win — and returning AMBIGUOUS instead would have flooded the channel.
    - ⚠️ **NOT closed, deliberately: `City, ISO2, postcode` with no subdivision token.** `Wustermark, DE, 14641` is structurally identical to `Denver, CO, 80202` and both stay **US**; neither city is in the hint list. **The supported route is the caller passing `country_code`**, which is decisive. Both halves are pinned in `test_jobscan.py`.
    - The original write-up of mechanisms A–C follows.
    - **(A) "A real city precedes it, so XX is the subdivision" is wrong when a subdivision is ALREADY present.** The heuristic assumes the colliding token is the last meaningful one, so in `City, SUBDIV, ISO2` the **country** is read as the subdivision. Verified US-scoring: `Toronto, ON, CA`, `Montreal, QC, CA`, `Calgary, AB, CA`, `Berlin, BE, DE`, `Hannover, NI, DE`, `Mumbai, MH, IN`. The `PLACEHOLDER_CITY` guard cannot help — it only fires when there is *no* real city, i.e. the `Remote, MT` form.
    - **(B) Worse: some foreign SUBDIVISION codes are US state abbrevs ABSENT from `AMBIGUOUS_STATE_COUNTRY`, so the resolver returns US on the FIRST candidate and never reads the country slot at all.** `Amsterdam, NH, NL` (Noord-Holland → New Hampshire), `Utrecht, UT, NL` (→ Utah), `Almere, FL, NL` (Flevoland → Florida), `Milan, MI, IT` (Milano → Michigan). Netherlands and Italy boards are the exposure.
    - **(C) The regex reads a POSTCODE as a state.** `_COMMA_STATE` is `,\s*([A-Za-z]{2})(?![A-Za-z])` — the lookahead blocks a following **letter** but not a following **digit**, so `England, GB, TN24 0DQ` yields `TN` → Tennessee → **US**. Against the real UK outward-code set, **15 collide** with US state abbrevs: `AL CA CO CT DE GU KY LA ME NE PA PR TN WA WV`. ⚠️ `GU` and `PR` are in that list **because of** the 2026-08-31 territory fix — that fix widened this surface. Unlike (A) and (B) this fires with only **two** comma tokens.
    - **THE MIRROR GAP, same branch, opposite direction:** `NON_US_ISO2` is consulted **only** in the leading position (`_LEADING_CC`), so a **trailing** unambiguous foreign ISO2 is never tested against it — the `_COMMA_STATE` loop skips any non-US-state code and the country slot is dropped. `remote, GB`, `remote, DE`, `Cybercity 72201, Ebene, MU` all fall to **AMBIGUOUS**. Safe under `rules.md` §2 (report flagged) but noisy: **8 of Parallels'/Overhaul's 15 distinct location strings, 7 of them unambiguously foreign.** ⚠️ `México` also defeats `FOREIGN_HINTS`, which matches `\bmexico\b` against an unaccented string.
    - 🔵 **ONE FIX COVERS ALL FOUR: test the trailing token's POSITIONAL ROLE, and check it against `NON_US_ISO2` BEFORE reading it as a US state.**
    - **Blast radius measured 2026-08-31 r3: 50 records carry the shape and ZERO reached the report** — 46 at Under Armour (CA 28, DE 17, ID 1), all store titles with no data word; 4 at Travelers, all saved because the structured `primary_country` short-circuits before the text branch. 🔴 **Latent by luck, not by design:** a single data-word title in a Canadian or Dutch store would put a non-US posting into the report. Clean negative worth keeping: on boards where the text path is the *only* path (Parallels, Overhaul, Easy Dynamics), the shape does not occur at all.
    - ⚠️ **This is the same family as the `Bangalore, IN` → Indiana and `Remote, MT` → Malta traps below, and it is why neither of those is safe to "fix" by trusting a two-letter suffix in either direction.** Read the employer's own convention off its other records.
  - **🔴 A TRAILING POSTCODE HIDES THE COUNTRY — `City, {SUBDIV,} {ISO2}, {postcode}`** *(found 2026-08-30 at Under Armour)*. **431 of 487 records scored AMBIGUOUS**, including 31 Netherlands, 28 Spain, 10 Mexico and 10 Ontario. It fails in **BOTH** directions: it blinds the non-US exclusion *and* floods the ambiguous-flag channel. **Rule: if the LAST comma-token is a postcode, the token before it is the country slot.** ✅ Validated live: **431 AMBIGUOUS → 8, with ZERO US→NONUS flips.**
    - 🔴🔴 **`bin/jobscan_location.py` DOES NOT IMPLEMENT THIS RULE — there is no postcode or country-slot logic anywhere in the module** *(corrected 2026-08-31 r3; an earlier version of this bullet said "`jobscan_location` encodes this — use it", which was false and caused a dispatch group to build its own caller-side country derivation)*. The rule above is a **caller-side** obligation today. If you implement it in a caller, pass the derived country as `is_us(location, country_code=...)` — that branch is decisive and short-circuits the text path, so **your derivation, not the module, chooses the answer**; say so when reporting numbers derived that way.
    - ⚠️ **Do NOT apply the rule literally without a US guard.** `Denver, CO, 80202` would read the country slot as `CO` = Colombia and silently drop a US posting. Read the slot as a country only when it is US/USA or a 2-letter ISO2 that is **not** also a US state abbreviation. *(Shipped `is_us()` gets this string right on its own — `CO` is not in `AMBIGUOUS_STATE_COUNTRY` — so the hazard is the prose rule, not the module.)*
    - 🔴 **The guard does NOT fix the colliding-slot case, which is a separate live defect** — when the country slot collides (`CA`, `DE`, `IN`, …) the guard passes no country code and hands control back to the text path, which returns a **false US**. See the false-US-positive item in the r3 corrections index. Measured at Under Armour: **46 records, shipped and guarded both US, 0 reached the bar.** Under Armour's corrected census is **312 US / 169 non-US / 7 AMBIGUOUS** against the 359/122/7 the run used.
  - **🔴 A TRAILING BARE `US` TOKEN IS NOT MATCHED BY A `, XX`-STATE TEST** *(found 2026-08-30 at Home Depot)*. `Atlanta, GA, US` read AMBIGUOUS and flagged **3,806 of 3,870 records**. **At that volume the ambiguous flag stops carrying information at all** — which is worse than a wrong verdict, because it silently disables the channel `rules.md` §1 relies on. Accept a trailing `US`/`USA` token as a US signal.
  - **🟡 CIGNA USES A 3-LETTER ISO COUNTRY PREFIX SEPARATED BY A SPACE, NOT A DASH** *(found 2026-08-30)* — `IND Bengaluru`, `ESP Madrid - 38.75 hrs`, `CHN Shanghai Lujiazui Software Park (LJZ)`. The documented Teladoc matcher requires `^[A-Z]{3}\s*[-:]` and **reads none of them**. **Honour the space form ONLY for a known ISO country code**, so a 3-letter non-country prefix cannot collide. 5 records rode on this.
  - **🟡 CENTENE EMITS THE STATE LAST, HYPHENATED AND UNSPACED** — `Remote-MO`, `Remote-IL` *(found 2026-08-30)*. Neither the CVS state-first matcher nor the comma-state matcher reads it. ✅ **CLOSED — `jobscan_location` has read this shape correctly for FIVE consecutive runs as of 2026-09-03.** *(The sentence previously here — "this shape scored AMBIGUOUS across Centene's entire board" — was stale from 2026-08-30 and is deleted; a dispatch group re-reported it as a live defect, which is what stale prose costs.)* Keep the trap documented, because it is why the resolver has the branch; do not read it as a current failure.
  - **🟡 INDEED APPENDS A ZIP TO THE STATE** — `Chicago, IL 60617` alongside a bare `Chicago, IL` **on the same board**, so an **end-anchored** `, XX` test scores some rows US and some AMBIGUOUS. Do not end-anchor the state test.
  - **🔴 SMARTRECRUITERS `location.country` IS A LOWERCASE ISO-2 CODE** (`us`, `gb`) *(found 2026-08-30)*. Joining it into a display string yields `London, gb`, which reads AMBIGUOUS to a `, XX` matcher — pushing a UK posting into the report **flagged rather than excluded**. **Read it as structured data and uppercase it; never round-trip it through the display string.** Same shape as the Oracle-RC `PrimaryLocationCountry` win. NBC's `Business Systems Analysis Manager` rode on this.
  - **🔴🔴 A STRUCTURED COUNTRY FIELD MUST NEVER BE ROUND-TRIPPED THROUGH THE TEXT HEURISTICS — THIS IS THE RULE THE THREE BULLETS ABOVE ALL POINT AT.** Quest/Lyra JSON-LD emits ISO2 (`US`), so a reconstructed `Toronto, ON, CA` would hit a `, XX` state test and score **US** — the Parallels ISO-vs-state trap in the **false-US** direction. **Make the country field decisive where it exists; fall back to text ONLY when it is absent** (and see the Workday CXS recipe, where `country.descriptor` is sometimes absent and the fallback must be flagged, not silent).
  - **🔴🔴 A CITY NAME ALONE IS NEVER A NON-US SIGNAL — REQUIRE A COUNTRY TOKEN OR RESOLVE THE STATE** *(found 2026-08-29 at Ross Stores)*. **Ross's `Dublin, CA` is Dublin, CALIFORNIA — its corporate HQ — not Ireland.** This is the exact **mirror** of the UHG `/job/dublin/` trap below, and it is worse, because it fails toward a **FALSE NEGATIVE in the one exclusion that runs BEFORE the bar**: a city-slug non-US hint list sent **18 of Ross's 28 net candidates to a silent drop**, with nothing in the output to show for it. **Never put a bare city name on a non-US hint list.** Same family as `san-juan` (Puerto Rico, US) and `dublin` (both).
  - **🔴 CVS PUTS THE STATE FIRST: `RI - Woonsocket`, `TX - Irving`, `IL - Work from home`** *(found 2026-08-29)*. A `, XX` test reads **none** of CVS's board and scores the whole thing AMBIGUOUS — which under the ambiguous-location rule floods the report rather than erroring. **Matcher: `^([A-Z]{2})\s*-\s*\S`. It is only safe if the non-US hint list is tested FIRST**, since the same shape could front a foreign code.
  - **🔴 TELADOC USES A 3-LETTER ISO COUNTRY PREFIX: `USA - Any Location (Remote)`, `ESP - Barcelona`, `CAN - Toronto`** *(found 2026-08-29)*. A 2-letter matcher reads nothing on this board. Note it collides visually with the CVS state-first shape above — **the token length is the discriminator, so test 3-letter before 2-letter.**
  - **🔴 ASHBY: `addressCountry` returns the ISO code `"US"`, NOT the full name `"United States"`** *(found 2026-08-28 at Hinge Health)*. A matcher testing only for `united states` **false-dropped three real US postings** — `Staff Data Engineer - Data & ML Platform` ×2 and `Data Engineering Manager, Data & ML Platform` — before it was caught. Match `^(united states|us|usa)$` case-insensitively. **Same family as Marriott's full-name-vs-ISO problem, in the opposite direction**, which is the point: neither convention is safe to assume, so test for both at every ATS.
  - **🟢 Radancy/TalentBrew boards: job pages carry JSON-LD `JobPosting` with a full `addressCountry`.** The sitemap method yields only a city slug. **One plain GET on the canonical URL resolves it AND supplies the API-grade `title` the slug cannot.**
    - 🔴🔴 **`/job/dublin/` IS AMBIGUOUS — RESOLVE EVERY ONE. The former note "confirmed on UHG: `dublin` = Dublin, Ireland" was WRONG and is deleted.** UHG's board carries **50 `dublin` slugs**, and both **Dublin, Ireland** and **Dublin, OHIO, United States** occur on it. Measured 2026-08-26 r2: of the six resolved, five were Ireland and **one was Ohio — and that one was the run's only UHG core match** (`Sr Data Engineer/ Test Automation`, 99768065280). **A city-slug shortcut drops real US matches silently.**
    - ⚠️ **`san-juan` resolves to Puerto Rico, which is US** — same failure direction.
    - **Consequence: the JSON-LD GET is MANDATORY on every candidate, not an optimisation.** On a Radancy board the slug is not a location.
  - **Workday CXS `locationsText` can read `"2 Locations"` / `"3 Locations"`** (seen on Adobe, eBay, J&J). A targeted `searchText={reqId}` POST is a reliable **1-request** resolver; use it rather than dropping the record. Marsh needs it more than most — its location field is `City - Building`, which sends nearly the whole board to UNKNOWN.
    - 🔵 **The Dublin ambiguity is NOT UHG-specific — it generalises to any tenant.** Confirmed 2026-08-27 r2: eBay's `Database Software Engineer` (R0069326) resolved to **Ireland** via the CXS detail endpoint. Treat the resolution GET as mandatory everywhere, not as a Radancy/UHG rule.
  - **🔴 MARRIOTT'S `country` FIELD RETURNS FULL NAMES, NOT ISO CODES** *(found 2026-08-27 r2)* — `"UNITED STATES"`, not `"US"`/`"USA"`. A resolver written against the ISO-code convention used elsewhere scores every Marriott record **ambiguous**, which under the ambiguous-location rule floods the report rather than dropping records — noisy, but it masks the real signal. **3,372 Marriott records ride on this field**, so it is the highest-volume location field on the roster. Marriott also exposes `countryAbbr` — prefer it, and fall back to matching the full name.
- **🔵 Keywords ENUMERATE the board; titles FILTER it. Do not confuse the two.** *(Clarified 2026-08-21 after the distinction caused real confusion.)* A sweep's keyword list has **nothing to do with** `profile.md`'s target titles and must never be trimmed to match them. Keywords exist only to drag postings out of a search endpoint we cannot paginate whole — which is why they include things like `snowflake`, `tableau` and bare `data`, and why several are deliberately noisy. The match decision is made **client-side, against the title**, after everything is pulled.
  - Evidence the endpoints are not title-scoped: Travelers' `fuzzy=false` is full-text over the whole JD body (bare `data` → 362 hits); Ross Stores' `business intelligence` returns **7,099** via full-text OR across all fields; Marriott's bare `data` returns 1,905; Apple's `search=` is relevance-ranked and its `snowflake`/`tableau` keywords match JD bodies (+190 reqs).
  - **Consequence: dropping a redundant keyword cannot cause a missed target title** — redundant means two keywords return the same postings. Conversely, **adding a keyword that looks like a target title buys nothing** if the endpoint isn't title-scoped.
  - **Prefer full-board pagination wherever the board is small enough** — it removes the miss-risk entirely rather than trading against it. Already primary on Comcast, eBay, Adobe, Humana, Centene, Allstate, Cigna, Zillow, Hartford, Nationwide, ICF, Pfizer, Stryker, Etsy, Lyra, Wintrust, Alteryx, Empower, Ryze, **Marsh and Capital One** (Workday CXS), and **Under Armour** (SAP SuccessFactors `?q=&startrow=`). Migrating a site *off* a keyword sweep is always the safer direction.
  - The one filter that **does** need to track the target titles is the client-side title regex, and it must carry abbreviations: `\bBI\b` alongside `business intelligence` (13 tracked BI-abbreviated titles as of 2026-08-21, incl. Humana "Senior BI Engineer", Parallels "Staff BI Engineer"), plus `information architect|business information|informatics` for Humana's convention, and `analy` rather than `analyt` so "Analyst" matches.

- **🔴🔴 THE MANDATED ENUMERATION NET — THIS IS THE AUTHORITATIVE TEXT. Keep the dispatch brief in sync with it, in both directions.** *(Reconciled 2026-08-27. The stated net here had drifted ~26 tokens NARROWER than the brief's, and the gap accounted for 36 of that run's 40 reported borderline items — a one-time visibility backlog, not hiring.)*

  ```
  data|analy|business intelligence|\bBI\b|information architect|business information|
  informatics|intelligence|insight|reporting|metrics|governance|database|dba|dbre|visuali|
  warehouse|lakehouse|etl|elt|pipeline|ingestion|streaming|snowflake|kafka|hadoop|hive|
  impala|spark|databricks|tableau|dbt|airflow|looker|power bi|bigquery|redshift|informatica|
  sql|nosql|mongo|postgres|mysql|cassandra|presto|trino|flink|python|scala|mdm|edw|
  aws|azure|gcp|docker|kubernetes|k8s|terraform
  ```

  - **🔵 WHY THE CLOUD TOKENS ARE IN THE NET, and why removing them is a coverage regression, not a criteria tightening.** the operator's 2026-08-26 ruling made `aws|azure|gcp|docker|kubernetes|terraform` **qualifying** (at borderline). The standing rule is that **a widened bar must widen the net** — a title the net cannot see can never be judged. This net exists to FIND; `rules.md` §1 decides. **Never trim it to match the target titles and never trim it on location grounds.**
  - **The 2026-08-26 r2 note about `streaming` being absent from the brief's net was a symptom of this, not the whole problem** — the two texts differed by ~26 tokens, not one. **Whenever either is edited, edit both.**
  - **Per-board exception: drop bare `warehouse` for Sysco only** — it is a food distributor and the token returns ~125 Warehouse Order Selector roles. Everywhere else `warehouse` stays: it costs classification time only and it is what finds "Data Warehouse" titles.

- **🔴 TITLE FALSE FRIENDS — tokens that clear the net or the WIDENED BAR on something that is not what it looks like.** *(Consolidated 2026-08-27. The three marked 🔴 fail toward a FALSE POSITIVE in the CORE table, which no `distinctkeys` or coverage assertion catches.)*
  - 🔴 **`spark` matches the city Sparks, Nevada.** CVS's `Field Service Technician - Reno/Sparks/Carson City NV` (R1001431) auto-scored **core** on the data-stack token rule. **Anchor as `\bspark\b(?!s)`**, and note plain `\bspark\b` still matches `Sparks` unless the plural is excluded.
  - 🔴 **`dbt` is Dialectical Behavior Therapy at Lyra Health.** Long known as a pool inflator, but it became **worse** on 2026-08-26 when `dbt` became a data-stack token: those postings now land in **core**, not merely in the candidate pool. **At Lyra, a `dbt` hit co-occurring with `psychologist|therapist|clinician|licensed` is always the therapy modality.** 3 of 7 candidates on 2026-08-27.
  - 🔴 **`azure` is a venue name at Marriott AND HILTON** — `Sous Chef II - Azure` (Marriott, Tampa FL) and, **confirmed 2026-08-28 r2, Hilton's `Chef de Partie - Azure Restaurant` and `Waiter/Waitress - Azure Restaurant` at Waldorf Astoria Ras Al Khaimah.** Both Hilton instances were AE, so **the location rule rescued them — that is luck, not the guard.** It is now a two-employer pattern, not a Marriott quirk: anchor or hand-check `azure` on Marriott, Hilton, McDonald's and Wendy's.
    - 🟢 **STANDING MITIGATION (adopted 2026-08-29) — a TITLE guard, so it no longer depends on the location rule.** Co-occurrence: `azure` **and** `chef|waiter|waitress|restaurant|de partie|sous|bartender` → **bin at the NOISE stage**, before the location rule ever runs. Verified 2026-08-29: it bins Hilton's two Waldorf Astoria reqs cleanly. **Ship this rather than relying on the location rule to catch them** — "the location rule caught it" is luck on a title-level defect, and the two employers already known guarantee more.
  - **🔴 NEW FALSE-FRIEND CLASS (2026-08-29): MISSPELLED QUALIFYING TOKENS — the failure direction is a SILENT CORE/BORDERLINE FALSE NEGATIVE.** Two employers this run, joining the recorded J&J `Sciencist`: **Home Depot `SR ANALYST DECISION ANALYSTICS`** (23741098, Atlanta GA, **US**) and **Marsh `Practicante Data Analitycs`** (R_363438, Bogotá). Both fail `\banalytics\b`. Nothing was lost this run — each title carried a second qualifying token — but **a title whose ONLY qualifying token is misspelled is binned as noise with nothing in the output to show for it**, which no `distinctkeys` or coverage assertion catches. **Cheap mitigation: add `analy(t|s)?ic` as a BAR-ROUTE FALLBACK** (it absorbs `analystics`, `analitycs`, `analytic`, `analysic`). Keep it off the net route, where `analy` already covers everything.
  - 🔴 **`Pipeline - {Title}` is an EVERGREEN REQUISITION-TYPE PREFIX at American Express** *(found 2026-08-28 r2)* — `Pipeline - Product Director, Resy` (26012502), `Pipeline - Future Software Engineer Opportunity` (26010568 US), `Pipeline Future - Senior Analyst-Risk Management` (26010895). **These clear the 2026-08-28 route-5 CONCEPT bar on `pipeline` and 3 of 4 are US**, so they land in the borderline table on a requisition-type marker. Same family as Hartford's `(2026 Talent Pipeline)` and the Pfizer/Cigna drug sense, but this one is a *leading* prefix. **Amex mints them in a block — expect them every run.**
  - 🔴 **`placement` is an INSURANCE-BROKING term at Marsh** *(found 2026-08-28 r2)* — `Placement Analyst` (R_358750, Clearwater FL, **US**) is insurance placement, not a student placement. A fixed-term-programme regex carrying bare `\bplacement\b` mislabels it. **Match programme shapes (`industrial placement`, `placement year`, `placement student`), never bare `placement`.**
  - **`edw` matches R**`edw`**ood City** and **`elt` matches Ch**`elt`**enham** and Bras`elt`**on** (Stryker) — 4 Gopuff retail cards and a Sysco slug on 2026-08-27, 3 more Gopuff Redwood City retail titles on 2026-08-27 r2. Redwood City is common across Bay Area boards.
  - **`warehouse` in the FULFILMENT sense is now confirmed at Cigna** (CuraScript / Accredo / Express Scripts pharmacy distribution) and **J&J** — it is not only a retailer problem. See also the classifier caution above: this token now clears the *qualifying bar*, not just the net.
  - **`pipeline` in the DRUG sense is confirmed at Cigna too** — `Senior Manager, Drug Pipeline & Strategy - Evernorth` — joining the Pfizer anchor. Expect it at any pharma or payer employer.
  - **`Financial Planning and Analysis` spelled with `and`** slips past an `FP&A|Financial Planning\s*&?\s*Analysis` pattern; widen to `Financial Planning\s*(&|and)?\s*Analysis`. **More generally: a title whose ONLY qualifying token is bare `analysis` is reliably a business-function title** — that single test subsumes the whole enumerated family (FP&A, Escrow Analysis, Business Analysis, Quantitative Analysis) and caught two Capital One titles on no documented list.
  - **`ssis` fires inside "A**`ssis`**tant"** — ~85 false hits in a single audit. **Word-bound it (`\bssis\b`) in any second net**, or it buries a real gap.
  - **`visuali` is surgical visualization at Stryker** (6 sales/marketing reqs) and **weather-map rendering at AccuWeather**. It is a *net* token, not a *bar* token — keep it, but it never qualifies on its own.
  - **`pipeline` in the DRUG / SALES sense** — Pfizer's `Director, HTA … Obesity Early Pipeline` and Hartford's `(2026 Talent Pipeline)`. New at Pfizer 2026-08-27, reproducible at any pharma employer.
  - **`Analytical` means analytical CHEMISTRY at pharma employers** — documented at J&J, **and it generalises: it fired 4× at Pfizer on 2026-08-27** (`Associate Scientist Chem - Analytical`, `Senior Scientist- Analytical Research and Development`, …). **Record it as a pharma-industry rule, not a J&J one.**
  - **`Pre-Analytical` / `Bio-Analytical` at Quest Diagnostics** — laboratory specimen-processing and instrument roles; `Analytical` names the lab's *pre-analytic phase*, not analytics-as-a-discipline. **12 of 57 candidates on 2026-08-27.** Noise, same family as the anchored-out `Data Entry Clerk`. Expect them every run — Quest is a clinical lab.
  - **`warehouse` in the fulfilment sense** — reproduced across Kohl's, PetSmart, Under Armour, Cigna, Post Holdings, Dick's, Pfizer, Stryker, PSA. Harmless; inflates the pool at any employer with fulfilment ops.
- **🔴🔴 THE WORKDAY "REQ ID IS EVERYTHING AFTER THE LAST UNDERSCORE" RULE IS WRONG WHERE THE REQ ID ITSELF CONTAINS AN UNDERSCORE — AND MARSH IS THAT CASE** *(reproduced live 2026-09-03; this is the `R_`-prefix dedup death `companies.md` already records at this board, arriving again by the same route)*. Marsh `externalPath`s end `..._R_345449`, so last-underscore yields `345449` and **silently drops the `R_`**. Every canonical URL is then wrong, nothing matches the tracker, and on 2026-09-03 **all 1,954 Marsh postings read as new while 13 were tracked.**
  - **Correct pattern: `_(R_\d+)(?:-\d+)?$`.** Note the trailing revision-suffix group — Workday appends `-1`, `-2` on re-posts.
  - 🔴 **There is no loud symptom.** Enumeration is unaffected if pagination is keyed on the whole `externalPath`, so there is no exception, no short board and no failed offset. **The only signal is 0 suppressions across all four tiers** — which is exactly the "0 new with 0 suppressions" dedup-death shape `rules.md` §4 exists to catch, and why dedup must run before judgement.
  - ⚠️ **Last-underscore is CORRECT for J&J-style `_R-093799` and WRONG for Marsh-style `_R_345449`, and the two are indistinguishable until you look at the id's own prefix separator.** Read the employer's own id convention off its records before choosing an extractor — the same instruction the `, XX` location traps carry, in the dedup layer.
- **🟢🔴 USE ONE SHARED, SELF-TESTING DEDUP HELPER — STANDING METHOD.** *(Adopted 2026-08-27 r2. Six independent re-derivations is why these collapses keep recurring.)* All six dispatch groups import a single helper implementing every dedup rule in this section, with a `selftest()` that **must return `[]` before any "0 new" is believed.**
  - It asserts against **known collapses in both directions**: Hilton `CX_1009`, Amex `CX_1`, ADP one-character keys, Workday location slugs, UUID segments, Pantheon's dual scheme, the `ashby_jid` query form, the digit-guarded revision strip — **and, critically, that the frequency guard does NOT fire on the postings stored under several URL shapes** (Hartford `r2625625`, Humana `r-425799`, Marsh `r_345593`, Adobe `r170861`, J&J `r-091239`, Cigna `26007037`, Under Armour `1403993400`).
  - ⚠️ **Every defect found in this helper across two runs has been in the guard's own posting-identity or same-employer function, not in key extraction** — and each was found by the self-test or a manual cross-check, never by the scan noticing something looked wrong. **Budget for that: the guard is the fragile part.**
  - ⚠️ **Do not read a low hot-key count as a broken guard** — the per-carrier rules keep most collapses out of the index upstream. Healthy recent range: 5–12 hot keys, all of them genuine board constants.
  - **`distinctkeys` per board must be > 0.** A zero means the extractor failed and silently degraded to exact-URL matching — a defect, not a result. **The one legitimate exception is Etsy**, whose URLs carry no ID at all.
  - **🔴🔴 A HEALTH CHECK THAT LOOKS HEALTHY IS NOT A PROOF — GENERALISE THIS, IT IS THE RECURRING SHAPE** *(established 2026-08-30 on two independent instances in one run)*. Every assertion in this section answers *"did the extractor produce keys?"*, and **none of them answers "are they the RIGHT keys?"**
    - **(1) Travelers `seo_url`.** Reading the wrong URL field reported **31 of 31** qualifying items as brand new while **`distinctkeys` read a healthy 361** — the keys were plentiful, well-distributed and from the wrong ID space entirely. See the Findly/CWS recipe.
    - **(2) PetSmart's compound-id collapse.** `distinctkeys` **79 against 10,894 records** — a **collapse, not a zero**, so the `> 0` assertion passed cleanly. A `> 1` assertion would have passed too.
    - **🔴 The assertion that actually discriminates is a RATIO, not a floor: `distinctkeys` should be within an order of magnitude of the record count**, and a board where it is not is a defect until proven otherwise (Hilton's legitimate `distinctkeys=38` over 38 candidates passes; PetSmart's 79 over 10,894 does not). **Pair it with a spot-check that a sample of extracted keys actually appears in `seen_jobs.json`'s stored URLs for that employer** — that is the only check that catches a wrong-ID-space read.
  - **🔴 PETSMART'S REQ ID IS A COMPOUND `{POSTING_ID}-{LOCATION_ID}` AND MUST NOT BE REDUCED** *(measured 2026-08-30)*. Exact arithmetic on the full 10,894-record board: **10,868 compound + 26 plain → 53 distinct POSTING_IDs + 26 plain = `distinctkeys` 79.** `companies.md` already mandates the compound key; this is why.
    - **🔴 The digit-guarded trailing-`-\d+` revision strip EATS THE LOCATION_ID here — it must be BOUNDED TO A 1–2 DIGIT SUFFIX.** A Workday revision is `-1`/`-2`; PetSmart's LOCATION_ID is **4+ digits**. Unbounded, the strip collapses every location variant of a posting onto one key. **0 false suppressions this run only because the 2 stored PetSmart URLs happen to be plain 4-digit ids** — that is luck, not safety, and it will not survive the first compound URL entering the tracker.
  - **🟢 THREE MORE KEY-EXTRACTION GAPS, all found at COMPILE TIME on 2026-08-29 and all fixed — recorded so they are never re-derived.** Each is the same family as the UUID letter-budget defect: a legitimate ID shape the extractor rejects by construction.
    - **(a) Paradox.ai 32-hex `uniqueID` (Marriott, McDonald's).** `…/job/097A78BE…` is ID-shaped but carries **~16 letters**, so the whole-segment letter budget rejects it — yielding **no id key AND no `primary_id` for those two entire boards**. **Fix: index a `^[0-9a-f]{32}$` segment (case-insensitively) VERBATIM, exempt from the letter budget — exactly as UUIDs are.**
    - **(b) J&J's front-end scheme is `/en/jobs/{r-081694}/` — `jobs`, PLURAL, not `job`.** A `job`-only test returned **`primary_id = None` for all 70 stored J&J front-end URLs** — the Progressive-class structural death of the frequency guard, on a second employer. **Fix: accept `job` OR `jobs`.**
    - **(c) Breezy HR `/p/{12-hex-id}-{slug}` (Collectiv)** yielded no id key at all. **Fix: index a leading `^([0-9a-f]{8,})[-_]` hex run of the last path segment.** (This is the hex sibling of the SmartRecruiters/Progressive `^(\d{5,})-` rule.)
    - **✅ After all three, `primary_id` is non-`None` for EVERY stored URL except the 5 Etsy ones — which is the documented true exception.** That is the assertion to keep: not "non-None for a sample", but **non-None for the whole tracker, with Etsy as the only allowed exception**.

- **🔴 CLASSIFIER CAUTIONS — every one of these produced FALSE POSITIVES that reached the report line.** *(For any group building an ad hoc classifier rather than hand-classifying.)*
  - **🔴 `analy` IS A *NET* TOKEN, NOT A *BAR* TOKEN.** Treating any title containing `Analyst` as clearing the qualifying bar produced **~180 false-positive borderline items at Marsh alone**, and **reproduced at 174 on 2026-08-28** by a different group that had read the warning. The net finds `Accounting Analyst`; `rules.md` §1 then says bare-function analyst titles are **noise**. Same failure as the 2026-08-21 r2 14× over-report. **This is the single most reproducible classifier bug in the project — assume you will make it.**
  - **🔴🔴 THE CLASSIFIER AND THE EXCLUSIONS MUST MATCH PLURALS — A PLURAL HEAD NOUN BREAKS ALL THREE CODE PATHS, IN ALL THREE DIRECTIONS** *(measured live 2026-08-30; this is the single highest-leverage classifier fix on record because one edit closes three failure modes at once)*.
    - **Into the report (FALSE POSITIVE):** `Data Scientist` → correctly excluded, but **`Data Scientists` → borderline.** Live at Shopify: `Staff Product Data Scientists - Multiple Roles`.
    - **Into the bin (SILENT PERMANENT LOSS):** `Database` / `Pipeline` / `Lakehouse` → borderline, but **plural → noise.** Live at eBay: `Sr. SDE, Distributed Databases` **cleared the net and was dropped by the BAR** — nothing in the output looks wrong.
    - **Demoted:** `Data Engineer` / `Analyst` / `Architect` → core, but **plural → borderline.**
    - **✅ Fix: EVERY head noun needs `s?`.** This is the same family as the data-science word-order gaps below, and it has the same cure — **ship it ONCE in the shared classifier helper, with a plural fixture for each of the three directions in `selftest()`.** Per-group re-derivation is what makes these recur.
  - **🔴 A FALSE-FRIEND `pipeline` MUST *BIN*, NOT MERELY FLAG, WHERE `pipeline` IS THE ONLY QUALIFYING TOKEN** *(found 2026-08-30)*. `rules.md` §1 route 5 already names Hartford's `(2026 Talent Pipeline)` and the Pfizer/Cigna **drug** sense as documented false friends that stay noise — but the shipped guard only attached a **flag**, so **three items reached the report line**: Cigna `Drug Pipeline & Strategy`, Cigna `Drug Pipeline Strategy`, Hartford `(2026 Talent Pipeline)`. **A flag is not a decision; bin them.**
    - ⚠️ **Precedence matters: a data-stack or route-1 token still BEATS the guard**, so `Data Engineer - Drug Pipeline Analytics` stays **core**. The guard fires only when `pipeline` is carrying the title alone.
  - **🔴 THE `azure`-AS-VENUE GUARD HAD A LIVE HOLE: `barback`** *(found 2026-08-30)*. Marriott serves **`Barback - Azure` (US)**, which scored **BORDERLINE** on the cloud/infra route. Verified by a targeted `keyword=azure` query (`totalJob` 12): `Azure Host or Hostess` and `Sous Chef II - Azure` binned correctly; `Barback - Azure` did not. **Add `barback|bar back|busser|banquet|lounge|server|cook|dishwasher` to the co-occurrence guard.**
    - ⚠️ **The shipped guard was ALREADY BROADER than this file documented** — it carried `steward|barista|host(ess)?` on top of the recorded list. **The two texts had drifted. Reconcile them whenever either is edited**, exactly as the net-token bullet above requires.
  - **🔴🔴 A VERDICT-STRING CASE MISMATCH SILENTLY BINS EVERY GENUINE MATCH AND LOOKS EXACTLY LIKE A CLEAN ZERO** *(found 2026-08-30)*. A group tested `verdict in ("CORE","BORDERLINE")` against a classifier returning **lowercase**: **six boards reported "0 new" with exact board totals, healthy `distinctkeys` and no exception.** Same family as the `re.I` defect below, but at the *verdict* layer rather than the regex layer, so a regex audit does not catch it.
    - **✅ CHEAP STANDING CHECK, AND IT GENERALISES: `0 new` with `0 suppressions` is a BUG; `0 new` with `N suppressions` is a RESULT.** A working board almost always suppresses *something*. **Record the suppression count next to every "0 new"** — in the report and in `companies.md` — and treat a zero-zero pair as unproven.
  - **🟢 THE SHIPPED DATA-SCIENCE EXCLUSION FIRED LIVE ON ITS HARDEST DOCUMENTED FORM, WITH NO GROUP RE-DERIVING IT** *(2026-08-30)*. UHG's `Senior Director, Applied AI & Data Science` — the case that defeated every pattern below — was correctly excluded. **This is the first run in four where that recurring defect did not recur, and the cause is identifiable: it was shipped ONCE in a shared helper instead of re-derived per group. Keep doing that; it is the standing cure for this whole class.**
  - **🔴 A DATA-SCIENCE EXCLUSION REGEX MUST MATCH BOTH WORD ORDERS *AND* ALLOW CONNECTOR WORDS.** Matching only `Data Science Manager` misses `Manager, Data Science`; and the natural fix `(Director|Manager)[^a-z]{0,15}Data Science` **silently fails on `Director of Data Science`**, because the letters in `of` are not in the `[^a-z]` class. Between them these let **4 Home Depot postings through as false-positive core** on one run and `Director of Data Science - Online Search and Recommendations` through on another. **Write this exclusion once and reuse it.**
  - **🔴 THE DATA-SCIENCE EXCLUSION HAS TWO MORE WORD ORDERS, AND BOTH FAIL TOWARD FALSE-POSITIVE CORE** *(found 2026-08-28 r2 independently by two dispatch groups; this extends the connector-word bullet above, it does not replace it)*. The documented forms are all keyed on the **Manager/Director** tier. Two live families evade them entirely:
    - **The `Principal Scientist, Data Science` tier.** J&J minted **five US reqs** this run (`Principal Scientist, Data Science …`, `Senior Principal Scientist, Data Science RWE`). They name neither `Data Scientist` nor a management tier, so a `(Manager|Director)…Data Science` pattern misses all five. **Caught only by hand review.** Add `\bscientist\b[^a-z]{0,4}data scien`.
    - **The hyphen-joined ANALYST tier.** Amex `Senior Analyst-Data Science` (26012539) **auto-scored CORE** on route 1 and was likewise caught only by hand.
    - ⚠️ **Note both were caught by a human pass, not by any assertion — budget for the hand review; the regex is not sufficient on this family.**
  - **🔴 TWO FURTHER DATA-SCIENCE WORD ORDERS — found 2026-08-29, and this again EXTENDS the two bullets above rather than replacing either.** Both fail toward **FALSE-POSITIVE CORE**.
    - **(a) The tier word separated from `Data Science` by ARBITRARY WORDS.** UHG's `Senior Director, Applied AI & Data Science` defeats **every** documented pattern, including the `of`-connector fix — the intervening `Applied AI &` is neither a connector nor inside any bounded character class.
    - **(b) The documented `Director of Data Science` connector hole FIRED LIVE AND UNTRACKED**, at Home Depot: **req 23665004, `Director of Data Science - Online Search and Recommendations`**. It auto-scored **borderline** and would have reached the table. The hole was already written down; it had simply never been shipped.
    - **✅ WORKING PATTERN — ship this one, plus its mirror (`data\s+scien…` followed by the tier word), and nothing narrower:**
      ```
      \b(manager|director|head|vp|vice\s+president|chief|avp|svp|sr\.?\s*manager|senior\s+manager|asst\.?\s*manager|lead)\b(?:\s*(?:of|for|the|and|&|,|-|–|—|/|:)\s*)*\s*data\s+scien
      ```
    - 🔴 **THIS DEFECT HAS NOW RECURRED FOR AT LEAST THREE CONSECUTIVE RUNS, each time re-derived from scratch by a different dispatch group. STOP RE-DERIVING IT: ship the data-science exclusion ONCE, inside the shared dedup/classifier helper**, alongside the `selftest()` fixtures — with `Senior Director, Applied AI & Data Science`, `Director of Data Science - …`, `Principal Scientist, Data Science`, `Senior Analyst-Data Science` and `Manager, Data Science` as its assertions. **Per-group re-derivation is the root cause, not the regex.**
  - **🔴 `\bdata\b` DOES NOT MATCH GLUED FORMS, AND THAT GAP IS NO LONGER THEORETICAL** *(confirmed live 2026-08-28 r2)*. United Health is running **`Lead Bigdata Engineer, DC or MN (Hybrid)`** (96537911920, **US**) — it clears the *net* on an unanchored `data` but **failed the qualifying bar** and was binned as noise until the pattern was widened. It happened to be already-tracked, so nothing was lost, but it fails toward a **silent core false-negative**. NBC served a truncated `Senior Forward-DeploData Scientist/…` for the same reason. **Use `\bdata\b|\bbig\s*data\b|\bbigdata\b|\bdata-?ops\b` on the bar route.**
  - **🔴 THE MULTILINGUAL INTERNSHIP NET IS MISSING FRENCH `stage`** *(found 2026-08-28 r2)*. Amex `Campus - Stage - Assistant(e) Junior Chef de Projet SEO/GEO & Web Analytics` (26007593) carries `analytics`, clears the bar, and is an internship. The documented set has `stagiaire` and `alternance` but not the bare noun. **Add `\bstage\b` guarded against English "stage gate"/"staging", or match the `Campus -` programme prefix.**
    - 🟢 **Both guards behaved correctly on 2026-08-29** — the French `stage` / `Campus -` pair fired as intended, and the Marsh `placement` guard correctly left `Placement Analyst` alone. Neither needs revisiting.
    - **🔴 STILL MISSING (found 2026-08-29): SPANISH `practicante` AND DUTCH GLUED `Bachelorstage`.** Marsh serves `Practicante Data Analitycs` (R_363438, Bogotá); Pfizer serves `Graduaat / Bachelorstage: Optimalisatie van data-opzoeking`. ⚠️ **The Dutch one is GLUED, so even the `\bstage\b` fix above misses it** — the word boundary falls before `Bachelor`, not before `stage`. **Suggested: `\b(bachelor|master|zomer)?stage\b`** (same staging/stage-gate guard) **plus `practicante|practicas|prácticas`** — note the accented and unaccented Spanish forms both occur.
  - **🔴🔴 A BAR REGEX COMPILED WITHOUT `re.I` BINS LITERAL `Data Engineer` TITLES AS NOISE — AUDIT THE CASE FLAG ON EVERY BAR REGEX, EVERY RUN** *(found 2026-08-29 at Direct Travel and Easy Dynamics)*. A route-1 bar pattern was compiled case-**sensitively** by omission, so any title not in the exact case the pattern was written in failed the bar. **Fixing the one flag took a dispatch group's candidate pool 60 → 115.** It **fails toward silent PERMANENT BINNING** — the titles never reach the report, never reach the tracker, and nothing in the output looks wrong; a whole board can read "0 new" on it.
    - **Standing check: every bar regex needs an explicit case-flag audit** — assert that a known-good literal (`Data Engineer`, `data engineer`, `DATA ENGINEER`) clears the bar in all three casings before a "0 new" is believed. Put it in `selftest()`.
    - ⚠️ **THE ONE DELIBERATE EXCEPTION: `\bBI\b` MUST STAY CASE-SENSITIVE.** Case-insensitising it matches `bi` inside ordinary words and floods the pool. **Everything else on route 1 is case-insensitive; `\bBI\b` alone is not** — compile it separately rather than flipping the flag on the whole pattern.
  - **🔴 EVERY NEW QUALIFYING ROUTE IN `rules.md` §1 INHERITS THE WHOLE TITLE FALSE FRIENDS LIST — the net's guards do NOT transfer automatically.** The bar and the net are separate code paths. Demonstrated 2026-08-28, the day the pipeline-concept route opened: bare `warehouse` became a qualifying token and **Home Depot's `Warehouse Manager` and CVS's `General Warehouse` both cleared the bar through it**, because the fulfilment-sense guard had only ever been applied to the net. **Require `data warehouse` on the bar route; keep bare `warehouse` on the net route.**

- **🔴 COVERAGE CAPS — record these as capped BY DESIGN so a future partial fetch is not chased as a bug.**
  - **🔴 CVS SWEEP CONCURRENCY IS ≤2, NOT ≤5 — CORRECTED 2026-08-29.** History: HTTP 429 on 16 of 57 net keywords at 10-way; then **5 of 55 keywords exhausted retries at 5-way on 2026-08-28 r2**, concentrated in the *deep-paginating* keywords. **At 2 workers it is clean: 55-token sweep, 528 requests, ZERO 429s, ZERO failures, every keyword's `unique == its own total` exactly, 116 s wall** (2026-08-29). Two workers costs almost nothing here — use it and stop re-testing 5.
