  - **🔴 FULL EMPTY-`searchText` PAGINATION IS **NOT** A VIABLE REPLACEMENT AT CVS — tested and rejected 2026-08-29.** At 3 workers with backoff it had **not completed after ~18 minutes** (against the sweep's 116 s) and was killed. **Keep the keyword sweep primary at this tenant; do not migrate CVS to full pagination.** This is the one CXS tenant where the standing "prefer full pagination" preference is overridden by board size (~19,000 postings).
  - **✅ CVS `reporting`, `business information` and bare `BI` — THE 3-TOKEN REDUCTION IS CONFIRMED SAFE AND SHOULD NOT BE RE-LITIGATED.** They are unusable as exhaustive sweep keywords on its ~19,000-posting board: measured 2026-08-29 at `totalHits` **7,072 / 10,865 / 17,374**, each capped at **200 pages / 4,000 records** — the cap reproduced exactly. **They were run anyway as a capped supplement and added 5,243 records and ZERO net-qualifying titles.** Combined with the 2026-08-28 r2 11-keyword supplemental probe (0 net-qualifying titles missed), the reduction is closed. ⚠️ *Distinguish this from the r1 **18**-token reduction, which is closed as **unsafe**.* CVS remains a high-yield board, so any *further* token reduction still needs disclosure in the report.
  - **PetSmart `reporting`/`metrics`/`bi` and Ulta `metrics`** hit an 80-page / 800-record cap against reported totals in the thousands. Partial coverage on those keywords only; none of them clears the bar alone.

- **🔴🔴 THE WORKDAY CXS `/jobs` SEARCH ENDPOINT ALSO HAS A PER-TENANT CONCURRENCY LIMIT, AND AT MARSH IT FAILS SILENTLY TOWARD AN 84% UNDER-FETCH** *(found 2026-08-28 r2; distinct from the DETAIL-endpoint limit in the next bullet)*. At **8 concurrent workers** (4 retries, no backoff) Marsh full pagination returned **320 of 1,951 unique = 16.4% across two passes — with no exception, no failed-page count, and a clean exit.** The run printed a healthy-looking "after pass 2: 320 unique". At **4 workers with 1.5 s × n backoff it returned 1,951/1,951 exact in ONE pass.**
  - **The standing "run the CXS tenants concurrently" guidance needs a per-tenant cap; 8 is too many at Marsh, 4 is proven.** G5's nine tenants remain fine under concurrency (10th consecutive exact run), so this is not a blanket reduction.
  - 🔴 **The ONLY thing that caught it was the `unique == page-1 total` assertion. Keep that assertion mandatory on every CXS tenant** — a silent 16% is indistinguishable from a small board without it.
- **🔴 A PAGINATION LOOP MUST RETRY *INSIDE* ITSELF, NOT AROUND ITSELF — a mid-run abort terminates the loop "cleanly" and the short board looks real** *(found 2026-08-28 r2 at Stryker and PSA)*. Stryker aborted at offset ~760 of 1,193 on a transient connection failure; the loop broke and reported a plausible 760. A dedicated retry-until-clean repair pass (6 attempts per offset, full sweep to `total`) recovered **1,193/1,193 with zero errors** for 60 extra requests. PSA showed the same shape and recovered to **192/192**. Same family as the documented CVS `WinError 10060` and Dick's HTTP 503. **Add PSA and Stryker to the retry-until-clean list, and never let a paginator discard partial results on a mid-run exception.**
- **🟢🔴 THE NULL-RECORD PROBE IS ASYMMETRIC: `total:1` IS A POSITIVE EXISTENCE PROOF, `total:0` PROVES NOTHING** *(settled 2026-09-03; this SCOPES the 2026-09-02 r3 claim that "the probe cannot discriminate", which was written from a single `total:0` at Pfizer and read as a general property)*. A targeted `searchText={reqId}` returning **`total:1` where the garbage control returns `total:0`** proves the requisition exists and is a usable result. Only a **`total:0`** is uninformative, because the garbage control returns `total:0` too.
  - **Measured across three tenants on one run:** Stryker `R571258` → `total:1` vs control `total:0`; Allstate `R34465` → same; **Marsh's 9 nulls all → `total:1`, and all nine returned HTTP 410 on the public page — proving they are CLOSED requisitions**, which took Marsh from 1,954 to an exact 1,954 + 9 = 1,963. Pfizer served **zero** nulls this run, so its unresolved case is still open but is not evidence about the technique.
  - **🟢 NEW AND LOAD-BEARING: THE FACET BLOCK SURVIVES WHEN EVERY DISPLAYABLE FIELD IS NULL.** Allstate's `R34465` had title, `externalPath` and `locationsText` all null, but `jobFamilyGroup` read `Adjusters/Adjuster Support Group` — **proving it was never a tier candidate without any further request.** A null CXS record is therefore **triageable, not merely loggable**: read the facets before spending a probe.
- **🟢 RESOLVE WORKDAY CXS NULL RECORDS, DON'T JUST LOG THEM — it is ONE request and it closes the coverage gap** *(confirmed at a 4th tenant, Adobe, 2026-08-28 r2)*. Adobe served one record with `externalPath` and `title` both null and the req ID recoverable only from `bulletFields[0]` (`R171522`); a targeted `searchText=R171522` POST returned `Head of Campaign, Creative Studio`, San Francisco — noise, but it turns Adobe from 740/741 into an **exact 741/741**. The null-record shape is **per-run, not a tenant property** (Humana's documented instance did not reproduce this run; Marsh had 1 on r1 and 0 on r2).
- **🔴 THE WORKDAY CXS JOB-DETAIL ENDPOINT HAS ITS OWN SOFT RATE LIMIT, SEPARATE FROM `/jobs` SEARCH** *(found 2026-08-28)*. Resolving `"N Locations"` candidates at 12 concurrent workers failed **46 of 315** at Capital One (2 of 58 at J&J); dropping to 4 workers recovered 48/48. **Cap detail-resolver concurrency at ≤4–6 regardless of the main-endpoint concurrency policy.** The failures are silent — a dropped resolution looks like an unresolvable location, which under the ambiguous-location rule floods the report rather than erroring.

- **🔴 A REQ'S TITLE CAN CHANGE UNDER A STABLE REQ ID — and the edit can cross a classification boundary.** Documented at **eBay** (`Senior Systems Engineer` R0074720-1, `CRM Analytics Manager` R0075057 — ID-keyed dedup handled both; a title-based dedup would have re-reported them) and, more seriously, at **Airbnb**: req **7702714** was `Staff Software Engineer, **Streaming** Infrastructure` and is now `Staff Software Engineer, Infrastructure`, **losing its only qualifying token**. ID-keyed dedup is what makes this survivable; the classification question it raises is open in `rules.md` §4.

- **WebFetch can't POST.** Any API that requires a POST body (Workday CXS, some Oracle Recruiting Cloud setups) needs Bash `curl` or PowerShell `Invoke-RestMethod` instead.
- **Dedup gotcha confirmed 2026-08-08: don't rely on grepping `seen_jobs.json`'s `"company"` field name to find a company's prior matches — the stored name can drift.** Fanatics Betting is stored as `"Fanatics Betting & Gaming"` (its original full name from 2026-07-27) while `companies.md` and later scans refer to it as just `"Fanatics Betting"` — a sub-scan that grepped for `"Fanatics Betting"` missed the 3 existing entries and re-reported them as new. Caught and corrected during report compilation (see `matches/2026-08-08.md`), not re-added to `seen_jobs.json`. **When checking a company's prior matches, grep the actual job-board URL prefix (e.g. `fanaticsfbg`) or a loose/partial company-name match, not an exact company-name string — and always double-check any "new" match's URL against the whole file (not just the company's grepped subset) before reporting it.**
- **🔴🔴 THE SAME-EMPLOYER GUARD IS ITSELF A DEDUP TRAP — STORED-NAME DRIFT BREAKS IT, AND IT FAILS TOWARD FALSE POSITIVES** *(found 2026-08-28, five items at J&J)*. The guard that blocks the Cigna/Amex `26######` cross-employer collision works by requiring the candidate's company to agree with the stored entry's `company` field. **Implemented as a name test alone it rejects genuine matches**, because the stored name drifts from `companies.md`'s name — the same drift the "never grep by company name" rule already documents, one level up.
  - **The live failure: `companies.md` says `J&J`, `seen_jobs.json` stores `Johnson & Johnson`.** Normalised, `jj` and `johnsonjohnson` are not prefixes of one another, so prefix containment rejected **five genuine same-host matches** on `jj.wd5.myworkdayjobs.com` and the scan reported five already-seen postings as new core. Exact-URL matching missed them independently because the stored URLs carry an `/en-US/` path segment the served URLs do not — **so the ID key was the only route, and the guard was blocking it.**
  - **🟢 CORRECT RULE: accept a non-exact key match when EITHER the hosts agree OR the company names agree.** The host clause carries same-host/stored-name-drift (J&J, Fanatics); the name clause carries cross-host/same-company (Pantheon's `job-boards.greenhouse.io` ↔ `pantheon.io`, Amex's dual scheme). **The Cigna/Amex collision is still blocked because it differs on BOTH.**
  - ⚠️ **Prefix containment alone is not enough and should not be relied on.** It happens to absorb `Fanatics Betting` → `Fanatics Betting & Gaming`, which is what made it look sufficient. Abbreviations (`J&J`), acronyms and reorderings all defeat it.
- **🔴 THE FREQUENCY GUARD'S POSTING IDENTITY MUST BE A SINGLE PRIMARY ID, NOT THE WHOLE KEY SET** *(found 2026-08-28)*. `implementation.md` already says to exclude `host+path` from identity. That is necessary but **not sufficient**: using the whole *key set* reproduces the same defect in a subtler form, because different URL shapes of one posting extract slightly different key sets. Measured on the live tracker it marked six real postings hot — Hartford `R2625625` (3 shapes), Marsh `R_345593`, Adobe `R170861`, J&J `R-091239`, Cigna `26007037` (dual host), Under Armour `1403993400` — each of which is **one posting**, so dropping their keys re-reports them as new.
  - **Identity = `(company, primary id)`,** where the primary id is always the digit-guarded BASE form so a revision shape (`_R2625625-1`) and a bare shape (`_R2625625`) agree.
  - **🔴 Precedence: query id > ID-SHAPED `/job/` segment > underscore req id > uuid/hex > numeric segment.** The `/job/` segment **must outrank the underscore form**: on Oracle Recruiting Cloud the only underscore in the URL is the *site constant* (`.../sites/CX_1009/job/207092`), so underscore-first gives every Hilton posting the identity `1009`, the guard sees one identity, and **the whole board collapses onto the constant.** Where the `/job/` segment is a location slug (`Hartford-CT`, `Noida`, `Raynham-...`) it fails the id-shape test and the underscore form is used, which is what Workday needs.
- **🔴 A `/job/` PATH SEGMENT IS ONLY AN ID IF IT HAS ID SHAPE — otherwise it is a LOCATION SLUG entering the index as a key** *(found 2026-08-28)*. Ungated, these all became keys: `belfast-10-mays-meadow` (Allstate), `kohls-corporate-offices-0900`, `arlington-va-va31` (ICF), `west-virginia-field-office-wv66`, `co---englewood-183-inverness-dr-west`, `pa---philadelphia-1701-john-f-kennedy-blvd`. They survive only because the frequency guard then drops them — **but that is the guard cleaning up after the extractor, and on a board where one slug serves a single posting it would not fire.** Gate on `^[a-z]{0,3}[-_.]?\d[\w.\-]*$`: `26011525`, `207648`, `r_345593`, `jr20615` pass; every slug above fails.
- **🔴🔴 ADP WORKFORCE NOW: `ccId` IS A BOARD CONSTANT AND MUST NOT BE A DEDUP KEY — IT COLLAPSES A WHOLE EMPLOYER, AND NEITHER BACKSTOP CAN FIRE** *(found 2026-08-28 r2 at Overhaul and Parallels)*. The documented ADP fix ("drop any extracted key that is a bare 1–2 character digit string") addresses only the `_1`/`_2` suffix the **underscore matcher** pulls off `ccId`/`jobId`. It does **not** address the full **query value**: a key list carrying `ccid` indexes `9201289910657_2` → `9201289910657`, which is identical on **100% of that employer's postings**.
  - **Why nothing catches it:** the frequency guard needs **≥3 distinct stored postings** and these boards are tiny (Overhaul has **1** stored posting, Parallels **2**); the ≥90% constants detector needs **≥5 stored URLs** per employer. Both thresholds sit above the board size, so the collapse is invisible. **Measured: 26 of 27 postings reported as already-seen when none of them was in the tracker.**
  - **Fix: `ccId` is NOT an id — drop it from the query-string key list.** `jobId` already carries dedup on this carrier.
  - ⚠️ **Generalise the lesson: a threshold-based guard cannot protect a board smaller than its own threshold.** On small boards the per-carrier key rules are the *only* defence.
- **🔴 ADP myjobs: `reqId` MUST BE IN THE QUERY-STRING ID KEY LIST** *(found 2026-08-28 r2 at Direct Travel)*. Its canonical URL is `myjobs.adp.com/{co}/cx/job-details?reqId={id}`. The list carried `req_id` but **not** `reqId`/`reqid`, and the path holds no digit so no `host+path` key was added either — `distinctkeys = 0` and the board silently degraded to exact-URL matching. **Same class as the Shopify `ashby_jid` defect.** **Updated standing query-string ID key list: `gh_jid`, `ashby_jid`, `jobId`, `jobkey`/`jk`, `POSTING_ID`+`LOCATION_ID` (compound), `req_id`, `reqId`. `ccId` is deliberately NOT on it.**
- **🔴🔴 THE SAME-EMPLOYER GUARD MUST NOT ACCEPT ON HOST AGREEMENT ALONE — SHARED ATS HOSTS SERVE MANY EMPLOYERS** *(found 2026-08-28 r2; raised as latent by one group and confirmed LIVE by another)*. The 2026-08-28 r1 fix ("accept when **either** the hosts agree **or** the names agree") is correct in direction but its host clause is too loose: `job-boards.greenhouse.io` serves **nine roster employers**, and `workforcenow.adp.com` hosts **both Overhaul and Parallels** while `myjobs.adp.com` hosts Direct Travel. Host agreement alone lets those employers cross-match — the Cigna/Amex collision shape on a carrier where ids are not guaranteed unique.
  - **Correct rule: on a shared ATS host, employer identity is `host + FIRST PATH SEGMENT`** (the employer token). Everywhere else the bare host is fine.
  - **Shared-host list:** `job-boards.greenhouse.io` · `boards.greenhouse.io` · `boards-api.greenhouse.io` · `jobs.lever.co` · `api.lever.co` · `jobs.ashbyhq.com` · `api.ashbyhq.com` · `ats.rippling.com` · `jobs.smartrecruiters.com` · `api.smartrecruiters.com` · `myjobs.adp.com` · `workforcenow.adp.com` · `recruiting.ultipro.com` · `*.breezy.hr` · `jobs.jobvite.com`.
  - ⚠️ **This must not break the J&J clause it sits beside.** `jj.wd5.myworkdayjobs.com` is not a shared host, so stored-name drift (`J&J` vs `Johnson & Johnson`) is still carried by the host clause. **Marsh exercises the same drift live — the tracker holds `Marsh McLennan` alongside `Marsh` on one host.**
- **🔴 `primary_id` MUST RESOLVE THE `{numericId}-{slug}` FORM, OR THE FREQUENCY GUARD IS STRUCTURALLY DEAD FOR THAT BOARD** *(found 2026-08-28 r2 at compile time, on Progressive)*. `careers.progressive.com/jobs/18184697-data-governance-steward-senior-or-lead/` yields a correct dedup **key** via the Progressive rule, but the identity function returned `None` — so **every Progressive posting shared the identity `(company, None)`**, the guard saw one posting, and it could never fire on that board. It fails toward *retaining* keys, so it is silent in both directions.
  - **Fix: as a final fallback, return the leading `\d{5,}` run of the last path segment.** This also covers the SmartRecruiters slug-suffix form (`744000144140099-data-architect`).
  - ⚠️ **Standing check: assert `primary_id()` is non-`None` for a sample of every board's URLs**, not just `distinctkeys > 0`. The two failures look nothing alike and neither existing assertion catches this one.
- **🟢 THE FREQUENCY GUARD'S "0 DROPPED" MUST BE PROVEN BY A NON-DEGENERATE FIXTURE INSIDE `selftest()`** *(added 2026-08-28 r2, requested independently by two dispatch groups)*. Hot keys and constants both read **0** against a 1,897-entry tracker — correct behaviour, because the per-carrier rules strip every known collapse (`cx_1`, `cx_1009`, Radancy `34088`/`38852`/`43250`) upstream. But "0 dropped" is otherwise indistinguishable from a structurally dead guard.
  - ⚠️ **A naive fixture proves nothing, and one group's did not.** Building it around a constant `/job/{id}/` segment makes `primary_id` return that constant for every URL (the documented precedence puts the ID-shaped `/job/` segment first), so all the fixture's postings collapse to **one identity** and the guard *correctly* declines to fire — which reads as "unable to fire".
  - **A valid fixture needs ≥3 postings with DISTINCT primary ids that share one secondary key** — e.g. `/careers/{constant}/req_R00000{i}`, where the underscore req id is the identity and the constant is the shared key. Keep it under 5 URLs so the ≥90% constants detector cannot mask the hot-key result, and **assert the fixture is non-degenerate** (`len({primary_id(u)}) == n`) alongside the hot-key assertion.
- **Dedup gotcha (2026-08-21 r2): a bare company token substring-matches an unrelated employer's URL.** Grepping `seen_jobs.json` for `vanta` returns a United Health posting — `careers.unitedhealthgroup.com/job/pearland/director-network-strategy-and-analytics-medicare-ad`**`vanta`**`ge-…`. Keying on the board fragment `jobs.ashbyhq.com/vanta` gives the correct 5 entries; the bare token gives 6. Same family as the §7 "never grep by company name" rule, but a *false positive* rather than a miss — short company tokens (`alt`, `vanta`, `goat`, `reply`) are especially exposed.
- **Dedup gotcha (2026-08-21 r2): Wendy's stored URLs are CASE-SENSITIVE mismatches.** The tracker holds `.../posting/Engineer---Data/209972` (capitalized slug); the site now serves `engineer---data`. A case-sensitive URL comparison misses it. **Key on the trailing numeric ID.**
- **Dedup gotcha confirmed 2026-08-17: Prize Picks' canonical URLs are stored as `http://`, not `https://`, in `seen_jobs.json`** (e.g. `http://prizepicks.com/position?gh_jid=7826517003`). A dedup grep that assumes `https://` for every entry will silently miss all tracked Prize Picks postings and misreport them as new. When grepping `seen_jobs.json` for a company's prior matches, don't hardcode the scheme — search for the domain/path fragment only, or check both schemes explicitly.
- **HTTP 200 confirmed NOT a validity signal on Workday CXS front-end job pages (2026-08-17).** Tested directly on Comcast: a request with a garbage slug AND a garbage req ID still returned HTTP 200 with an empty client-rendered shell (`<title></title>`, no og:title) — the front-end page doesn't validate the URL server-side at all, it just serves a shell that fetches the real content client-side via JS. **Only the `wday/cxs/{tenant}/{site}/job/{exact externalPath}` detail JSON endpoint reliably confirms a specific posting ID is real** — hitting it with the correct ID but a wrong slug/location returns an empty response, not the job data. If you need to verify a specific req ID without already knowing its exact slug, re-derive the slug from a fresh keyword/facet search response (which includes `externalPath`) rather than guessing — the front-end page alone can't tell you.
- **Workday CXS `total:0`-discard rule refined (2026-08-17): the 2026-08-16 fix ("capture `total` from offset=0, discard any page's contents once it reports `total:0`") is not safe to apply blindly to every tenant.** Allstate's `offset=20` page reported `total:0` in its response metadata while still containing 20 genuinely new, unique postings (not filler/unrelated content like the 6 tenants documented 2026-08-16). Blindly discarding on `total:0` would have silently truncated Allstate's pagination to page 1 only (20 of 422 total postings). **Corrected guidance: don't discard page content based on `total:0` alone. Instead, keep all returned content and detect true pagination end via duplicate-req-ID wraparound** (if a page returns req IDs already seen on a prior page in the same pagination run, stop). This doesn't contradict the 2026-08-16 finding for ICF/Hartford/Nationwide/Pfizer/Stryker/Teladoc — those tenants' `total:0` pages really were unrelated filler — it just means "discard on `total:0`" isn't a universal Workday CXS behavior and needs a per-tenant check (or the safer duplicate-ID approach) rather than blanket application.
- **🔴 PM head-noun exclusion now covers the NOUN FORM too (ruled 2026-08-21 r2).** "Product Man**agement**" is excluded exactly like "Product Man**ager**" — "VP, Product Management, Analytics Experience" (Alteryx R12404) is a PM role whatever the grammatical form. **Boundary: the head noun must actually be the product phrase** — "Sr. **Product Marketing Manager**, CDP & Data Connectivity" (R12285) has head noun *Marketing Manager* and is NOT caught; it stays borderline. Regex note: match `product manage(r|ment)` as a head noun, and do **not** let it fire on `product marketing manager`.
- **🔴 PM head-noun exclusion rule — REWRITTEN 2026-08-21, this is no longer a "prefix" test.** *(Was: "a title **starting with** 'Product Manager'/'Senior Product Manager' etc. … is excluded.")* **If a title's head noun is "Product Manager," it is excluded — whatever modifies it, and wherever it sits in the string.** The old prefix framing split identical roles on nothing but each company's title-writing convention. All excluded: "Senior Product Manager – Customer Data Platform", "Staff Product Manager, Data Platform", "**Digital** Product Manager – Cyber Platforms, Data & Tooling", "Device Insights **Product Manager**", "Sr Technical Product Manager, AI & Data Platforms", "Data Solutions **Product Manager**". Confirmed at Capital One (R247257-2), American Express (26011525), and 2026-08-21 at Allstate, Adobe and Apple. **Product-*owner* framing without a PM title is a different rule and stays borderline** (J&J "Data Product Lead", Travelers "Agile Product Owner", Hartford "Sr. Product Owner"). Full statement and the boundary between the two: `rules.md` §1.
- **ADP Workforce Now dedup gotcha (confirmed 2026-08-17 r3 on Overhaul and Parallels): a job requisition's internal `itemID` field does NOT match the public-facing `jobId` used in the canonical application URL.** The true cross-reference is nested at `customFieldGroup.stringFields[]`, in the entry where `nameCode.codeValue == "ExternalJobID"` — that value is what appears in the stored `seen_jobs.json` URL's `jobId=` param. A dedup check that compares `itemID` directly against a stored `jobId=` URL will always miss and misreport an already-tracked ADP posting as new.
- **United Health sitemap coverage gotcha: `/sitemap.xml` and `/sitemap-jobs.xml` are not identical sets — take the union of both.** A scan that only pulls `sitemap-jobs.xml` (as some earlier scans' phrasing implied) can silently miss postings. **⚠️ The SIZE of the asymmetry is not a stable property and must never be used as a health check.** Measured: 48 URLs in one and not the other, then 5-in-one/4-in-the-other, then **zero difference at all** (23,029 `<loc>`, 5,472 `/job/`, identical sets), then 1-2 URLs again (5,425 vs 5,424, union 5,426), then **byte-identical again** (22,938 `<loc>` / 5,426 `/job/` in each, union **5,426**). **Keep taking the union — it is one cheap GET and it still does real work — but do NOT read a symmetric, near-symmetric or byte-identical result as a fetch bug.**
- **Dedup gotcha confirmed 2026-08-15: a Greenhouse posting's canonical URL can be stored in query-string form (`?gh_jid=NNNNNN`), not just path form (`/jobs/{id}`).** A dedup check that only strips trailing path segments to find the ID will miss these and misreport an already-tracked posting as new — extract the `gh_jid` param value too, not just the last path segment. Caught on Pinterest, which stores its canonical URL as `https://www.pinterestcareers.com/jobs/?gh_jid={id}`.
- **Dedup key: prefer a stable ID over the full URL string.** Workday job URLs can drift on the human-readable slug (punctuation/spacing changes) while the trailing requisition ID stays constant, e.g. `..._R227774-1`. When comparing against `seen_jobs.json`, extract the req ID (regex `_(R\d+)(-\d+)?$`) and match on that as a fallback if the exact URL isn't found. Same idea applies to Greenhouse (`gh_jid` / numeric ID in the URL) and SmartRecruiters (numeric posting ID) — the descriptive URL slug Google/SmartRecruiters appends can change without the ID changing.
- **🔴 CRITICAL dedup bug — the Workday `-N` revision-suffix strip MUST be digit-guarded (found 2026-08-20).** The rule above says to extract the req ID and strip a trailing `-\d+` revision suffix to get the base ID. Applied naively this **destroys dash-form req IDs**: `R-425252` → stripping `-425252` leaves the bare string `R`, which then substring-matches *every* dash-form req ID in `seen_jobs.json`, so every candidate looks already-tracked. On 2026-08-20 this silently over-suppressed **123 J&J and 23 Humana candidates** on a dispatch group's first pass — a near-miss that would have reported those companies as "0 new" with no error anywhere. **Correct rule: only strip the trailing `-\d+` if the remainder still contains a digit.** `R-091172-1` → `R-091172` ✅ (remainder has digits, strip is safe). `R-425252` → keep as-is ✅ (remainder `R` has no digits, do not strip). This affects every dash-form tenant (J&J `R-NNNNNN`, Humana `R-NNNNNN`) and is invisible in output — it fails toward false negatives, so it will never announce itself.
- **Dedup gotcha (found 2026-08-20): `seen_jobs.json` stores some Workday URLs with a trailing `/apply` path segment**, e.g. `https://adobe.wd5.myworkdayjobs.com/external_experienced/job/Lehi/Sr-Data-Solution-Engineer_R170420/apply`. An ID-extraction regex anchored to end-of-string (`_(R\d+)(-\d+)?$`) misses these entirely — Adobe's R170420 and R169523-1 were both false-positive "new core" candidates until `/apply` was stripped first. **Strip any trailing `/apply` before extracting, and match `_ID` anywhere in the URL rather than anchoring to end-of-string.**
- **🔴 CRITICAL — a dedup regex can fail to ZERO keys, not just to one key (found 2026-08-24).** The standing "extract the `_ID` from the slug" rule above is commonly implemented as `_([A-Za-z]?\d[\w.\-]*)$`. That pattern **matches nothing at all** on two very common req-ID shapes, because the optional *single* letter cannot cover a `R-` dash form or a two-letter prefix: `_R-427706` ✗ and `_JR20615` ✗. On 2026-08-24 this produced `distinctkeys=0` for **Humana, VSP, Teladoc and Cast and Crew**, silently falling back to exact-URL-string matching. **Correct pattern: `_([A-Za-z]{0,3}[-_.]?\d[\w.\-]*)$`.**
- **🔴🔴 CRITICAL (2026-08-27) — A UUID PATH SEGMENT YIELDS ZERO KEYS UNDER ALL THREE MATCHERS, AND THE 2026-08-26 LETTER BUDGET IS WHAT CAUSES IT.** Lever (`jobs.lever.co/{co}/{uuid}`), Ashby (`jobs.ashbyhq.com/{co}/{uuid}`), Rippling (`ats.rippling.com/{slug}/jobs/{uuid}`) and Ross Stores (`/search/jobdetails/{slug}/{uuid}`) all key on a bare 36-char UUID. It has **no `_` separator**, is **not all-digits**, and the whole-segment letter budget (`len(re.sub(r'[^A-Za-z]','',seg)) <= 3`) **rejects it by construction** — a UUID carries far more than 3 letters. On 2026-08-27 this produced `distinctkeys = 0` at **Versapay, Foodsmart, DNC, Aurora Solar and Ross Stores simultaneously** (and independently at Easy Dynamics), silently degrading all six to exact-URL matching — and **Ross's slug is title-derived, so it is exposed to drift**, failing toward false positives. **Fix: index any path segment matching `^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$` verbatim, EXEMPT from the letter budget.** The `distinctkeys > 0` assertion is what surfaced it — keep it.
- **🔴 `ashby_jid` MUST BE IN THE QUERY-STRING ID LIST — Shopify yields ZERO keys without it (found 2026-08-27 r2).** Shopify's canonical URL is `shopify.com/careers?ashby_jid={uuid}` — a **query-string UUID with no path ID at all**, so the path matchers find nothing and, without `ashby_jid` in the query-string key list, `distinctkeys = 0` and the board silently degrades to exact-URL matching. **It cost nothing the run it was found**, because Shopify's URLs carry no slug to drift — but that is luck, not safety: **on any board whose slug does drift the identical defect fails toward FALSE POSITIVES, which no `distinctkeys` assertion catches** (same direction and same blind spot as the documented Adobe bare-segment trap). ⚠️ **Note the asymmetry with Ashby's other URL form:** `jobs.ashbyhq.com/{co}/{uuid}` is a *path* UUID and is already covered by the UUID rule — so a helper can pass every Ashby board and still fail Shopify. **Standing query-string ID key list: `gh_jid`, `ashby_jid`, `jobId`, `jobkey`/`jk`, `POSTING_ID`+`LOCATION_ID` (compound), `ccId`, `req_id`.**
- **🔴🔴 THE FREQUENCY GUARD'S POSTING-IDENTITY FUNCTION MUST BE THE LONGEST ID KEY, AND CONSTANTS MUST BE STRIPPED BEFORE IT RUNS (2026-08-27).** The 2026-08-26 r2 correction — count ≥3 distinct **postings**, not ≥3 distinct stored URLs — is right, but it is silently defeated by a naive choice of *which* extracted key identifies a posting. **Two groups hit opposite versions of this in the same run:**
  - Using the **lexical minimum**: on Amex-shape URLs (`…/sites/CX_1/job/26012386`) the keys are `{'1','26012386','cx_1'}` and the minimum is the constant `'1'`, so **all 69 stored Amex URLs collapsed onto one "posting"** and `cx_1` looked like a 1-posting key. The guard dropped nothing.
  - Using the **longest**: at Hilton, `cx_1009` (7 chars) beat the real job ID `217847` (6 chars), collapsing 7 stored URLs to 1 posting. The guard dropped nothing.
  - **Correct rule, both halves required: (1) use the LONGEST extracted ID key as the posting identity, AND (2) before the guard runs, mark any key present on ≥90% of that employer's stored URLs as a CONSTANT and exclude it from identity computation entirely.** Longest alone is not sufficient — Hilton proves it.
  - **🔴 A guard that reports "0 dropped" must be VERIFIED against a known collapse (`cx_1`) before it is believed.** Its output is otherwise indistinguishable between "working correctly with nothing to catch" (which is genuinely common — see the r2 note on the 9–22 figure) and "structurally unable to fire."
- **🔴 ORACLE RECRUITING CLOUD: the scoping rule must run BEFORE the underscore matcher, not merely instead of the bare-segment matcher (2026-08-27).** `CX_1009` / `CX_1` **contain an underscore**, so `_([A-Za-z]{0,3}[-_.]?\d[\w.\-]*)` extracts `1009` / `1` from every posting *before* any scoping branch is reached. On 2026-08-27 Hilton presented the documented symptom exactly — **37 of 37 candidates suppressed, 0 new, no exception, and a healthy-looking `distinctkeys=8`** — and the frequency guard did not save it (see the bullet above). **On an Oracle-RC host, CLEAR the key set and index ONLY the segment following `/job/`.** After the fix Hilton went 0 → 32 genuinely-untracked.
- **🔴 ADP WORKFORCE NOW YIELDS ONE-CHARACTER KEYS — the Amex `CX_1` collision family in an undocumented carrier (2026-08-27).** Parallels' stored URLs are `workforcenow.adp.com/…?cid=…&ccId=9201289910657_2&type=JS&lang=en_US&jobId=9201339260824_1`. The standard matcher fires on the `_2` of `ccId` and the `_1` of `jobId`, yielding bare keys **`2`** and **`1`**. ⚠️ **The ≥3-distinct-postings guard is NOT a backstop here** — only 1–2 stored postings carry each. **Fix: unconditionally drop any extracted key that is a bare 1–2 character digit string, independent of frequency.**
- **🔴 iCIMS: the employer token is the LAST `-` segment of the subdomain — record the RULE, stop enumerating the hosts (2026-08-27).** The documented five Post Holdings variants are now **eight**: `michaelfoodshrjobs-` (143), `postconsumerbrandshrjobs-` (44), `postconsumerbrandssljobs-` (40), `michaelfoodssljobs-` (24), **`bobevanshrjobs-` (20, new)**, **`bobevanssljobs-` (11, new)**, `postholdingsjobs-` (7), `postconsumerbrandscasljobs-` (1). PetSmart serves three (`2careers-`, `1cacareers-`, `crcareers-`). The last-segment rule handles all eleven. **The enumerated list has now grown twice; it is not a list, it is a rule.**
- **🔴 PetSmart/Cadient: `POSTING_ID` is a TEMPLATE id, not a posting id (2026-08-27).** Adding `POSTING_ID` to the query-string extraction (ruled 2026-08-26 r2) is necessary but **not sufficient** — one `POSTING_ID` is served at many `LOCATION_ID`s as separate postings (`4956040045` at **9** locations, `73355831856` at **8**). Keying on `POSTING_ID` alone collapsed 58 net candidates onto **13** keys, failing toward false negatives — the Radancy org-ID trap in a new carrier. **Fix: key on the compound `POSTING_ID`+`LOCATION_ID`** (58 → 70 keys).
- **🔴 `/en-US` LOCALE-DRIFT LIST — add Dick's Sporting Goods (2026-08-27).** Its one non-exact suppression (`Compliance Insights Analyst`, `202624019`) was stored as `dickssportinggoods.wd1.myworkdayjobs.com/`**`en-US`**`/DSG/job/…` against a served `/DSG/job/…`. **Current list: J&J, Cigna, eBay, Marsh, Dick's.**
  - **The standing "verify the dedup key actually varies across postings" sanity check catches this direction too.** It was written for the *constant*-key collapse (Radancy org ID); a **zero**-key result is the same class of silent failure and the same check catches it — assert `distinctkeys > 0` as well as `distinctkeys > 1`.
  - **✅ CONFIRMED FIXED — the corrected pattern cleared all four zero-key failures**: Humana **0 → 60** distinct keys, VSP **0 → 12**, Teladoc **0 → 2**, Cast and Crew **0 → 2**. Teladoc's `_JR20615` and Humana's `_R-427706` both key correctly, and **the digit-guard is confirmed load-bearing** — `R-426591` is correctly NOT stripped. Ship the `{0,3}` form; do not "simplify" it back.
- **🔴 A BARE ID-SHAPED PATH SEGMENT WITH NO `_` SEPARATOR YIELDS *ZERO* DEDUP KEYS — and it fails toward FALSE POSITIVES, which no existing sanity check catches (found 2026-08-24 r2).** `careers.adobe.com/us/en/job/R166535/{slug}` defeats all three standing matchers at once: `R166535` has **no underscore** (so `_([A-Za-z]{0,3}[-_.]?\d[\w.\-]*)` misses it), is **not all-digits** (so the numeric-segment matcher misses it), and **does not lead with digits** (so the Progressive `\d{5,}`-prefix matcher misses it). It produced exactly the three false positives already documented as Adobe's dual-scheme trap — **R166535, R169988, R170588**. Note the direction: the zero-key case documented above degraded to exact-URL matching and hid postings; **this one re-reports already-seen postings as new, and the `distinctkeys` assertions do not fire.** **Fix: also index any bare path segment matching `^[A-Za-z]{0,3}[-_.]?\d[\w.\-]*$`, with the same digit-guarded `-N` strip.** This also covers **Humana's `/job/{reqId}/{slug}` middle-segment shape** — see the per-company dedup traps.
- **🔴 `gh_jid` QUERY-STRING IDs AND PATH-SEGMENT IDs MUST SHARE ONE KEY NAMESPACE — a second, independent way to break Pantheon that produces the IDENTICAL symptom (found 2026-08-25 r2).** The `host+path` digit-guard below is correct and necessary, but it is **not sufficient**: if the helper files query-string IDs and path-segment IDs into *separate* namespaces, Pantheon's **stored** path form `job-boards.greenhouse.io/pantheon/jobs/8056205` never matches the **served** query form `pantheon.io/about/careers/detail?gh_jid=8056205`. That re-reported all 6 Pantheon candidates as new — **including both `Senior Analytics Engineer` reqs (8056205, 8056238), which are precisely the postings the documented digit-guard fix exists to protect.** Fails toward **FALSE POSITIVES**, and **no `distinctkeys` assertion catches it** (Pantheon reported a healthy `distinctkeys=30`); only the whole-tracker spot-check did. **Rule: one namespace for all extracted IDs, whatever part of the URL they came from.**
- **🔴 A CONSTANT *WHOLE-PATH* KEY COLLAPSES A COMPANY — the Radancy org-ID trap in a new carrier (found 2026-08-24 r2).** The standing constant-token rule is written for *path segments*; a normalised **`host+path`** key fails the same way and in the same direction (**false negatives**) whenever a board keeps its ID in the **query string**. `pantheon.io/about/careers/detail` is byte-identical for all 31 Pantheon postings, so two real `Senior Analytics Engineer` reqs (**8056205**, **8056238**) were suppressed against an unrelated stored req. **This affects three of the five Greenhouse URL shapes — Pantheon (`/about/careers/detail`), Cribl (`/job-detail/`) and Coalition (`/job-posting`).** **Fix: only add the `host+path` key when the path itself contains a digit.** (The `gh_jid` extraction rule is what actually carries dedup on these boards — see the Greenhouse recipe.)
- **🟢🔴 SHIP THE GENERIC FREQUENCY GUARD: DROP ANY DEDUP KEY THAT MAPS TO ≥3 DISTINCT STORED POSTINGS. It replaces every per-carrier special case above (found 2026-08-26, measured independently by three dispatch groups).** This is the single highest-value dedup rule in this file, on two counts.
  - 🔴 **COUNT DISTINCT POSTINGS (base req IDs), NOT DISTINCT STORED URLs — corrected 2026-08-26 r2.** As originally written ("≥3 distinct stored URLs") the guard **fires on a single posting stored under three URL shapes**, which is a false-drop risk. Measured: `r2625625` is **one** Hartford req (`Data & AI Delivery Lead`) stored under the public form, the `wday/cxs` form and a `-1` revision; `r-425799` is **one** Humana req (`Business Intelligence Lead`) stored under two `careers.humana.com` shapes plus the Workday shape. Both were harmless only because they also matched on exact URL. **The dual-scheme and locale-drift rules guarantee legitimate 1-posting/3-URL cases exist, so this is not an edge case.**
  - ⚠️ **The "~9–22 hot keys" figure is IMPLEMENTATION-DEPENDENT — never use it as a health expectation.** Four groups independently measured **2** on 2026-08-26 r2. That is the per-carrier rules working *upstream*: Oracle-RC scoping keeps `CX_1`/`CX_1009` out of the index entirely, the Radancy last-segment rule keeps org IDs out, and the whole-segment letter budget keeps Workday location slugs out — so the guard has little left to catch. A low count is correct behaviour, not a broken guard.
  - **The blast radius without it is total and silent.** Measured 2026-08-26: **American Express reported 100/100 candidates as dedup hits and Pinterest 24/24** — every candidate "matched something," so both boards would have reported 0 new with no error anywhere. A genuine new core match (Pinterest `Sr. Staff Software Engineer, Big Data Platform`, `gh_jid=7494956`) was recoverable *only* because the guard was in place. **Neither `distinctkeys > 0` nor `distinctkeys > 1` fires on this** — the keys vary fine board-wide; it is the *stored* side that collapses.
  - **It is carrier-independent and caught every documented trap in one pass**, with no per-ATS logic: Radancy org IDs (`34088` ×196, `38852` ×13, `43250` ×6), **Amex `1` and `cx_1` (×69 stored URLs each — the `CX_1` trap, now quantified)**, Hilton `1009`/`cx_1009` (×7 each), Comcast `45483` (×11), the Workday location slugs below, and the constant `host+path` keys for Pantheon, Cribl, Coalition, Prize Picks, Direct Travel and the Greenhouse embed form. Typical effect: 1,750 tracker URLs → ~1,761 distinct keys after dropping ~9–22 hot keys.
  - **Prefer this over adding another per-carrier rule.** The Oracle-RC, Radancy, ADP and `host+path` rules above all remain correct and worth keeping as documentation of *why* — but a new collapse family does not need a new rule, it needs this guard.
- **🔴 THE BARE-PATH-SEGMENT MATCHER IS WRONG AS WRITTEN — IT INDEXES WORKDAY *LOCATION* SEGMENTS AS IDs (found 2026-08-26, independently by two dispatch groups).** The documented pattern `^[A-Za-z]{0,3}[-_.]?\d[\w.\-]*$` looks like it caps letters at 3, but **the trailing `[\w.\-]*` swallows arbitrary letters**, so any location slug containing a street or store number matches. Confirmed live: `belfast-10-mays-meadow` (Allstate ×15), `hkgn-348-kwun-tong-road` (Cigna ×14), `PA---Philadelphia-1701-John-F-Kennedy-Blvd` (Comcast ×12), `Kohls-Corporate-Offices-0900` (×10), `VA---Reston-11951-Freedom-Dr-Ste-900` (Allstate ×5), `de-newark-2051-old-coochs-bridge-rd` (Cigna ×4). Workday's job URLs are `/job/{location}/{slug}_{reqId}`, so this is a whole *class* of collapse, not a handful of strings. Fails toward **false negatives**.
  - **Fix: enforce the letter budget on the WHOLE segment** — `len(re.sub(r'[^A-Za-z]', '', seg)) <= 3`. Removing these keys cost **zero** true suppressions (399 before and after on the group that measured it) and cleared every hot key.
  - **Corollary: the Oracle-RC "key only on the segment following `/job/`" rule MUST stay scoped to Oracle RC.** Applied globally it indexes exactly this Workday location segment, because on Workday the segment following `/job/` *is* the location.
- **🔴 PLAIN 8-DIGIT REQ IDs COLLIDE ACROSS EMPLOYERS — Cigna and American Express share the `26######` ID space (found 2026-08-26).** Cigna `26009848-1` was suppressed against a stored **American Express** posting, `careers.americanexpress.com/en/sites/CX_1/job/26009848`. Fails toward **false negatives**, and **no existing check catches it**: `distinctkeys` is healthy, the frequency guard does not fire (the key is on exactly one stored URL), and a same-company spot-check cannot see it because the collision is with a *different* company. It surfaced only from a cross-host audit of every non-exact suppression — 1 of 45.
  - **Fix / standing check: for every ID-key suppression, assert that the candidate's host and the stored URL's host belong to the same employer.** Only exact-URL matches and same-employer key matches should suppress.
  - ⚠️ **Cigna and Amex sit in different dispatch groups** (G1 and G3), so neither group can see the collision alone — this one has to be checked at compile time or with a whole-tracker host comparison. Both employers mint hundreds of `26######` reqs per run, so the exposure recurs every scan.
- **⚠️ Etsy has NO ID IN ITS URLs AT ALL — the `distinctkeys > 1` assertion FIRES for Etsy and that is a TRUE CHARACTERISTIC, not a bug (recorded 2026-08-24 r2 so a later scan does not "fix" it).** Four of five stored Etsy URLs are pure slugs (e.g. `/jobs/senior-director-product-analytics-brooklyn-new-york-united-states`); only one carries a trailing UUID. **Consequence: Etsy dedup is necessarily URL-string-based, and is therefore exposed to slug drift failing toward FALSE POSITIVES** — the same family as the documented Progressive drift, and with no ID to fall back on. Spot-check any "new" Etsy match against the whole tracker before reporting it.
- **🔴 `seen_jobs.json` holds locale-prefix and dual-scheme duplicates — normalise before comparing (found 2026-08-21 r2).** ~36 duplicate base-ID pairs exist in G1's 16 companies alone. Three causes: **J&J locale drift** (`…/jj/job/…` vs `…/en-US/jj/job/…`, ~20 pairs), **dual front-end/Workday schemes** (Cigna `jobs.thecignagroup.com/us/en/job/{id}/{slug}`; J&J `careers.jnj.com/en/jobs/{lowercase r-XXXXXX}/{slug}/` — note lowercase `r-` and no `-N` suffix), and **Allstate location-slug drift** (`USA---IL-Remote` vs `USA---IL--Remote-`). **"Strip a leading `/en-US` locale path segment" is a standard normalisation step**, and Cigna and J&J belong on the Adobe/Pfizer dual-scheme list.
  - 🔴 **eBay is ALSO on the locale-drift list (added 2026-08-26 r2)** — all 3 of its non-exact suppressions that run were stored as `ebay.wd5.myworkdayjobs.com/`**`en-us`**`/apply/job/…` against a served `/apply/job/…`. The leading-locale strip is load-bearing there and eBay was not previously listed.
  - 🔴 **Marsh exercises THREE normalisations at once on a single URL** — candidate `careers.marsh.com/global/en/job/R_352810` suppressed against stored `mmc.wd1.myworkdayjobs.com/en-US/mmc/job/…_R_352810-1` via the one-namespace key **plus** the `/en-US` strip **plus** the digit-guarded `-N` strip. All three are required; drop any one and it false-positives. *(Status conflicts between the two copies of a pair do not matter — per `rules.md` §3 an already-seen posting suppresses regardless of tag, and stored tags are never corrected.)*
- **🔴 BOTH TRACKERS ARE STORED AS LITERAL UTF-8, NOT `\uXXXX` ESCAPES (as of 2026-08-24 r2) — always pass `encoding="utf-8"` explicitly.** The r2 write used `json.dump(..., ensure_ascii=False)`, so non-ASCII characters are now real bytes in the file rather than ASCII escape sequences. **64 stored titles contain non-ASCII characters** — mostly en-dashes in titles like `Senior Data Engineer – Data Mapping & Integration` and `Manager, Data & Analytics — Apple Retail Online`. On Windows, `open(path)` with no `encoding=` argument defaults to **cp1252** and will raise `UnicodeDecodeError` on these files. This is the same family as the standing `PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8` requirement, but it bites on *read*, not just on print. Verified clean at write time: zero replacement characters, full round-trip. **Keep writing with `ensure_ascii=False`** — mixing the two styles across runs would churn the whole file's diff for no benefit.
- **⚠️ `seen_jobs.json`'s `jobs` map contains two STRING values, not job objects (found 2026-08-21).** The keys `_comment_r4` and `_comment_2026-08-07` are legacy narrative markers left inside the map by earlier scans. They are harmless for dedup — they don't start with `http`, so any URL-keyed lookup skips them — but **any code that iterates `jobs.values()` and calls `.get()` will crash with `AttributeError: 'str' object has no attribute 'get'`.** Type-guard with `isinstance(v, dict)`. Also note this means `len(jobs)` overstates the posting count by 2 (1,687 entries = 1,685 postings as of 2026-08-21). Leave them in place; they're provenance.
- **🔴 CRITICAL dedup trap — TalentBrew/Radancy: the second-to-last path segment is the ORG id, not a job id (found 2026-08-21).** Canonical URLs are `/job/{city}/{slug}/{orgId}/{jobId}` — and `{orgId}` is **identical on every posting for that employer**: United Health `34088`, Quest Diagnostics `38852`, Lyra Health `43250`. A generic "scan the path segments for an ID-shaped token" matcher indexes the org ID and **collapses the entire company onto a single dedup key**, so every candidate matches something and the scan reports "0 new" with no error anywhere. On 2026-08-21 this produced *United Health 0 new / Quest 0 new* on a dispatch group's first pass; after the fix, UHG went 0 → 32 candidates and Quest 0 → 21, including 4 genuine core matches that would have been lost silently.
  - **Correct rule: if the last two path segments are both all-digits, key on the LAST one only.**
  - Same family as the ADP `itemID`-vs-`jobId`, Home Depot `ref`-vs-numeric and Travelers `ref`-vs-numeric traps, and it fails toward false negatives exactly as `rules.md` §7 warns. Affects every Radancy/TalentBrew site: United Health, Quest Diagnostics, Lyra Health, Sysco, Comcast, Dick's Sporting Goods.
  - **Sanity check that would have caught it:** if a company returns *zero* new candidates while its board count moved, verify the dedup key varies across postings before believing the result.
- **🔴 DEDUP TRAP — Home Depot and Travelers key on the numeric job ID in the URL, NOT the CWS `ref`/Req ID.** *(Found 2026-08-20 r2; folded up from that section.)* `seen_jobs.json` stores these as `careers.homedepot.com/job/{numericId}/{slug}/`. Keying on `ref` (`Req178712`, …) — the obvious choice, since that is what the CWS API calls the requisition — matched **0 of 18** Home Depot postings and would have produced 17 false "new core" items. Matching on `job/{numericId}` matched 18/18. Same family as the ADP `itemID`-vs-`jobId` trap.
- **🔴 DEDUP TRAP — Paradox sites key on the 32-hex `uniqueID`, NOT `requisitionID`.** *(Found 2026-08-20 r2; folded up.)* `seen_jobs.json` stores McDonald's/Marriott URLs as `/{slug}/job/{uniqueID}`. Deduping on `requisitionID` returns zero hits for every posting and would have reported all 11 McDonald's items as new. Use `originalURL`/`uniqueID` — and note `originalURL` is **relative**, see the Paradox.ai recipe.
- **🔴 Adobe has a second dedup scheme beyond the `/apply` suffix.** *(Found 2026-08-20 r2; folded up.)* Some postings are stored under the **Phenom front-end form** `careers.adobe.com/us/en/job/{REQID}/{slug}`, not the Workday form — a dedup keyed only on `adobe.wd5.myworkdayjobs.com` false-positives R166535, R169988 and R170588 as new core. **✅ Root cause identified 2026-08-24 r2: the bare `R166535` segment yields ZERO keys against all three standing matchers — see the bare-ID-segment rule above. Fixing that fixes these three.** Cigna, J&J and Pfizer carry the same dual-scheme shape (see the locale-prefix/dual-scheme rule below).
- **🔴 DEDUP TRAPS THAT FAIL TOWARD *FALSE POSITIVES* (found 2026-08-21 r2) — a direction none of the existing sanity checks catch.** Every trap above fails toward false negatives, and the standing check ("zero new while the board moved") is built for that direction only. These two fail the other way: an already-seen posting is re-reported as new, and nothing anywhere looks wrong.
  - **SmartRecruiters slug suffix.** `seen_jobs.json` stores Logic20/20's entry as `.../Logic2020Inc/744000144140099-data-architect` while the API's canonical form is the bare numeric `.../Logic2020Inc/744000144140099`. The trailing token is alphabetic, so the digit-guarded `-\d+` strip correctly does *not* fire — and the two forms never collide. The posting reported as a **new core match** until spot-checked. **Fix: also index the leading numeric run of any segment matching `^(\d{6,})-[A-Za-z]`.**
  - **Progressive slug drift.** Canonical URLs are `careers.progressive.com/jobs/{numericId}-{slug}/`. The tracker holds `18060418-a-slash-b-testing-analyst-lead`; the live board now serves the same req as `18060418-a-slash-b-testing-`**`data`**`-analyst-lead` (the title itself gained the word "Data"). A tokenizer harvesting only *all-digit* path segments extracts nothing from either form. **Fix: on Progressive, key on the leading `\d{5,}` prefix of the last path segment.**
  - **Corollary — verify a "new core match" against the whole tracker before reporting it.** Both of these were caught only by an explicit URL spot-check, not by any automated guard.
- **🔴 Constant-token key collapse generalizes BEYOND Radancy (found 2026-08-21 r2).** The org-ID trap above is one instance of a broader failure: indexing a bare last path segment collapses a whole company onto one key whenever that segment is constant. Confirmed live on **ADP Workforce Now** (`recruitment.html` — Overhaul, Parallels) and **ADP myjobs** (`job-details` — Direct Travel); Direct Travel silently reported **0 new** on the first pass and **4 new** after the guard. **Rule: only index a bare path segment as an ID if it contains a digit and is not a page filename (`.html`/`.aspx`/`.php`/`.json`).**
- **🔴 BORDERLINE BAR — the title must name `data`, `database`, `analytics` or `BI`, OR a named technology. Ruled by the operator 2026-08-21 r2, WIDENED 2026-08-26.** A title naming none of those is **enumeration noise**: not core, not borderline, **not reported and not persisted**. Explicitly NOT qualifying on their own: `reporting`, `governance`, `insights`, `intelligence` (unless *business* intelligence), `informatics`, `metrics`. So "Sr. Analyst, External Reporting", "Sr Staff Threat Intelligence Analyst", "Regional Manager, Clinical Informatics" and "Manager, Business Insights" are all noise, while "Senior Scrum Master – Data" and "Director, GL Pricing Analytics" are borderline. Full statement and worked table: `rules.md` §1.
  - **🔴 NEW 2026-08-26 — `database` is a QUALIFYING TOKEN and the ORG-CONTEXT TEST IS RETIRED.** Any title naming `database` reaches **borderline** at minimum, at every company, **regardless of what org it sits in**. The operator: *"if the title has anything to do with database, analytics, bi, I should see it."* **The standing gloss "`Database` is not `data`; DBRE is SRE work" is REVERSED** — Adobe's `Sr. Database Reliability Engineer` (R170522), noise for five consecutive runs, is **borderline** from now on. Do not bin a Database title for lacking a data-architecture org signal. This also closes the inherited Apple SRE question.
  - **🔴 NEW 2026-08-26 — NAMED TECHNOLOGIES QUALIFY AND THEY SPLIT.** The operator: *"if it lists tech such as snowflake, sql, python, core it, if it's more cloud based like aws/azure/docker/kubernetes borderline it."*
    - **Data-stack → CORE:** `snowflake` `sql` `python` `kafka` `hadoop` `hive` `impala` `spark` `databricks` `tableau` `dbt` `airflow` `looker` `power bi` `bigquery` `redshift` `etl` `informatica`. So Adobe's `Cloud Engineer 3 - Python, SQL` is now **core** (it was noise for four runs).
    - **Cloud/infra → BORDERLINE:** `aws` `azure` `gcp` `docker` `kubernetes` `terraform`.
    - **Both → CORE** (the data-stack token wins).
    - ⚠️ **Key on NAMED technologies, not the generic word `cloud`** — a bare `Cloud Architect`/`Cloud Engineer` naming no product is untouched by this and stays under the generic-architect exclusion.
    - ⚠️ **`AI`/`ML` are on NEITHER list** — `Director of AI Engineering`, `Sr Manager, AI Platform Architecture`, `Staff Decision Scientist` stay **noise**. The ruling deliberately does not open the AI-titled family.
    - 🔴 **Hard exclusions run FIRST and still win.** This bar only decides what happens to a title that already survived them: `Senior MLOps Engineer II` stays excluded on the MLOps head noun, a `Data Scientist - Python/Spark` stays excluded, a `Product Manager, Data Platform (AWS)` stays excluded. Order: non-US → hard exclusions → this bar.
  - **🔵 Both 2026-08-26 additions WIDEN the bar, so the usual coverage-vs-criteria warning inverts — but the conclusion is the same: the SWEEP DOES NOT CHANGE.** The enumeration regex already carried the stack tokens (that is why these titles were being found and then dropped) and must keep carrying `informatics|information architect|business information`, bare `intelligence`, and `kafka|hadoop|spark|streaming|snowflake|tableau|databricks|etl`. **Add `database` to the title net** if it is not already there. Expect reported volume to rise by roughly **+5–15 borderline and a small number of core per run**.
  - **⚠️ THIS IS A CRITERIA CHANGE, NOT A COVERAGE ONE. It must NOT shrink the sweep or the title regex.** The enumeration regex still carries `informatics|information architect|business information`, bare `intelligence` and the stack tokens — see the bullet immediately below, which is still fully in force. Those exist to *find* titles; the bar above only decides what happens to a title once found. An informatics or intelligence title that is genuinely hands-on data architecture is **core**; otherwise it is noise. Never trim the sweep to match the bar — `rules.md`'s coverage-vs-criteria warning exists for exactly this confusion.
  - Effect on the r2 numbers, for calibration: 41 borderline under the broad bar → **~26** under this one.
- **🔴 The client-side title regex has a confirmed gap — carry bare `intelligence` and the stack tokens (found 2026-08-21 r2).** G2 audited Humana's 361 *non*-matching titles against a second net and found two families a `data|analy|business intelligence|\bBI\b|informatic` filter misses **entirely**: **"Senior Kafka Developer"** and the **"Decision Intelligence Engineer"** family (Lead ×2, Senior, Principal). Both carry bare *Intelligence* with no *Business*, and no data token at all. Nothing was lost that run (all five were already-seen) but the gap is real and fails toward false negatives. **The standing regex should permanently carry bare `intelligence` plus `kafka|hadoop|spark|streaming|snowflake|tableau|databricks`** — these map directly onto `profile.md`'s must-have signals, so a title naming the stack is a match even when it names no data noun.
- **NEW TRAP (found 2026-08-20): SmartRecruiters' `/postings` endpoint returns HTTP 200 for *any* token, valid or not.** `api.smartrecruiters.com/v1/companies/{anything}/postings` returns `{"offset":0,"limit":100,"totalFound":0,"content":[]}` — confirmed identical for a deliberately garbage token (`zzqqxxnotreal999`) and for a real-but-wrong company. **The 200 status carries no signal whatsoever; only `totalFound` does.** This can be misread as a blocked→working flip (it was, briefly, on an iHerb probe). Same family as the Cribl Ashby decoy token. Affects Logic20/20 (`Logic2020Inc`) and NBC Universal (`NBCUniversal3`) — always sanity-check that `totalFound` is non-zero and plausible before concluding a SmartRecruiters board is empty.
- **🔵 BORDERLINE ITEMS ARE NOW PERSISTED (policy changed 2026-08-20) — this supersedes ONLY the "don't add to `seen_jobs.json`" half of the rule below. Borderline items are still REPORTED in their own separate section.** The operator, 2026-08-20: *"Make sure borderline jobs still go in the borderline section, I don't want those mixed, I just want them tracked."* Keep listing them under a dedicated `## Borderline` heading in `matches/{date}.md`, never merged into the core-match sections — persisting a borderline item does **not** make it a core match, and the core-vs-borderline judgment call is unchanged. The change is purely that a borderline item gets written to `seen_jobs.json` *after* it has been reported, so it doesn't get reported again. The operator: *"For borderline jobs, mark them as seen. I do look at them and consider them, I don't want to keep seeing the same borderline jobs though."* Add every borderline posting to `seen_jobs.json` with a **`"status": "borderline"`** field (core matches carry no `status` field). Dedup treats a borderline entry exactly like a core entry — it suppresses the repeat — so the recurring buckets stop coming back. The tag exists purely so borderline items remain distinguishable if matching policy changes later (as with the 2026-08-06 "Data Analyst is core" sweep, and the 17 borderline→core reclassifications found on 2026-08-20). **648 borderline items from every report 2026-08-08 → 2026-08-20 were backfilled on 2026-08-20.** The still-current parts of the rule below — *what counts as* borderline vs core, the management/hands-on exception, the PM-prefix exclusion — are unchanged; only the "don't add to `seen_jobs.json`" half is retired.
- **🔴 RETIRED 2026-08-21 — a dedup hit IS always a suppress.** The operator: *"Anything already seen, just leave it seen, borderline or core. I don't want to see them again."* An already-seen posting is never re-reported, whatever its tag, and stored `status` tags are never "corrected." There is no `## Reclassifications` section any more. See `rules.md` §3. The superseded rule is kept below for provenance only — **do not follow it**: <s>**🔵 Reclassification escape hatch — a dedup hit against a `status: borderline` entry is NOT automatically a suppress.** Because borderline items now live in `seen_jobs.json`, the default behavior would silently hide a posting that a later scan judges to be a genuine **core** match — exactly the promotion path that surfaced 17 items on 2026-08-20 and the 42-item Data Analyst sweep on 2026-08-06. **Rule: if a candidate dedup-matches an existing entry tagged `"status": "borderline"` but this scan's judgment is that it's a core match, report it — under a `## Reclassifications` heading, kept separate from both the new-core and borderline sections — and flag it for the operator's ruling rather than silently promoting or silently suppressing it.** On their ruling, either drop the `status` field (promoted to core) or leave it as-is (stays borderline, stays suppressed). A borderline→borderline match suppresses normally, with no report entry.</s>
- **🔴 Consequence: NEVER report a borderline bucket rolled up without URLs.** Recurring buckets used to be summarized as e.g. "Capital One — 52 recurring management-tier items, not itemized" to save report space. **That is now a defect**: an item with no URL cannot be persisted, so it resurfaces as "new" on every future scan indefinitely. This bit the 2026-08-20 scan — ~23 Capital One items were rolled up without links and could not be backfilled. Every borderline item a dispatch group finds must come back with its full canonical URL, even if the report prose groups them for readability.
- **Borderline titles — don't auto-add, list separately.** Profile targets *Engineer* / *Architect* (hands-on) / *BI Developer* / **Data Analyst** roles (Data Analyst added to core targets 2026-08-06 — no longer borderline, treat plain "Data Analyst" titles as a normal match same as "Data Engineer"). Titles like "Data Manager," "Advisor," or "Director"/"Manager" without clear hands-on data-architecture ownership are still borderline — report them in the matches file under a "borderline" heading but don't add to `seen_jobs.json`. Exception: management/Director titles are OK to treat as core matches if the JD explicitly describes hands-on ownership of data architecture/platform (per `profile.md`'s "open to management if still hands-on"). **Data Analyst reclassification pass COMPLETED 2026-08-06 r4.** A full 86-company reclassification sweep found and moved 42 previously-borderline "Data Analyst" postings to core (added to `seen_jobs.json`, dated 2026-08-06 with a `note` field marking them as reclassifications for provenance) — largest sets: Capital One (24, out of its ~220-entry Data Analyst/Analytics Manager-tier bucket), Centene (5), Fanatics Betting (3), Allstate (2). Full breakdown in `matches/2026-08-06-r4.md`. **2 judgment-call candidates were reviewed but deliberately NOT reclassified** because the title isn't literally "Data Analyst" (Ross Stores "Senior Analyst, Business Intelligence"; Marriott "BI Reporting Analyst, TPO Data & Insights") — flagged for the operator's own call rather than auto-added. Going forward, any *new* borderline "Data Analyst"-adjacent title should be judged directly against the current policy (plain "Data Analyst" titles are core, same as Data Engineer) rather than assumed stale — this was a one-time catch-up, not an ongoing caveat. **Precedent confirmed twice now**: Life360's "Director, Data Engineering (AI Native)" (JD-verified 2026-08-03) and Foodsmart's "Director, Data Platform" (2026-08-06 r3 — JD explicitly describes end-to-end ownership of pipeline orchestration, dbt project, and data modeling, "hands-on technical leader") both counted as core matches on this basis. When a Director-tier title shows up with an ambiguous JD, it's worth the extra read before defaulting to borderline.
- **Coverage catch-up ≠ new postings — say so explicitly.** When a site was previously blocked/under-paginated and a fix suddenly surfaces a big batch of "new" matches, flag in the matches report that this is a visibility fix, not overnight hiring growth (happened with Capital One's pagination fix, then again with Allstate/Cigna/Humana/CVS/J&J/Airbnb unblocking).
- **Closures are out of scope (removed 2026-08-13) — don't check them.** Do not spot-check whether previously-tracked postings are still open, and don't report closures in the matches file. This used to be standard practice (the old closure-signal notes — empty-og:title / 404 / HTTP 410 / `"active":false` checks — now live in `scan-history.md`, kept only in case discovery methods ever need them again), but it was the single biggest cost driver in a scan (an individual direct-fetch spot-check per previously-tracked ID) for information the operator doesn't need. Scans should only discover and report new postings against `seen_jobs.json`.
- **🔴 NEVER dedup on title + location. Always dedup on the req ID.** Same-title/different-ID postings are routine at large employers — Capital One had R248928-1 and R248849-1 both titled "Senior Data Analyst - Card" as genuinely distinct reqs. Title-matching silently suppresses real new postings.
- **🔴 The `-N` revision suffix appears on plain-numeric IDs too, not just `R`-prefixed Workday ones.** The dedup regex above is `R`-anchored; that is not sufficient. Cigna's tracked ID `26007037` resurfaced as `26007037-1`. Strip a trailing `-\d+` on numeric IDs as well — with the same digit-guard as the `R-` form.
- **`seen_jobs.json` is the ONLY authority for dedup.** Never dedup against `companies.md`'s prose summaries — the prose drifts from the tracker (confirmed drift at Quest Diagnostics, Ross Stores, Foodsmart and Kohl's, where a posting is tracked as core in `seen_jobs.json` but still described as borderline in prose).
- **Indeed borderline items must be persisted to `indeed_seen.json` too.** The borderline-persistence policy in `rules.md` applies to both trackers. Indeed's coverage window (`fromage=7`, page-1-only) makes an unpersisted item *more* likely to resurface within days than a company-list item, not less.
- **⚠️ Same-day r2+ scans: the compile step must diff both core AND borderline against every earlier same-day report.** A dispatch group only sees its own slice of companies, so it cannot do this itself. Borderline persistence has removed most of this need, but items from an earlier run the same day are not written to `seen_jobs.json` until that run is compiled.
- **When a site looks like an empty JS shell, look for SSR JSON in the raw HTML before assuming it needs a headless browser.** Cigna, Humana and CVS all looked JS-only and all embed job data server-side (Phenom People). Try a plain `curl` of the page — sometimes a direct job-search URL variant rather than the "search results" URL.
- **Retry blocked sites every scan.** Protections and rendering setups change over time — and the reverse happens too, so don't assume "still working" either. Companies that have flipped from blocked → working across scans so far: Geico, Kemper, BetMGM (2026-07-28), Allstate (further), Cigna, Humana, CVS, Airbnb, Direct Travel (2026-08-02); Adobe, Shopify, Pinterest, Apple's real-match query, United Health/Sysco (partial) (2026-08-03); Progressive, Airbnb (upgraded to fully-confirmed), Etsy (2026-08-04 morning); Apple (partial — 2026-08-04 r2); iHerb (2026-08-05 r2). Companies that have flipped working → blocked/regressed: **Apple (2026-08-04 morning)** — was flaky-but-sometimes-working since 2026-08-03, returned zero records on both retries that scan. **Apple then partially recovered the same day (2026-08-04 r2)** — retries a few hours later got HTTP 200 with a real non-empty payload again (not a dead shell), though the `search=` keyword param still doesn't actually filter results, so discovery remains unreliable even though the block itself eased. This flip-flopped twice in one day — never assume either direction holds without actually retrying. **2026-08-05 (morning): no blocked↔working flips** — Microsoft/Cotiviti still blocked, Apple still partial-with-broken-search, unchanged in direction. **2026-08-05 r2: iHerb flipped blocked → working** (Greenhouse token `iherb` discovered) — the only flip that scan; Microsoft and Cotiviti remained blocked, Apple remained partial with no direction change. **2026-08-06: iHerb flipped WORKING → BLOCKED (regressed)** — the same token that resolved cleanly the day before now returns HTTP 404 `{"status":404,"error":"Job board not found"}` on every endpoint variant, byte-identical across retries with different User-Agents — see iHerb section below for detail. Also 2026-08-06: **Cotiviti's failure mode changed shape** (AWS WAF CAPTCHA → silent HTTP 200 homepage with zero job data) without actually becoming visible — still fully blocked, just worth tracking that the block mechanism itself isn't static. **2026-08-06 r2 (same day, second scan): no blocked↔working flips** — Microsoft, Cotiviti, iHerb all still blocked, Apple still partial, unchanged in direction from the morning. **Cotiviti's failure mode changed shape a third time within the same day** — HTTP 405 this scan, after the silent-200 homepage this morning and the original WAF CAPTCHA before that; still zero visibility regardless. Never assume either direction without actually trying, in either direction.

## 2026-09-04 method corrections — READ FIRST, these supersede EVERYTHING below

Where these conflict with anything else in this file, **these win.**

> 📐 *Per the maintenance rule at the top, the `2026-09-03 r2` section was folded down and deleted on this pass, so **this is the only corrections section**. **This section is a PURE INDEX — the authoritative full text is at the home each item names, not here.** Write the next one the same way. ⚠️ **VERIFY the pointers before deleting — do not assume.** This pass: **33 pointer-carrying claims checked case-insensitively across all four homes; 33 of 33 resolved.** 🟢 **The FIVE-CLAUSE lesson held and produced ZERO false misses this pass — the first fold in five to do so** (match case-insensitively · choose tokens from the OLD text · prefer a distinctive phrase over a punctuation-bearing compound · search every home rather than asserting which one a claim should live in · normalise digit grouping). 🔵 **The reason it worked is worth copying: tokens were chosen mechanically from the old section's own text rather than retyped from memory, which is what generated the four prior folds' false misses.** 🟢 **Ninth consecutive fold in which running the verification was worth it.***

### 🔴 2026-09-04 — a FOURTH consecutive zero-drift fan-out, the FIRST with the reading side verified as well, and a silent defect found in `bin/` itself

- **🔴🔴 `jobscan_match.base_tier()` MAKES THE EXCLUDED-AUDIT LIST EMPTY BY CONSTRUCTION.** It early-returns `None` when `is_excluded()` is true, so any caller computing the audit's `would_be_tier` from it gets `None` for **every** excluded title and ships an empty `excluded_audit` — **with no symptom**, because `counts.excluded` still increments and every per-tier identity still balances. Found by G4b, then reproduced independently by G2 and by **the orchestrator's own Indeed pipeline, which had it**. Fix: match against `CORE_ITEMS` / `ANALYST_ITEMS` directly via `tokenize()` / `_matches_item()` with the exclusion branch bypassed. Proven in both directions before being trusted (`Summer 2027 Data Engineering Intern` → `None` vs `core`). 🔴 **This retroactively weakens every earlier run's audit-list zero** — a zero from a `base_tier()` caller was not a measurement. 🔵 **Same family as the `is_us()` tri-state bug and the `country_code` branch: invisible by construction, fails toward a clean-looking zero.** → *Dispatch-group deliverable contract; `rules.md` §4; `scan-history.md` 2026-09-04.*
- **🔴 TWO REAL UNDER-FETCHES, BOTH BEHIND A CLEAN EXIT.** **Sysco**: the narrow documented slug filter returned **2 candidates where the mandated enumeration net returns 20** — the narrow filter is **10% of the correct net**. **Home Depot**: a "stop when a pass adds no new URLs" rule returned **3,011 unique on 183 requests and exited cleanly**, because the CWS endpoint re-serves records across pages; the correct rule is **"short page or past `totalHits`"** → **6,217 on 276 requests**. ⚠️ **Do not assert `served == totalHits` at Home Depot** as the previous pass did — that gap is duplicate serving, not an under-fetch. 🔵 **Third independent demonstration that a clean exit is not a completeness proof.** → *Sysco and Home Depot rows; the mandated enumeration net.*
- **🔴 THE AMBIGUOUS-vs-DEDUP INTERACTION IS NO LONGER FREE — 11 postings, three groups, one run.** Mozilla (`Marketing Data Science Manager` served as three per-location postings, the bare-`Remote` one a stored exact-URL hit), Alpaca ×8 + UHG ×1, and Airbnb's `Mexico`. All six groups used the pinned pass-through order (`non_us_dropped → seen_suppressed → judged_away → ambiguous_flagged → new`), giving 11 correct suppressions; a short-circuiting order would have re-reported 11 tracked postings. **Raised for a ruling and recorded as OPEN — not closed by a scan.** → *`rules.md` §2 (open item); §4 filter order.*
- **🔴 MARSH'S NULL-RECORD METHOD IS REFUTED AND WOULD HAVE DROPPED A LIVE US POSTING.** The one distinct null (`R_359809`) is `Insurance Commercial Lines Senior Account Manager`, Denver CO — **US and live**, HTTP 200 with a complete `JobPosting` ld+json. 🔴 **The 410 test does not discriminate**: the garbage control *and* two live non-US reqs all return 410, while the null and a live US req return 200. **Resolve every null via the targeted `searchText` POST and add it to the candidate set.** Cost zero only because `classify()` returned `None`. → *Marsh row.*
- **🔴 A BLOCKED BOARD'S `failure_signature` IS A CONTRACT FIELD, NOT PROSE — EIGHTH instance of the fan-out pattern.** Cotiviti shipped `status:"blocked"` with `failure_signature: null`, the complete signature living in the per-company `notes` string. The compiler now **halts** if a blocked board has no signature anywhere, and **recovers-and-prints** if it is only in `notes`. **It must never normalise a value it cannot name.** → *Dispatch-group deliverable contract; `rules.md` §6.*
- **🟢 THE READING SIDE IS NOW ASSERTED, NOT JUST THE WRITING SIDE.** The compiler asserts, per tier per source, `new + ambiguous_flagged == len(items[])` — the check that would have caught the previous pass's wrong-nesting-level read. Combined with the per-tier per-company identity, both sides of the contract are now checked. **Fourth consecutive zero-drift fan-out on the writing side; zero identity failures.** → *Dispatch-group deliverable contract.*
- **🔴 BYTE-COUNT *AND* BODY-HASH DISCRIMINATORS FAILED TWO MORE WAYS.** **HCA**: six probe bodies, **six distinct SHA-1s** (per-request `CF-RAY`), and the garbage control (5,824 B) sits **inside** the real-path band (5,694–5,905 B) — neither size nor hash separates; header plus absent job-data tokens is the only test. **Cotiviti**: four bodies again identical in length (2,115 B) with **four distinct SHA-1s**, a 2nd confirmation. Plus **`curl -w size_download` with `--compressed` reported 6,623 B for a 33,739 B body** at iHerb. 🔴 **ASSERT CONTENT.** → *HCA Healthcare, Cotiviti and iHerb rows; Environment section.*
- **🔴 THE "64-TOKEN ENUMERATION NET" IS A PHANTOM — DELETE IT.** The authoritative net holds exactly **58** tokens, counted mechanically; "64" appears only in run prose and matches no text in the net. **The real evidence is the pool, and it reproduces**: Kohl's 3,575 (vs 3,576), Dick's 2,866 (vs 2,869), each run twice identically. The previous pass's 2,988/1,197 under-fetch was **an execution failure of that run, not a short token list.** ⚠️ Two further discrepancies **flagged, not asserted equivalent**: **Travelers 59 vs 60 recorded** (the 60 cannot be reconstructed) and **Wendy's 297 vs 254** (a strict widening, all 8 required tokens present). → *The mandated enumeration net; Kohl's, Dick's, Travelers and Wendy's rows.*
- **🔴 THE iCIMS CROSS-CHECK NEEDS A DOCUMENTED DEPTH BOUND.** The recipe says "≥6 keywords" but never how deep. Walking all 27 tokens to exhaustion is **a second full enumeration per keyword** and failed to terminate inside 35 minutes on both 10k boards. **Bounded to 40 pages/keyword the payout was unchanged** (still caught Ulta's six). **An unbounded cross-check is not more thorough, it just doesn't finish.** → *iCIMS recipe; Ulta and PetSmart rows.*
- **🔴 CIGNA'S CXS FACET BLOCK CARRIES `Country`, NOT JUST A JOB FAMILY — and the field name is TENANT-DEPENDENT.** Null `26008657` reads `Country=India`, provably non-US despite an unobtainable title. ⚠️ **The facet is named `Job Category` on that tenant, not `jobFamilyGroup` — iterate the facet list, never index it.** First case where the triage answered on **location** rather than family. → *Cigna row; Workday CXS recipe.*
- **🔴 CAPITAL ONE'S REVISION-SUFFIX ARTIFACT HAS A VISIBLE MECHANISM NOW.** The record's own **`bulletFields[0]`** holds the bare `R999963` while `externalPath` carries `R999963-1`, and the index keys the bare id. **Read the probe term off the record rather than deriving it by stripping.** → *Capital One row; Workday CXS recipe.*
- **🔴 AIRBNB SERVES HTML-ENTITY-ENCODED TITLES** (`&#038;`, `&#8211;`) — **unescape before `classify()`**, or you are not classifying the displayed title, which `rules.md` §4 requires. → *Airbnb row.*
- **🔴 ROSS'S SERVE-SIDE EMPTY LOCATION IS RESOLVABLE — the open handling item is CLOSED.** Req `e6532db5` reproduced a 2nd run, so it is a property of that requisition rather than a per-run event, and its **detail page carries JSON-LD `jobLocation[0].address.addressCountry = "US"`** with no city, state or `<address>` anywhere. **Resolve via the detail page rather than letting it fall to AMBIGUOUS.** → *Ross Stores row; global rules, location traps.*
- **🔴 iHERB'S BLOCKED-DETECTION CLAIM NEEDS NARROWING.** The false all-clear applies only to **URL query params** (`?keyword=`/`?search=`/`?q=` all return the full 60); the **POST body's own `key_words` field DOES discriminate** (true `total` 0, 120 B) and is cheaper than the facet control. → *iHerb row.*
- **⚠️ G5'S NINE-WAY CXS CONCURRENCY WALL WAS 177.6 s, NOT THE RECORDED 48.8 s** (Stryker alone accounts for it; 320 requests, no 429s, no unrecovered failures). **Treat 48.8 s as one draw, not a budget.** → *Dispatch groups section; Stryker row.*
- **🟢 STOP RULES — one hardened, two reversed, and the reversals are the point.** **Progressive: TWO consecutive zero-add passes were BOTH wrong** — `[269, 293, 309, 311, 311, 311, 313]`; a one- *and* a two-zero-add stop both ship **311 of 313**, and only the documented **three-consecutive** rule reaches 313 (**4th and strongest confirmation**). **Marriott converged on its FIRST pass**, a **third direction-flip in three runs** — **neither "converges" nor "doesn't" may be recorded as a board property.** **Stryker's repeat pass was NOT load-bearing** (+0), reversing last run's +6. **Airbnb converged on the first pass** where last run needed four — **the stop rule was right both times; a fixed pass count would have been wrong on one.** → *Progressive, Marriott, Stryker and Airbnb rows.*
- **🔴 THE MULTILINGUAL EARLY-CAREER BLIND SPOT IS NOT A WORKDAY-TENANT PROPERTY.** **Stryker 56, Marsh 17, Pfizer 14**, plus **two new families — a French `Alternant` at Pfizer and a `Stagiaire` at TRAVELERS, the first non-CXS instance on record.** `is_excluded()` sees **1 of Stryker's 56**, so these land in `judged_away`/`non_us_dropped` and appear in **neither `counts.excluded` nor the audit list.** All 87 non-US, cost zero again; the Stryker figure has been revised upward **three runs running** and **is not converging**. Measured only — **no exclusion implemented.** → *Stryker, Marsh, Pfizer and Travelers rows; `rules.md` §4.*

### 🔴 2026-09-04 r2 — the `would_be_tier()` fix SHIPPED, a req-ID retitle false positive found, and NOT a zero-drift fan-out

- **🟢 `jobscan_match.would_be_tier()` IS NOW IN `bin/` — USE IT FOR `excluded_audit`, NEVER `base_tier()`.** The r1 correction above diagnosed the defect but left the fix caller-side; it was still live in the checked-in module at the start of this run. Shipped once in the shared helper rather than six times in the groups — **the standing cure this file already prescribes for the data-science exclusion ("ship it ONCE in the shared helper… per-group re-derivation is the root cause") applied to its third instance.** Suite **278 → 294**; proven in both directions before being trusted (7 excluded titles returning `None` under `base_tier()` and `core`/`analyst` under the new helper). 🔵 **Immediate payout: the audit list went from empty-by-construction to 25 rows, 23 US-eligible** — Amex's 12-row `Campus … 2027` block, The Hartford's 7-row `Tech & Data Program Summer 2027` block, plus Sysco, Post Holdings, Stryker and a US Marsh `Summer Analyst 2027`. ⚠️ **An empty audit list is still legitimate** — G1's 13 excluded titles are all Data Science internships, which reach only borderline and correctly never enter a core/analyst audit. → *`rules.md` §4; `bin/jobscan_match.py`; `bin/test_jobscan.py`.*
