- **🔴🔴 `build_reqid_index()` KEEPS ONLY THE FIRST STORED KEY PER REQUISITION AND MANUFACTURES RETITLES.** It does `index.setdefault(rk, key)`, so a requisition with several stored keys — which this file calls normal ("Stryker holds three non-collapsing namespaces, Adobe three") — has its served title compared against **one arbitrary stored key**. Live: Ulta `490486`, stored as `Senior Data Engineer` (08-06) *and* as **`Sr Data Engineer (Remote)` (08-31)**; the index kept the 08-06 key, so the byte-identical 08-31 title was never consulted and the run reported a retitle for a posting tracked four days. 🔵 **Fails toward RE-REPORTING, not silent loss**, so nothing has ever been lost — but it recurs every run for any multi-key requisition. **Not applied in-run** per §3's "do not touch `jobscan_dedup` during a scan" — the row was suppressed by hand at compile and the module left alone. ✅ **RULED AND FIXED IMMEDIATELY AFTER THE RUN** (the operator: *"ok, one-line fix"*): `build_reqid_index()` now maps a requisition to the **list** of its stored keys, and the new **`reqid_verdict()`** returns `("suppress"|"retitle"|"new", stored_url)` so callers stop re-deriving the decision. ⚠️ **`seen_by_reqid()` survives but must NOT decide suppress-vs-retitle** — it returns an arbitrary key. **Measured on the live tracker: 13 multi-key requisitions, exactly 3 change verdict** (Progressive `18060418`, Ulta `490486`, Ulta `500906`, all `retitle → suppress`) — **the same three recorded as "carrying a changed title", each of which had BOTH titles persisted and so re-reported forever under the old code.** Pinned by an Ulta multi-key fixture with the stale key inserted first. 🔵 **The pattern to copy: find it in-run, report it in-run, change the module out-of-run.** → *`rules.md` §3 (ruled).*
- **🔴 A `/login` SUFFIX IS A NON-CANONICAL URL AND SPLITS THE DEDUP KEY.** Ulta and Post Holdings served `…/jobs/{req}/login`; `/jobs/490486/login` and `/jobs/490486` **normalise to different keys**, so persisting the former creates a second key for one posting and resurfaces it forever. 3 rows corrected at compile before any write. **Strip a trailing `/login` segment on both hosts.** → *Ulta and Post Holdings rows; `rules.md` §3.*
- **🔴 A GROUP RETURNING FEWER COMPANIES THAN ASSIGNED IS AN UNFETCHED BOARD, AND NO IDENTITY ASSERTION CAN SEE IT.** G1 returned **16 of 17** with every per-tier per-company identity holding — **Marriott, ~62% of its request volume, was simply absent**, and no other group carries it. Same failure class as AccuWeather. **Standing check: assert each group's returned company set against its assignment, not just its counts.** Re-fetched: 6,031 unique / 1,159 requests / 0 new. → *Dispatch groups section.*
- **🔴 CVS WAS UNDER-FETCHED BY A PAGINATION BOUND, CAUGHT BY THE GROUP ITSELF.** The `bi` and `report` keywords were each capped at **4,020** records against own-totals of **17,920** and **7,911**; re-fetched to completion and merged, recovering **6,772 additional unique records** (board **18,294**). **Fourth independent demonstration that a clean exit is not a completeness proof**, joining Sysco, Home Depot and Travelers. → *CVS row.*
- **⚠️ iHERB IS NOT BLOCKED AND THIS FILE SAYS IT IS.** The standing-split line annotated it `iHerb *(blocked)*` while `companies.md` recorded 5+ consecutive unblocked runs via the native `POST careers.iherb.com/api/Jobs/Search`. **Corrected in the split below.** Re-verified fresh this run: 56/56 clean. **A stale block annotation propagates into every dispatch brief written from this file.** → *Dispatch groups section; iHerb row.*
- **🔴 SHOPIFY RSC SCRAPE — A NAIVE BARE-PAIR REGEX INFLATES 110 REAL POSTINGS TO 539 PHANTOMS.** A fallback matching any `"X","UUID"` pair over the RSC payload picks up internal component references, so department/category strings (`Crafter Pipeline`) masquerade as titles. **Anchor on each `ashby_jid=` occurrence and read only the immediately-preceding window for the `"TITLE",("DATE",)?` shape ending at that URL — never a blind whole-payload scan.** → *Shopify row.*
- **🟢 API FIELD NAMES THAT SILENTLY RETURN ZERO WHEN WRONG:** **Travelers** CWS is `queryResult` (not `requisitionList`/`jobs`) — and never `seo_url`; **Home Depot** CWS is `queryResult`/`id` (not `jobs`/`jobId`); **Easy Dynamics** (Rippling) carries a full `locations[]` with per-entry `countryCode`, resolving location with no extra request. → *Travelers, Home Depot and Easy Dynamics rows.*
- **🟢 TWO HTML FIELD-ORDER CORRECTIONS:** **Under Armour**'s anchor attribute order is **`class` before `href`** — a regex assuming `href` first matches zero; **Ross Stores**' `jobsHtml` order is title → address → city/state → **anchor last**, not title → anchor → address as previously noted. → *Under Armour and Ross Stores rows.*
- **⚠️ MARRIOTT: `reporting` IS A KNOWN-BROAD KEYWORD, not a site change** — `totalJob` **7,419** against `business intelligence` 159, and it drove the pool ~3,600 → **6,031**. Same class as CVS `power bi` and Marsh `bi`. **New false friend: `Costumed Character Performer - Snowflake - Seasonal`** (a seasonal costume theme, not the data platform). → *Marriott row.*
- **✅ SAME-DAY RE-RUNS SHARE ONE `.bak-{date}` AND THAT IS TOO COARSE — FIXED 2026-09-06.** The r2 tracker write found `seen_jobs.json.bak-2026-09-04` already present from r1 and correctly kept it (the older copy is the safer recovery point), but there was then **no backup of the post-r1/pre-r2 state**. The recorded fix was `.bak-{date}` on r1 and `.bak-{date}-rN` on re-runs; **what shipped instead is a per-write timestamp**, `.bak-{YYYY-MM-DD}T{HHMMSS}`, with a `-N` suffix if two writes land in the same second. Same guarantee — every write gets its own recovery point — without the caller having to know its run number, which nothing in `save()`'s signature carried. 🔵 **Shipped with pruning to `jobscan_tracker.KEEP_BACKUPS` (3), because one-per-write on top of the old no-prune behaviour would have grown without bound.** Pruning runs **only after the post-write assertions pass**. → *Non-negotiables, tracker writes.*
- **⚠️ HILTON'S FP&A NOISE ANCHOR RECURRED A 6TH CONSECUTIVE RUN** — `Manager Global Financial Planning & Analysis` (req 207648), re-judged away every time. **Worth a standing `companies.md` note so it stops costing judgement time.** **Wendy's** needed `specialist` and `coordinator` keywords not in its standing required-token set (`manager` and `e` both hit the page-1 cap of 10). → *Hilton and Wendy's rows.*
- **🔴🔴 THE `companies.md` UPDATER CORRUPTED EIGHT ROSTER ROWS ON r1 BY SPLITTING ON A BARE `|` — REPAIRED, AND THE PARSER RULE IS NOW STANDING.** Roster cells are full of inline regex alternations written with **escaped** pipes (`analy(t\|s)?ic`, `information architect\|business information\|informatics`). **An updater that splits a row on a bare `|` splits at those too**, invents a cell boundary mid-regex, and rejoining with space padding breaks the escape into `\ | ` — leaving **5 content cells in a 4-column table**. Traced through the backups: `bak-2026-09-03` holds `analy(t \|s)?ic` intact; `bak-2026-09-04` (r1's write) holds `analy(t \ | ✅ **2026-09-03 r2: 5474.**…`, i.e. r1 injected its scan sentence *inside the regex* and displaced the trailing fragment. Affected: **Humana, United Health, Hinge Health, Hilton, Pfizer, Quest Diagnostics, Home Depot, Marsh** — 8 of the roster, several of them the highest-traffic boards.
  - **🔴 RULE: split roster rows on `(?<!\\)\|` — unescaped pipes ONLY — and never pad a cell you did not author.** Write the pattern to a file with `Write` and execute it; never inline (this harness mangles backslashes).
  - **Repaired 2026-09-04 r2** by rejoining each split cell with an escaped pipe: +8 bytes total, line count unchanged, and **stripping all escapes reproduces the pre-repair file byte-for-byte**, so nothing was rewritten or lost. Backup at `companies.md.bak-2026-09-04-r2-prepipefix`. ⚠️ **Home Depot's `s)?ic` fragment was already displaced by r1 and is not recoverable from the current file** — restore it from `bak-2026-09-03` if that row's guidance is ever needed verbatim.
  - 🔵 **This is the same family as the `.bak-*-prepipefix` and `*-tablefix` copies already sitting in `resources/`** — the defect that once stopped 70 of 97 rows rendering. It has now recurred at least three times, each time from a naive pipe split, which is why the parser rule is recorded here rather than in a script comment.
- **⚠️ NOT A ZERO-DRIFT FAN-OUT — two groups needed a correction round, and the final clean files must not be recorded as if they arrived that way.** **G2** shipped three defects at once: `found` computed **after** removing ambiguous records, so **10 AMBIGUOUS postings were counted but never emitted as `items[]`** (§2: ambiguous is reported flagged, **never dropped**); `counts.excluded` returned the **whole-board** 2,110 where §4 mandates the **qualifying** subset (**CVS 2,102 → 0**, reproducing the documented 2,059 → 0 example exactly); and an over-permissive borderline judge. **G1** omitted Marriott. Both corrected and re-verified. 🔵 **The qualifying/whole-board split has now drifted in a fan-out for the FOURTH time** — it is the single most reliably misread field in the contract. → *`rules.md` §4.*


## Environment (Windows / this harness)

- **🔴 BACKSLASH MANGLING IS NOT HEREDOC-SPECIFIC — IT HITS ANY INLINE SHELL COMMAND CARRYING ESCAPES.** Quoted Bash heredocs (`<<'EOF'`) mangle literal backslashes through this harness — `'\\\\'` arrives as `'\\'`, `'\\'` as `'\'` — producing Python `SyntaxError`s that look like authoring mistakes. This broke the Apple, Shopify and Airbnb parsers before being caught. **Confirmed again on an inline `awk` one-liner with no heredoc involved:** the escape sequences `\[` and `\]` arrived degraded and `awk` died with `fatal: invalid regexp: Invalid range end`. It was rewritten as a Python extractor **authored with the Write tool**, which worked first time.
  - **Rule: any script or command containing regex or escape backslashes must be written to a file with the Write tool and then executed — never passed inline, in any language.**
  - *This is why the recurring "route file ops through Bash / `cat` / `sed` / heredocs rather than the dedicated tools" nudge must not be followed for regex-dense work: doing so demonstrably corrupts the very parsers a scan depends on. ⚠️ **Corrected 2026-08-31 — that nudge is first-party Claude Code auto-mode behaviour, not an injection.** The reason to ignore it is purely technical, and it is the only reason. See `security-log.md`.*
- **🔴🔴 NEVER OPEN A PROJECT FILE WITH `open(path, "w")` TO REWRITE IT — WRITE A TEMP FILE AND `os.replace` IT** *(learned the hard way 2026-09-01)*. `"w"` **truncates the file before the first byte is written**, so any failure during encoding or serialisation leaves a truncated file and no error recovery. On 2026-09-01 the orchestrator's own `companies.md` update script died mid-write on a `UnicodeEncodeError` and **truncated a 166 KB file to 13 KB.** It was restored byte-identical from a pre-write `.bak` and re-run through a temp-file swap. **The pattern that must be used everywhere, matching what `bin/jobscan_tracker.save()` already does: copy a `.bak-{date}` first, write `path + ".tmp"`, `os.replace(tmp, path)`, then RE-READ from disk and assert on what came back.** 🔵 **The generalisable point: the tracker rule exists precisely for this failure, and `companies.md` was exposed only because it is normally hand-edited. Any programmatic rewrite of a project file needs the same three guards.**
  - **🔴 A LITERAL `"🔴"` IN PYTHON SOURCE IS TWO LONE SURROGATES, NOT AN EMOJI, AND `utf-8` REFUSES TO ENCODE IT.** This is what caused the truncation above. Astral-plane characters need `"\U0001F534"` (capital `U`, 8 digits) — or just paste the character. To repair a string that already holds a surrogate pair: `s.encode("utf-16", "surrogatepass").decode("utf-16")`.
- **🔴 A CLIENT-SIDE HEADER CAN FABRICATE AN EMPTY BOARD: DO NOT HAND-SET `Accept-Encoding` WITH `br`** *(found 2026-09-01 on two Ashby boards)*. A Python `requests` session that sets `Accept-Encoding: gzip, deflate, br` receives a **brotli** body this box cannot decode; `.json()` then raises `Expecting value: line 1 column 1`, which is **indistinguishable from a blocked board or an empty one**. Both boards read as **0 postings** until the header was dropped. `curl --compressed` was unaffected throughout. **Let `requests` negotiate its own encoding, and treat a JSON-decode failure as a FETCH failure to retry with a different client — never as a zero.** Same "0 found vs the fetch failed" conflation `rules.md` §6 forbids, arriving from our own side of the wire.
- **⚠️ PIPING A PYTHON RUN INTO `tail`/`head` MASKS THE EXIT CODE** *(found 2026-08-30)*. A run that died on a traceback reported **`[exited with code 0]`**, because the pipeline's status is the last command's. **Redirect to a file and read it, or check `PIPESTATUS`** — otherwise a crashed parser is indistinguishable from a clean empty board, which is the "0 found vs the fetch failed" conflation `rules.md` §6 forbids.
- **🔴🔴 `jobscan_location.is_us()` RETURNS `True` / `False` / `None` — NOT THE STRINGS `US` / `non-US` / `AMBIGUOUS`. USE `verdict_label()` TO MAP IT.** *(Found 2026-09-03, independently, by TWO callers on the same run.)* The module's docstring and every prose description of it — including the dispatch brief's — say "returns US / non-US / AMBIGUOUS", which reads as a string return. It is not.
  - **`if is_us(loc) == "non-US"` matches nothing, so the non-US exclusion silently becomes a NO-OP**, and `ambiguous_flagged` is pinned at 0 board-wide. It fails toward **FALSE POSITIVES** in the one exclusion that runs before the bar.
  - 🔴 **There is almost no symptom.** The per-tier identity still balances, dedup still fires, and every count still sums — the only visible trace is a board-wide `ambiguous_flagged == 0` with verdicts printing as `True`/`False`. G2 caught it on its own first pass, where **79 non-US rows (22 core, 6 analyst, 17 lead_manager, 34 borderline — overwhelmingly UHG India/Ireland/Philippines) would have been reported.** The orchestrator's Indeed pipeline carried the identical bug the same run.
  - **Correct usage:** `verdict = jobscan_location.verdict_label(jobscan_location.is_us(loc, country_code=cc))`.
  - 🔵 **Two independent callers reaching the same wrong reading of the same signature is the fan-out drift pattern arriving in a HELPER'S RETURN TYPE rather than in a contract field.** A string-returning wrapper in `bin/` would remove the trap outright; not added in-run.
- **🔴 `subprocess.run(..., text=True)` DECODES STDOUT AS cp1252 ON WINDOWS AND MANGLES UTF-8 TITLES** *(found 2026-09-03 at Progressive)*. `Adjuster – Auto` came back as `Adjuster â€“ Auto`. **Capture bytes and decode `utf-8` explicitly** (`subprocess.run(..., capture_output=True)` then `.stdout.decode("utf-8")`). Same family as the `PYTHONIOENCODING` rule above, on the input side of the pipe rather than the output side. Cosmetic only when no affected posting is reported — silent corruption of a stored title when one is.
- **No `jq` and no `node` on this box.** Working combination: `curl` for fetching + PowerShell `ConvertFrom-Json` **or** Python via the project venv `.venv\Scripts\python.exe` for parsing. **Never bare `python`/`python3`, and never bare `py`** — a Store shim on `PATH` triggers a Windows Store popup. 🔵 **Superseded 2026-09-06: this bullet used to pin `py -3.11` and call the pin load-bearing.** It never was — the interpreter version is not load-bearing (the 294 cases pass on 3.11 and 3.14 alike); what was load-bearing is having `requests` on whatever interpreter runs, which is now the venv's job. Create it with `py -3.11 -m venv .venv` + `pip install -r requirements.txt` if it is missing. **Client choice per site is unaffected** — `requests` is still hard-403'd where `curl` is not.
- **Set `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`.** Python's import machinery writes a **shared** `__pycache__/` in the scratchpad root, which collided across three dispatch groups on 2026-08-21 — exactly what the per-group prefix rule exists to prevent.
- **Set `PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8`**, or printing job titles containing en-dashes raises `UnicodeEncodeError` on cp1252 and truncates output mid-run.
- **`grep -o` on a single-line multi-hundred-KB sitemap HANGS** (killed a 2-minute command on UHG's 351KB sitemap). Use Python `re.findall` or PowerShell instead.
- **Python `urllib.request` is a valid third client for Workday CXS POST** (the file's "needs curl/PowerShell" note is too narrow) — but it still gets **HTTP 404 from `careers.airbnb.com`** even with a browser UA, where `curl` works. Client choice matters per-site.

---

## Per-ATS default recipes

### Greenhouse
- API: `https://boards-api.greenhouse.io/v1/boards/{token}/jobs?content=true`
- No auth, no pagination needed (single JSON array). Straightforward.
- **🔴🔴 THE GARBAGE CONTROL ON `/jobs` CANNOT DISCRIMINATE A DEAD TOKEN — CORRECTED 2026-08-29.** `boards-api.greenhouse.io/v1/boards/zzqqxxnotreal999/jobs` returns **HTTP 404 / 38 B / `{"status":404,"error":"Job not found"}`** — and that is **byte-identical to what a real-but-dead board's token returns**. The standing note "clean 404 / 38 B control, so a zero is trustworthy" (it appears on the InStride and Webflow rows) is therefore **not a discriminator on this endpoint**; it proves only that the endpoint is up. A dead token read through it is indistinguishable from a garbage token, and both are indistinguishable from an empty board — the "0 postings found vs the fetch failed" conflation `rules.md` §6 forbids.
  - **✅ WORKING DISCRIMINATOR: `GET https://boards-api.greenhouse.io/v1/boards/{token}` — NO `/jobs` SUFFIX.** A dead/garbage token returns **44 B `Job board not found`**; a live token returns the board object. **Run this, not `/jobs`, whenever a Greenhouse board reports 0 or collapses.**
    - 🟢 **IT PROVED ITSELF TWICE ON 2026-08-30 AND IS NOW STANDING METHOD, NOT A NEW IDEA.** It resolved **Alt** (Greenhouse token confirmed dead → the board was found on **Ashby**) and **iHerb** (token proven dead for the first time on record, after weeks of an ambiguous 404). **Two dead-token diagnoses in one run, on a check that costs one request.** Run it on every Greenhouse board that reports 0.
- **🔴🔴 `offices[]` IS A COMPANY ATTRIBUTE, NOT THE POSTING'S LOCATION — USING IT AS A LOCATION SLOT SCORES FOREIGN POSTINGS AS US** *(found 2026-09-01 at Fanatics Betting)*. `Data Scientist I` (4356668009) is **Dublin, Ireland**, while its `offices` reads `["Denver, Colorado, United States"]` — a caller reading the offices array resolved it **US** and would have reported a non-US posting. **`location.name` is primary; consult `offices[]` only when the posting carries no location of its own**, and treat a location that came from `offices[]` as AMBIGUOUS rather than authoritative. ⚠️ Note this is a *different* failure from a missing location: the offices value is well-formed and plausible, so nothing downstream looks wrong.
  - **🔴 A GREENHOUSE BOARD SERVING THE BARE STRING `Remote` IS PERMANENTLY UN-RESOLVABLE, AND TWO ROSTER BOARDS DO IT ALMOST UNIVERSALLY** *(quantified 2026-09-03)*. **Jellyvision 7/7 and Digible 7/8** serve `Remote` with no other location signal. With no structured country on this ATS and `offices[]` barred as a location slot, **every future data posting at either employer will report AMBIGUOUS-flagged rather than US, indefinitely.** There is no fix available — report them flagged per `rules.md` §2. **Structurally identical to the all-ambiguous Shopify board that prompted the 2026-09-02 §2 ruling**, and worth reading alongside it: a board that is 100% ambiguous makes the flag channel carry no information for that employer. Cost was zero on 2026-09-03 only because both boards' candidates were already tracked.
  - ⚠️ **Greenhouse also carries NO structured country field at all**, which is what makes the region-scoped strings at Alpaca (`Remote - EMEA`, `APAC`, `LATAM`, `Middle East`, `Americas`, and — added 2026-09-01 — `Europe - Remote`, `North America and Europe`, `Remote - Global Anywhere`) resolve AMBIGUOUS. There is no fix available on this ATS; report them flagged.
- **🔴 Read `absolute_url` from the API; NEVER synthesize the board URL.** Greenhouse tenants point `absolute_url` at their own careers domain, and there are **five** distinct stored URL shapes across the boards in this project — a synthesized `job-boards.greenhouse.io/{token}/jobs/{id}` would have false-positived Gemini, Cribl and Pantheon *entirely*:
  1. path form — `job-boards.greenhouse.io/{token}/jobs/{id}`
  2. embed form — `boards.greenhouse.io/embed/job_app?for={co}&token={id}&gh_jid={id}` (Gemini)
  3. `cribl.io/job-detail/?gh_jid={id}`
  4. `pantheon.io/about/careers/detail?gh_jid={id}`
  5. **`coalitioninc.com/job-posting?gh_jid={id}`** (Coalition — added 2026-08-24)
  6. **`{token-domain}/jobs/{id}?gh_jid={id}` — a redundant `gh_jid` on the SAME path the tracker stores bare** (Scopely — added 2026-08-26 r2). This one is nastier than the others because the path matches and only the query string differs.
  - The **`gh_jid`-extraction dedup rule is load-bearing on all of them.** Prize Picks' `http://` scheme comes straight from the ATS's own `absolute_url` — it is not a historical artifact and will keep reproducing.
  - **🔴 ONE NAMESPACE FOR ALL EXTRACTED IDs — path-segment IDs and `gh_jid` query IDs must share it.** With separate namespaces a stored path form never matches a served query form. **Load-bearing at three companies now**: Pantheon (5 of 6 suppressions non-exact, incl. both `Senior Analytics Engineer` reqs, held for a 3rd run 2026-08-27), **Scopely (10 of 11 suppressions; all ten would have been false positives)**, and Cribl.
- **Try this even if a company's public careers page shows no Greenhouse markers at all.** Tailscale's careers page is a fully custom Next.js/Sanity-CMS site with zero embedded job data and no `greenhouse`/`boards-api` strings anywhere in the HTML or its `_next/data` payload — yet the plain guessed token (company-name-as-slug) still resolved on the standard boards-api endpoint. Worth a quick guess-the-token check before concluding a custom-looking site needs real reverse-engineering. Also worked this way for Alpaca (token found via its `hiring-app.js` bundle referencing `boards.greenhouse.io/alpaca` directly) and for a Cloudflare-challenge-blocked public page (Gopuff's main site 403s, but that's irrelevant once you're hitting the ATS API directly on Lever — see Lever section).
- Some sites embed a Greenhouse *embed widget* instead of linking the boards-api site directly — look for `greenhouse.io/embed/job_board/js?for={token}` in the HTML (Celigo) or plain `job-boards.greenhouse.io/{token}/jobs/{id}` links embedded straight into the page (Human Interest) as alternate ways to recover the token.
- Confirmed via token-guessing/embed-widget recovery: Tailscale (`tailscale`), Alpaca (`alpaca`), Celigo (`celigo`), Human Interest (`humaninterest`), **iHerb (`iherb` — 2026-08-05 r2, see below)**, Cribl (`cribl`).
- **Token-guessing gotcha (2026-08-06): an HTTP 200 alone doesn't confirm the right ATS.** When guessing a company-name token, try it against Greenhouse, Lever, AND Ashby — more than one can return 200 even when only one is real. Confirmed with Cribl: `api.ashbyhq.com/posting-api/job-board/cribl` returns HTTP 200 but a near-empty body (0 postings, stale/unused board), while `boards-api.greenhouse.io/v1/boards/cribl/jobs` is the real one (~65-69 postings). **The decoy is still live and the Greenhouse token is still real — re-confirmed on every scan since.** Always check the actual job count/content, not just the status code, before concluding which ATS is correct.
- **iHerb cracked 2026-08-05 r2 (previously blocked).** `careers.iherb.com` is a genuinely blocked front end — Remix/React SPA, `__remixContext` loaderData has only page meta, no static API found in 3 earlier passes (common `/api/jobs` guesses, SmartRecruiters token guess, JS bundle inspection). What finally worked: plain token-guessing against the Greenhouse boards-api directly — `https://boards-api.greenhouse.io/v1/boards/iherb/jobs` resolves HTTP 200 with real content (`"company_name":"iHerb"`), completely independent of the broken front end. Same category of trick as Tailscale/Alpaca/Celigo — **always try guessing the plain Greenhouse token before spending more effort reverse-engineering a "blocked" custom front end.**
- **iHerb regressed 2026-08-06 (WORKING → BLOCKED).** The very same token/endpoint that worked cleanly the day before (`https://boards-api.greenhouse.io/v1/boards/iherb/jobs`, plus `?content=true`, plus a direct job-detail lookup on the known match ID 7578260003) now returns HTTP 404 `{"status":404,"error":"Job board not found"}` on every variant, byte-identical across 2 retries with different User-Agent headers. This means even a confirmed-working Greenhouse token guess isn't permanently stable — the token could have been deactivated, the board reorganized, or this could be transient. **Always retry iHerb's token fresh each scan rather than assuming yesterday's success holds.** Previously-tracked match (Sr. Data Engineer I, R106560) status is unconfirmed as a result, not closed — a source failure isn't a listing-disappearance signal.

### Ashby
- API: `https://api.ashbyhq.com/posting-api/job-board/{token}`
- No auth, single JSON response.
- **🟢🔴 ASHBY CARRIES A FIRST-CLASS STRUCTURED COUNTRY AT `address.postalAddress.addressCountry` — READ IT, NOT THE DISPLAY STRING** *(found 2026-09-02 r3 at Alt; previously undocumented here)*. This is the same class of rule as Workday CXS's `country.descriptor`: **an authoritative country field wins over any text read of a location string.**
  - **It is load-bearing, not cosmetic.** At Alt, `Data Engineer`'s display `location` is the bare string `Remote` — which resolves **AMBIGUOUS** — while `addressCountry` reads `United States`. A caller using only the display string flags a US posting as ambiguous every run.
  - ⚠️ **Assert its presence rather than assuming it**, the same way Hinge Health's `addressCountry` is 63/64 for a third run on a *different* ATS. A missing structured field must fall back to the display string, not silently resolve to nothing.
- **🔴 Do not hand-set `Accept-Encoding` with `br` on a Python `requests` session for these boards** — this box cannot decode brotli, `.json()` then raises `Expecting value: line 1 column 1`, and the failure is **indistinguishable from a blocked or empty board**. It read two Ashby boards as 0 postings on 2026-09-01. Let `requests` negotiate its own encoding; treat a JSON-decode failure as a FETCH failure to retry with a different client, never as a zero. *(Clean on 2026-09-02 r2 and r3 with encoding left to the client.)*

### Breezy HR

*Added 2026-08-26 with Collectiv, the first Breezy board on the roster.*

- **Endpoint: `GET https://{company}.breezy.hr/json`** — one call, no auth, no pagination, no page-size cap. `Content-Type: application/json`.
- 🔴 **The response is a TOP-LEVEL JSON ARRAY, not an object wrapper.** `d["positions"]` / `d["jobs"]` both fail — iterate the array directly. (Collectiv: 5 records, 10.9 KB.)
- **Per-record fields:** `id` (12-hex), `friendly_id` (`{id}-{slug}`), `name` (the title), `url` (canonical, absolute), `published_date` (ISO 8601), `type.name` (`Full-Time`…), `department`, `salary`, `location`, `locations`.
- 🟢 **Location is fully structured — no text parsing, and the `, XX` trap is defused by construction:** `location.country` is `{"name": "United States", "id": "US"}`, with `location.state` (`{"id": "IL", "name": "Illinois"}`), `location.city` (a plain string), `location.is_remote` (bool) and `location.remote_details.label`. **Read `location.country.id`/`.name` for the non-US decision.**
  - ⚠️ **`state` and `country` are OBJECTS while `city` is a STRING.** A naive `", ".join([city, state, country])` raises `TypeError: sequence item 1: expected str instance, dict found`. Normalise with a `.get("name")` helper. Hit live on the first parse.
  - `location.name` is a pre-joined `"Chicago, IL"` convenience string, and `location.streetAddress` carries a full Google-components block — neither is needed once you read `country`.
- **Dedup: key on the 12-hex `id` leading the `/p/{id}-{slug}` path**, or equivalently the record's `id` field. **The slug is derived from the title, so it drifts whenever a posting is retitled** — the standing slug-drift trap. The id is stable and is what the tracker stores.
  - The bare-path-segment matcher handles `/p/{id}-{slug}` **only if the whole-segment letter budget is NOT applied to it**, since the slug carries many letters. **Extract the leading `^[0-9a-f]{12}` explicitly** rather than relying on the generic matcher.
- 🟢 **Garbage control is unambiguous: a nonexistent subdomain returns a clean HTTP 404** (`https://zzqqxxnotreal999.breezy.hr/json` → 404, 3,265 B). Unlike iHerb's SmartRecruiters decoy there is no 200-with-empty-body shape here, so a zero is trustworthy when the control 404s.
- The HTML page at `https://{company}.breezy.hr/` renders server-side too (13.9 KB for a 5-posting board), but the JSON is strictly better — use it.

### Lever
- API: `https://api.lever.co/v0/postings/{company}?mode=json`
- No auth, no pagination needed (single JSON array). Canonical job URL: `https://jobs.lever.co/{company}/{id}`.
- Confirmed working for: Versapay (token `versapay`), Gopuff (token `gopuff` — the public `gopuff.com/go/careers` page itself is Cloudflare-challenge-blocked (`Just a moment...`), but the Lever API token still resolves fine directly; worth guessing/trying the API even when the front-end site is bot-walled).

### SmartRecruiters
- API: `https://api.smartrecruiters.com/v1/companies/{companyId}/postings`
- **Paginated** — must page through `offset`/`limit` until an empty page. NBC Universal runs ~390-400 postings (`totalFound` 394); sampling page 1 only will badly undercount.
- **✅ The garbage control is the working discipline and it fires correctly** — re-confirmed on Logic20/20, where `zzqqxxnotreal999` → `totalFound: 0` against the real token's 6. **Run it alongside every SmartRecruiters query**; the HTTP 200 carries no signal on its own (see the trap in "Global / common rules").

### Workday (CXS API)
- **🔴🔴 THE ENUMERATION KEY IS EVERYTHING AFTER THE LAST UNDERSCORE, REVISION SUFFIX INCLUDED — DO NOT STRIP REVISIONS WHILE PAGINATING** *(found 2026-09-01 at J&J, where it under-fetched the board by 52% while reporting a clean stop)*. A revision-stripping pattern like `_([A-Za-z0-9][\w.\-]*?)(?:-(\d+))?$` is unsafe as a **pagination** key because **some tenants' req IDs contain an internal hyphen**: J&J's are `_R-093799`, so the non-greedy group takes `R` and the optional revision group eats `-093799`, **collapsing every non-revisioned posting onto the single key `R`.** The collisions then read as "this page contained no new run-local IDs", so the walk terminates early — **at 758 of 1,579, with zero errors, and a second pass reproduces the same 758.**
  - 🔵 **Why this class is so dangerous: it is invisible except against the page-1 total.** The five G1 tenants whose req IDs carry no internal hyphen were completely unaffected, so the bug looked tenant-specific rather than systemic. **Always compare `unique` against the offset=0 `total`** — that comparison is the only thing that catches it.
  - 🔴 **Revision folding belongs in DEDUP, not in enumeration.** `normalize_url` already folds Workday `-N` revision suffixes, and there a collision is harmless (two URLs for one posting is the intended outcome). In enumeration a collision can only **lose records**. Same family as the Marsh `R_`-prefix death: a req-ID parse that looks tidier and silently drops postings.
- **🔴 `bulletFields` positional indexing is UNSAFE across tenants — parse the req ID out of `externalPath` instead.** The array shape differs per tenant and there is no way to tell from the response which you're looking at: The Hartford is 2-element `[reqId, "0"/"1"]` (so `bulletFields[-1]` collapses every posting onto the keys `"0"` and `"1"`), Nationwide is 3-element `[location, reqId, title]`, Zillow puts the ID at `[0]`. This is the standing rule the corrections sections refer to.
  - *Exception worth knowing:* Zillow's `bulletFields[0]` (e.g. `P751116`) is its true stable ID, and its URL slug carries a trailing `-N` disambiguator that is **not** part of the ID. Where `externalPath` and `bulletFields` disagree, prefer `externalPath` and reconcile.
- **🔵 FULL EMPTY-`searchText` PAGINATION IS PRIMARY wherever the board is small enough — prefer it over any keyword sweep or facet.** It removes miss-risk entirely rather than trading against it. **Standing full-pagination-primary tenants:** The Hartford, Nationwide, ICF, Pfizer, Stryker (promoted 2026-08-21), **Wintrust, Alteryx, Empower, Ryze** (promoted 2026-08-24), **Marsh, Capital One** (promoted 2026-08-24 r2), plus Adobe, Humana, Centene, Allstate, Cigna, Zillow, eBay, Comcast. **Accuracy measured across the same nine tenants on TWO CONSECUTIVE RUNS: unique records equalled the page-1 `total` EXACTLY on every one, both times** — Stryker 1,165, Pfizer 567, ICF 379, Hartford 202, Nationwide 170, Wintrust 163, Empower 146, Alteryx 73, Ryze 11 — each terminating on a genuinely empty page, and with **zero null records anywhere across all nine**. Pools when promoted off keyword sweeps: Hartford 118→205, Nationwide 106→186, Pfizer 218→583. **Stryker's real board is ~1,161-1,165, not the 266 the Paradox `totalJob` field reports** — that field reflects a filtered view, not the company board.
  - **🔴 Cost note — RUN THE TENANTS CONCURRENTLY, BUT SPLIT STRYKER OUT.** *(Corrected 2026-08-30 — the previous text said the opposite and is deleted.)* The other eight G5 tenants finish concurrently in **≤13.2 s**. **🔴 STRYKER RATE-LIMITS UNDER 9-WAY CONCURRENCY: HTTP 429, all 5 in-loop retries exhausted, and the WHOLE TENANT FAILED.** That is the in-loop-retry rule working as designed — it failed **loudly**, which is the only reason it was caught. ✅ **Working method: run Stryker SEQUENTIALLY AND ALONE, 0.35 s inter-page gap, exponential backoff 3 → 60 s → 1,202/1,202 in one pass, 61 pages, 68.8 s.**
    - **🔴 Two recorded figures are WRONG and are corrected: "under concurrency this tenant is effectively free — do not split it out" (it is not, at 9-way), and "nine CXS tenants concurrent in 14.4 s" (did NOT reproduce — measured 60.3 s, and that run lost Stryker entirely).** Do not restore either. **Keep full pagination everywhere; the fix is Stryker's isolation, not scope reduction.**
    - ✅ **Sequential-and-alone RECONFIRMED 2026-08-31:** zero 429s and zero retries across 186 pages / 3 passes at a 0.35 s gap, while the other eight tenants finished concurrently in **31.7 s**. The isolation rule is holding.
  - **🔴🔴 STRYKER NEEDS THE REPEAT-PASS METHOD, AND THE SINGLE-PASS EXACT RESULT WAS A LUCKY DRAW** *(2026-08-31)*. Pass 1 returned **1,206 against a page-1 `total` of 1,210** and **terminated on run-local wraparound four records short — silently, reporting a clean stop.** Pass 2 added **exactly those 4** (1,210, zero lost); pass 3 added 0. **So Stryker joins Progressive, Ross, Airbnb and Under Armour as boards that re-order between requests, and the previous run's 1,202/1,202 single-pass exact was NOT a property.** ⚠️ **The shortfall was visible ONLY because unique was compared against the page-1 `total`** — the zero-new-ID stop rule on its own reported a confident wrong answer. Stop condition is **"repeat until a pass adds nothing"**, never a fixed pass count.
- **🔴 `jobPostingInfo.country.descriptor` IS THE FULL NAME `"United States of America"`, NOT AN ISO CODE — and this ate `jobscan_location` until 2026-08-31.** Any country matcher testing for `US`/`USA`/`United States` **exactly** scores every US CXS posting as non-US and drops it with no trace. `bin/jobscan_location.py` now compares on letters only and handles it; **do not hand-roll a country test here.** ⚠️ The field is also **not always present** — it is tenant-dependent (present 38/38 at G5's tenants and 12/12 at Kohl's on 2026-08-31; absent on some Adobe/Zillow/Marsh records on 2026-08-30) — so keep the presence assertion rather than assuming it.
  - **🟢 SCOPE THE DETAIL RESOLVER TO POST-BAR CANDIDATES, AND USE IT ON THE SMALL TENANTS TOO, NOT JUST THE BIG ONES** *(extended 2026-08-31)*. Location affects neither the enumeration net nor the qualifying bar, so resolving only reportable records is not a narrowing of the sweep: **38 detail requests for 2,875 enumerated CXS records at G5, 30 across Humana + CVS, 12 at Kohl's.** 🔴 **Kohl's needs this as standing method — its `locationsText` is a FACILITY NAME for 97% of the board** (`Kohl's Corporate Offices (0900)`, store names; **2,901 of 2,981 records would read AMBIGUOUS**, which per `rules.md` §2 disables the ambiguous channel entirely). Scoped to the 12 post-bar candidates, **12/12 returned a country**. Same treatment confirmed on Dick's (`Customer Support Center`) and PSA.
- **Recovering an undocumented facet ID:** request facets with an empty search, then filter the `jobFamilyGroup` values for descriptors containing "data" / "analytic". This is how CVS's ID was recovered and is the general method for any tenant.
- **Never trust `total:0` as a pagination-end signal on any tenant.** Ignore `total` entirely; stop when a page contributes zero new req IDs. Verified unmodified across 25+ tenants across two full runs with zero hangs. **This is the only CXS stop rule — no per-tenant caveats survive.**
  - **🔴 Capital One is a tenant where a `total:0` stop or a fixed page cap bites SILENTLY — do not let either back in.** Paginating to exhaustion there reaches **95 pages**, stopping on a genuinely zero-new-ID page with unique == page-1 `total` == **1,862** exactly; a run that reported roughly half that (968) is the signature of the Allstate `total:0` trap or a page cap, not a site change. **A board that halves or doubles between runs on this tenant is a helper bug until proven otherwise.** Wintrust, Alteryx and Empower have terminated on a genuinely empty page *and* wrapped around on different runs, so neither behaviour is a reliable per-tenant property; the stop rule is safe either way. Teladoc's documented duplicate-ID wraparound is handled correctly with no hang.
- **🔴 THE STOP RULE IS ONLY SAFE IF EACH KEYWORD RUN KEEPS ITS OWN SEEN SET.** *(Found 2026-08-21 r2; re-confirmed working 2026-08-24.)* Applied against a **globally** shared pool, "stop on a page contributing zero new req IDs" silently truncated Marsh by 264 records: the `analytics` keyword (`total`=284) terminated after page 1 because all 20 of its page-1 records had already been pulled by the earlier `data` run, so the page contributed zero *globally* new IDs and the loop stopped — with no error anywhere. **Fix: track newness per-run (a run-local seen set), not against the global pool. Stop on run-local wraparound, on `len(local) >= total`, or on a genuinely empty page.** Re-running that way took Marsh 1,362 → 1,604 unique. **Any multi-keyword CXS sweep is exposed.** Fails toward false negatives, exactly as `rules.md` §7 warns. ✅ Re-verified 2026-08-24 on Marsh, Hilton, Home Depot and both Paradox sites — every keyword terminated on run-local wraparound or `len(local) >= total`, and no keyword truncated at page 1 despite heavy pool overlap (Marsh `analytics` reached its full 281, `analyst` its full 997).
- **🔴 BUILD RETRY-UNTIL-CLEAN INTO THE CXS HELPER — a mid-run pagination abort must be retried, never accepted.** CVS's Tech facet aborted at `offset=140` with `WinError 10060` on attempt 1 (140 of 193 records); a plain retry completed 193/193. Dick's threw one transient **HTTP 503** mid-sweep, clean on retry. Both surfaced as exceptions only because the pagination loop propagates errors — **a stop rule that swallows exceptions would have reported a short board as complete.** (The CVS abort did not reproduce on 2026-08-24; the retry was built in and never fired. Keep it.)
- **🔴 Workday CXS can serve a record with `externalPath`, `title` AND `locationsText` all `null` — and it is NOT always transient.** Two distinct behaviours, do not assume the benign one:
  - *Transient* — Nationwide served req 099717 that way once and it came back complete on immediate retry. **The Hartford serves the same transient shape** and it crashed dedup keying with `TypeError: NoneType + str`.
  - *Was called "persistent" on eBay — SOFTENED, it CLEARS.* eBay served two such records (`R0072855`, tracked; `R0069441`, untracked) on every attempt within one run — reproducing on a full re-paginate, on a targeted `searchText=R0069441` (`total:1`, still null), with the CXS detail endpoint returning `404 "not found: Job_Posting_Anchor_ID=R0069441"` and `jobs.ebayinc.com/us/en/search-results?keywords=R0069441` returning `"jobs":[]`. **It was persistent on 2026-08-24 r1 and had cleared by r2** — 466 unique = page-1 `total` exactly, fully monotonic, zero null-title/null-`externalPath` records, and **both of those req IDs are absent from the feed entirely.** So "persistent" describes a run, not a tenant. **Keep the `bulletFields[0]` fallback and the null-title logging regardless — they cost nothing and the shape recurs.** *(Nine CXS tenants also saw zero null records across a full run, which points at the Hartford/Nationwide transient variant as the general case.)*
  - **Guidance, on EVERY tenant — not just Nationwide: guard key construction against a null `externalPath`, read `bulletFields[0]` as a fallback req ID when it is null, and LOG null-title records explicitly.** A title-null record is silently dropped by the client-side title filter and vanishes without trace.
- **🟢 THE CXS DETAIL-ENDPOINT RESOLVER IS THE STANDARD WAY TO RESOLVE A LOCATION — it is not a Marsh special case, and it is not an edge-case tool.** `GET https://{tenant}.wd{N}.myworkdayjobs.com/wday/cxs/{tenant}/{site}{externalPath}` returns **`jobPostingInfo.country.descriptor`** (a country *name*) plus `.location` / `.additionalLocations`. It removes the `N Locations` trap and Marsh's `City - Building` trap in one step, and it beats a targeted `searchText={reqId}` POST because it returns the country directly.
  - **Confirmed at 7 tenants with zero failures:** Marsh, Allstate, Geico, Cigna, Capital One, J&J (205/205 on 2026-08-26 r2), and eBay (2026-08-27).
  - **🔴 Volume — this is why it is standard, not optional: 468 of Marsh's 1,967 records (23.8%) carry an `N Locations` `locationsText`** (measured 2026-08-27). At that rate a scan that drops unresolved records loses a quarter of the board.
  - **🟢 SCOPE THE RESOLVER TO POST-BAR CANDIDATES — THIS IS NOT A NARROWING OF THE SWEEP** *(established 2026-08-30)*. Location affects **neither the enumeration net nor the qualifying bar**, so resolving only the records that would actually be *reported* changes no decision — it is the one place where scoping is safe, and `rules.md`'s "criteria must never shrink the sweep" rule is not engaged. Measured: **Adobe needed 23 detail requests instead of the documented ~307** (`N Locations` on 41.5% of 739); Zillow **7**; eBay **14**; Marsh **217 of 1,927**. **Resolve after the bar, never before it.**
  - **🔴 BUT `jobPostingInfo.country.descriptor` IS NOT ALWAYS PRESENT — do not document it as a complete country oracle** *(found 2026-08-30)*. It was **missing on 8 Adobe/Zillow candidates**, all Bangalore/Bengaluru, where the decision then **silently falls back to a city-name verdict** — the exact shape the Ross `Dublin, CA` trap warns about. **Assert the field's presence and flag the record AMBIGUOUS when it is absent**, rather than letting the text heuristic answer unannounced. Marsh reproduced this at 212/217.
- **🔴🔴 CXS DETAIL-URL TRAP — build the path by splitting the job URL on `.myworkdayjobs.com/{site}`, NEVER on the bare site name.** J&J's site is `jj` and its host is `jj.wd5.myworkdayjobs.com`, so a bare split matches the **hostname**, yields a malformed path, and returns **HTTP 406 on 38/38 records** — which reads exactly like a blocked or rate-limited tenant rather than a bug in your own URL construction.
  - **Any tenant whose site string appears in its host is exposed, and that is common, not rare.** Measured 2026-08-27: **two of G3's three tenants** are exposed — Adobe (`adobe` in `adobe.wd5…`) and Zillow (`zillow` in `zillow.wd5…`). eBay is not, because its site slug is `apply`. **Assume exposure and split on the full `.myworkdayjobs.com/{site}` literal every time.**
- **✅ Filter and classify on the API `title` field, NEVER the URL slug — on every tenant, not just Adobe.** Confirmed disagreeing on eBay (`Hadoop-Senior-Software-Engineer_R0069542` → title "Staff Software Engineer - Lakehouse"), ICF, Stryker (`Lead-Clinical-Data-Analyst` → title "Lead Data Engineer"), Empower (`Principal-Software-Engineer` → title "Principal Data Engineer") and Comcast. Slug-based filtering both misses core roles and mislabels them.
- **Drop the roster-URL facets on Geico and Zillow — they buy nothing.** Geico's unfiltered board (~280-290) is effectively the same size as the `remoteType=` facet board (282), and unfiltered pagination is simpler and safe. Zillow's `locations=` facet is a **no-op for coverage** (unfiltered board = 116).
- `POST https://{tenant}.wd{N}.myworkdayjobs.com/wday/cxs/{tenant}/{site}/jobs`
- Body: `{"appliedFacets":{},"limit":20,"offset":0,"searchText":""}`
- Needs curl/PowerShell (POST). Plain GET on the public-facing URL returns an empty JS shell.
- **Pagination gotcha:** the `total` field in the JSON response can read `0` on pages after `offset=0` (observed on Geico/Zillow/etc.) — don't trust it on interior pages. But **don't stop on "first empty array" either** — several tenants (Geico, PSA/Collectors, Zillow, confirmed 2026-08-03) don't return an empty page after the real last page; instead they silently **wrap around and re-serve page 1's data** forever, which can cause an infinite/hung scan if your stop condition is "wait for empty." **Safe stop rule: capture `total` from the page-1 (`offset=0`) response, and stop once cumulative `offset` reaches that total** (or once a page's results exactly duplicate an earlier page's req IDs). `limit` is capped at 20 — anything higher (tried 30/40/50/100) returns HTTP 400.
- ICF (`icf`/wd5/`icfexternal_career_site`) — 379 postings company-wide (modest size, full pagination is feasible ~19 pages if the keyword search ever needs cross-checking), but the `searchText="data engineer"` keyword search (122 results, fuzzy) already works fine and surfaced all matches cleanly on first scan (2026-08-06) — use keyword search for speed unless it starts looking incomplete. **Confirmed clean again 2026-08-06 r3** (first scan as part of a normal automated flow rather than the manual baseline check) — keyword search result count held exactly steady at 122 (good stability signal) and surfaced 2 genuinely new matches (R2602427, R2602428). No method adjustment needed.
- Companies on this pattern: Geico (`geico`/wd1/`External`), Zillow (`zillow`/wd5/`Zillow_Group_External`), Capital One (`capitalone`/wd12/`Capital_One`), BetMGM (`betmgminc`/wd5/`BetMGM`), VSP (`vsp`/wd1/`VSPVisionCareers`), Teladoc (`teladoc`/wd503/`teladochealth_is_hiring`), Cast and Crew (`castandcrew`/wd503/`CastandCrew`), PSA/Collectors (`collectorsuniverse`/wd1/`collectors`), Allstate (`allstate`/wd5/`allstate_careers`), Centene (`centene`/wd5/`centene_external`), J&J (tenant `jj`, host wd5, **site `jj`** — POST `https://jj.wd5.myworkdayjobs.com/wday/cxs/jj/jj/jobs`; public search UI ignores filters and job-detail pages 403, API-only is the sole path), Adobe (`adobe`/wd5/`external_experienced` — found via reverse-engineering, real endpoint is `careers.adobe.com/us/en/search-results?keywords={kw}` plain GET for SSR JSON, backend confirmed Workday), Cigna (`cigna`/wd5/`cignacareers`), Humana (`humana`/wd5/`Humana_External_Career_Site`, **facet-filter shortcut confirmed to undercount 2026-08-05 — see below, prefer full pagination**), CVS (`cvshealth`/wd1/`CVS_Health_Careers`), Kohl's (`kohls`/wd504/`kohlscareers` — see note below, facet filter is the practical choice here despite the undercount risk documented for J&J/Humana), The Hartford (`thehartford`/wd5/`Careers_External`), Nationwide (`nationwide`/wd1/`Nationwide_Career` — sits behind a Paradox.ai front end, see Paradox.ai section below, but CXS is directly queryable and preferred), Stryker (`stryker`/wd1/`StrykerCareers` — also behind Paradox.ai, CXS directly queryable and far more complete than the Paradox SSR's top-10-per-keyword cap), Dick's Sporting Goods (`dickssportinggoods`/wd1/`DSG` — fuzzy `searchText` is noisy, prefer facet-filtering by job category, same family as Kohl's), Wintrust (`wintrust`/wd1/**site `Search`** — literally the string "Search," confirmed via the page's embedded `window.workday` config, no redirect involved), Alteryx (`alteryx`/wd108/`AlteryxCareers`), eBay (`ebay`/wd5/**site `apply`** — unusual slug, front end is Phenom-fronted but CXS resolves directly).
- **🔴 Kohl's — the IT-facet undercount is PROVEN and the independent `searchText` sweep is MANDATORY every scan.** *(This supersedes the old "facet filter is the practical method" and "recommend keeping the facet as primary" notes, both of which are deleted — they were written before the undercount was measured and stated the opposite of the truth.)* Reproduced for a **6th consecutive scan**: the `jobFamilyGroup` = "Information Technology Group" facet (id `9f24b2b0acd3012617796bc9c218da04`) returned **11** postings while an independent `searchText` sweep pooled **135** — *the facet sees ~8% of the relevant surface, and has never once agreed.* Per-keyword `total` is accurate on this tenant: `data` 96, `reporting` 110, `analytics` 23, `analyst` 21, `data engineer` 15, `business intelligence` 3, `intelligence` 3. **Run the sweep; treat the facet as a supplement, never as the method.** Full pagination remains impractical (CXS `total` reports a capped **2,000**, overwhelmingly retail/warehouse/store roles, 100+ pages) — **the sweep, not full pagination, is what replaces the facet.** The front-end's own fuzzy `keywords=` search is not an alternative either (73 hits for "data engineer", mostly irrelevant JD-text matches). Known facet-invisible titles, kept as worked examples: Senior Personalization Analytics Analyst R475228-1 and Manager Advanced Analytics and Research R475788 both sit **outside** the IT Group facet.
- **J&J facet-filter shortcut confirmed to undercount (2026-08-04) — use full pagination instead.** The previously-documented approach (filter via `jobFamilyGroup` facet to "Data Analytics & Computational Sciences" / "Technology Product & Platform Management" / "Technology Enterprise Strategy & Security" instead of paginating all ~1,745 postings) missed a confirmed-active, correctly-tagged posting ("Data and Generative AI Engineer," R-079234-1 — its own JD lists "Job Function: Technology Product & Platform Management," one of the very facets filtered on, yet it didn't appear in the filtered results or a `searchText` keyword search for it). Full pagination (88 pages @ limit=20) completed in ~1-2 minutes with no crashes/timeouts/wraparound — fast enough that the facet shortcut isn't worth the undercount risk. **Recommendation: paginate J&J fully each scan; don't trust the facet filter as complete.** If a facet-filtered approach is used anyway for speed, treat any previously-seen match that goes missing as unconfirmed until spot-checked via full pagination or a direct job-detail fetch, not auto-flagged closed.
- **Humana facet-filter shortcut confirmed to undercount (2026-08-05 morning) — same failure mode as J&J, use full pagination instead.** The documented facet combination (Analytics & BI "Data Analytics"+"Data Harvesting" + Technology "Architecture"+"Cloud Service Offerings") returned only 15 postings that scan, but full pagination of the ~327 company-wide postings surfaced 39 genuinely relevant titles and revealed 2 previously-tracked open core matches (R-408582 "Lead, Business Information Architect"; R-409275 "Senior, Business Information Architect") sit in a facet category outside the filtered set — invisible to the documented method even though they're live. **Recommendation: paginate Humana fully each scan rather than trusting the facet shortcut as complete.** **Confirmed clean 2026-08-05 r2**: switched to full pagination (17 pages, 335 total postings) and all 3 previously-facet-invisible matches (including a 3rd, R-422722) were correctly found. Full pagination is now the standing method for Humana — don't revert to the facet shortcut.
- **Kemper removed from the pipeline (2026-08-03)** — not a priority company, and its ~520-posting pagination repeatedly ran long or crashed scans (likely the same wraparound issue above, at larger scale). Checked manually by the operator instead. If re-added, apply the safe stop rule above from the start.

### ADP Workforce Now
- Public JSON API, no auth needed: `https://workforcenow.adp.com/mascsr/default/careercenter/public/events/staffing/v1/job-requisitions?cid={cid}&ccId={ccId}`
- `cid` and `ccId` are in the original career-page URL's query string.
- Job detail/apply URL for a given posting: `https://workforcenow.adp.com/mascsr/default/mdf/recruitment/recruitment.html?cid={cid}&ccId={ccId}&type=JS&lang=en_US&jobId={jobId}` (jobId comes from the requisitions response).
- **Field-name gotcha (confirmed 2026-08-06 r2, re-confirmed on Overhaul and Parallels 2026-08-24):** the requisition's title is `requisitionTitle` (top-level), but the `jobId` used in the apply URL is **not** `requisitionId`/`itemID` — it's nested at `customFieldGroup.stringFields[].stringValue` where the matching entry has `nameCode.codeValue == "ExternalJobID"`. Using `itemID` instead produces a broken apply URL that still 200s but points at the wrong job, and a dedup check comparing `itemID` against a stored `jobId=` URL always misses.
- **🔴 Page 1 UNDER-RETURNS even at `$top=20` — a "returned < `$top` ⇒ last page" stop rule silently truncates.** Parallels' `$skip=0` returned **19** against `$top=20`; `$skip=20` returned the remaining 3 (`meta.totalNumber`=22). This generalizes the earlier `$top>20` finding — the truncation is not exclusive to large page sizes. **Correct stop rule: page until an empty page, or until cumulative unique reaches `meta.totalNumber`.**
- **`meta.totalNumber` EXISTS and is accurate on Workforce Now** (Overhaul 12, Parallels 22, both verified 2026-08-24) — **but it does not exist at all on ADP *myjobs*.** Do not carry the stop rule across; see the myjobs section.
- **Parallels posts genuine same-title duplicate reqs** (Application Security Engineer ×2, etc.) — same family as SeatGeek's per-location reqs, and another reason the "never dedup on title+location" rule is load-bearing.
- Confirmed working for: Parallels (`cid=8a680274-0b2e-46cf-b2cc-49bd2ef0c20f`, `ccId=9201289910657_2`), Overhaul (`cid=0607f7e2-b2c8-4bd9-9db0-502820edc413`, `ccId=19000101_000001`).

### ADP myjobs (Angular SPA, `cx-root`)
- The `/cx/job-listing` page itself is a client-rendered shell with no embedded JSON — don't bother scraping it directly.
- Real flow (reverse-engineered from the Angular bundle, 2026-08-02):
  1. `GET https://myjobs.adp.com/public/staffing/v1/career-site/{company}` → returns career-site config incl. `orgoid` and `myadpUrl`. **🔴 Gotcha (field-path drift): the placement of these two fields is NOT converging — check BOTH locations for BOTH fields, every scan.** Four distinct combinations observed on Direct Travel alone: both nested under `properties` (2026-08-11 r2) · split, `orgoid` nested / `myadpUrl` top-level (2026-08-20 r2) · both top-level (2026-08-21 r2) · **`orgoid` top-level (`G347FF7XGV30VRMA`) with `myadpUrl` nested under `properties`** (2026-08-24, and **repeated identically on r2 — the first time any placement has held across two consecutive runs**). Reading only one location returns `null`, which is indistinguishable from a broken endpoint. **One repeat is not convergence — keep checking both locations for both fields.**
  2. `GET https://my.adp.com/myadp_prefix/mycareer/public/staffing/v1/job-requisitions` with header `orgoid: {orgoid}` → full requisition list, paginated via `$top`/`$skip` query params.
  - **🔴 THE LIST RESPONSE HAS NO `meta` OBJECT AT ALL on myjobs.** Top-level keys are exactly `["count", "jobRequisitions"]`. So `meta.totalNumber` reads `None` and the ADP Workforce Now stop rule ("page until cumulative unique reaches `meta.totalNumber`") **cannot fire** — it silently degrades to the empty-page rule without saying so. **On ADP myjobs the empty page is the only usable stop signal.** `meta.totalNumber` is a *Workforce Now* field; do not carry it across.
  3. `GET .../job-requisitions/{reqId}` (same header) → full JD text per posting.
  4. Canonical shareable URL: `https://myjobs.adp.com/{company}/cx/job-details?reqId={reqId}`
- **Field names (confirmed 2026-08-06 r2):** the job-requisitions list response uses `jobTitle` (not `requisitionTitle` — that's the Workforce Now field name, this is the different myjobs/Angular-SPA API) and `reqId` (not `requisitionId`) at the top level of each entry.
- Confirmed working for: Direct Travel (`company=directtravel`, `orgoid=G347FF7XGV30VRMA`).

### iCIMS
- **🔴🔴 FULL UNSCOPED PAGINATION IS PRIMARY — THE KEYWORD SWEEP UNDER-FETCHES BY 3–5× AND IS DEMOTED** *(measured 2026-08-30)*. Walk `/api/jobs?page={n}` with no `keywords=` and no facet until the `jobs` array is empty. Measured against the documented sweep: **PetSmart 10,894 vs 3,708 (2.9×)**, **Ulta 10,010 vs 1,989 (5.0×)**, Post Holdings 282 (the sweep agrees on the small board only). **It is also CHEAPER**: PetSmart 1,154 requests in 161 s, Ulta 1,058 in 133 s, Post Holdings 73 in 5 s at 6 workers — against a 47-keyword sweep that had to be **killed unfinished after ~20 minutes**. The sweep's under-fetch is silent: every keyword returned its own total exactly.
  - **🟡 THIS OVERTURNS THE 2026-08-29 "the facet is NOT contained in the unscoped keyword sweep" FINDING — it was the SWEEP that was incomplete, not the facet that was extra.** Against the **full board**, `categories=Home Office` (23 records) and `tags2=Corporate` (46) each add **0**. Both facets are strict subsets of the full walk. **Keep the facet as a CONTROL only** (the garbage-control discipline below still applies); it is no longer a coverage supplement.
- **🔴🔴 `unique == totalCount` IS NOT A COMPLETENESS PROOF ON THIS FEED.** Ulta's full walk hit **10,010 == `totalCount` exactly** — and a small keyword cross-check still surfaced req **`344378`** (`Financial Analyst II`, noise) that the walk never served. **An exact match is a coincidence, not a guarantee.** **Standing method: after any full board walk, run a small (≥6-keyword) cross-check and diff it against the walk.** PetSmart and Post Holdings cross-checked clean over 6 keywords each; Ulta did not. This is the same family as the Travelers `seo_url` and PetSmart `distinctkeys` failures — **a health check that looks healthy is not a proof**; see the global dedup bullets.
- **Jibe `/api/jobs` pagination is 1-INDEXED, not 0-indexed.** `page=0` now throws `{"error":"An unexpected error occurred"}`. Omit the param (defaults to page 1) or start explicitly at `page=1`. (PetSmart, Ulta Beauty.)
- **The Jibe API exposes exact posted-date timestamps** — useful on r2+ scans for confirming a posting appeared after the morning run.
- Search results page, sometimes an XML feed exists — not yet found a working bypass. Cotiviti sits behind AWS WAF CAPTCHA at the HTTP level, so this may just be a dead end without a real browser session.
- **Jibe-powered iCIMS sites are different** (check for `app.jibecdn.com` / `assets.jibecdn.com` script tags in the raw HTML) — these expose a real JSON API at `{careers-domain}/api/jobs?page={n}`. 10 postings per page, `totalCount` field is accurate (unlike Workday's), paginate `page=1,2,3...` until an empty `jobs` array. The `?keywords=` query param the site itself uses is fuzzy/OR-matched across many fields and unreliable for filtering — pull all pages unfiltered and match titles client-side instead. **Response-shape gotcha (confirmed 2026-08-06 r2): each entry in the `jobs` array is wrapped one level deeper than expected — the actual fields (`req_id`, `title`, `full_location`, `location_type`, `apply_url`) live under a nested `data` key (`job["data"]["req_id"]`, not `job["req_id"]`)** — a naive top-level field read silently returns `None` for everything while still reporting the correct page/total counts, which can slip through unnoticed. Canonical link format: `https://{subdomain}-{company}.icims.com/jobs/{req_id}/login`.
- **🔴 A `totalCount: 0` ON A FACET PARAM CARRIES NO SIGNAL BY ITSELF — control it before believing it.** On PetSmart 2026-08-24 r1, `categories=Home%20Office` returned HTTP 200 `{"totalCount":0}` and a deliberately garbage control (`categories=zzqqxxnotreal`) returned the **byte-identical** response — an unrecognised facet value and a genuinely empty facet are indistinguishable. Same family as the SmartRecruiters `totalFound` trap and the Cribl Ashby decoy. **The control is what tells the two apart, and it is what proved the recovery on r2** (`Home%20Office` → `totalCount: 21` while the garbage control → 0, so the param carries signal again). **Always fire a garbage control alongside any facet query on this family, in both directions — a non-zero result is only trustworthy when the control is zero.**
- Confirmed working for: Post Holdings (`jobs.postholdings.com`), Ulta Beauty (`careers.ulta.com` — **gotcha: the working API path is the bare `careers.ulta.com/api/jobs`, NOT `careers.ulta.com/careers/api/jobs`** despite the site's own front-end URLs living under a `/careers/` path prefix; the prefixed path returns the Angular SPA shell instead), and **PetSmart (`careers.petsmart.com`) — see its entry below; it has now flipped in BOTH directions inside one month, so run the garbage control every scan.** Same `page`/`keywords`/`categories` query params and `job["data"]` nesting throughout. On Ulta, category-facet filtering remains far more reliable than the `keywords=` fuzzy search — a "BI developer"-style keyword query on these retail sites returns thousands of irrelevant "Retail Store Manager" postings.
- **🟢 PetSmart — RECOVERED 2026-08-24 r2. The DEGRADED mark and the "a zero here is an UNDER-FETCH" caveat are both LIFTED.** `careers.petsmart.com/api/jobs?categories=Home%20Office` → **`totalCount: 21`** with real corporate titles, **and the garbage control now returns 0**, so the param carries signal again. Numeric req IDs are back (the compound `103810972945-…` format is gone) and `/jobs/7664/` — the Senior Data Engineer that 404'd during the regression — is live again. Corporate keyword counts: `analyst` 2, `engineer` 3, `data` 31, `analytics` 25, `business intelligence` 4 → 37 unique corporate records.
  - **🔴 KEEP THE GARBAGE-CONTROL DISCIPLINE ON EVERY SCAN — this feed has now flipped in BOTH directions inside one month.** Run the control alongside the facet query and believe the facet only when the control is zero.
  - *Regression signature, kept so it is recognised if it recurs:* `categories=Home%20Office` → `{"totalCount":0}` matched byte-for-byte by the garbage control; facet list reduced to `city / country / state / tags1 / tags4` with `tags4`'s 28 job families containing **none** corporate; `keywords=analyst`/`engineer` → 0 and `keywords=data` → 14, all Distribution Warehouse; tracked matches 404ing; req IDs switching from numeric (`7664`) to compound (`103810972945-51828312407`). *(This is also why the 2026-08-21 r2 "both methods work, either is safe" note stays deleted — it was written before either flip.)*
  - The site still routes **corporate applicants** to `internal-petsmart.icims.com`, and external iCIMS host guesses (`petsmart.icims.com`, `careers-petsmart.icims.com`, `petsmartjobs-petsmart.icims.com`) all return **HTTP 405** (WAF). Recorded so it is not re-derived as a fresh lead; the public API is the working path.

### SAP SuccessFactors
- The `careers.{company}.com/search/?q={keyword}` results page server-side-renders real job data directly — a plain GET and HTML parse is enough, no API reverse-engineering needed.
- Parse `href="/job/{slug}/{reqId}/"` links out of the raw HTML.
- The `q=` search param is fuzzy/OR-matched across words in the title (e.g. querying "data engineer" also surfaces unrelated "...Engineer..." or "...Data..." titles) — still filter titles client-side rather than trusting search relevance.
- **🔴 Pagination: the SSR cap is 10 links per response and `&startrow=N` paginates correctly** (0/10/20/30/40 return distinct sets). *(This retires the old "30-link SSR cap, board size unattributable" note for good.)* The count string **is** present in the response as e.g. `10 of 74`, and an empty `q=` gives the whole board.
- **🔵 FULL-BOARD ENUMERATION IS THE PRIMARY METHOD — the keyword sweep is now optional.** `?q=&startrow={0,10,20,…}` walks the **entire board in ~52 requests**. Cross-checked against the 4-keyword pool: the full board added only warehouse/DC roles and **zero data titles**, so the sweep is a redundancy check rather than coverage. Keyword totals drift run to run and are not a health signal — `data` 74→73, `analytics` 61→59, `analyst` 9, `intelligence` 3; board 503→511→509→**516**.
- **🔴 ENUMERATION DOES *NOT* TERMINATE ON AN EMPTY PAGE — THE OLD "terminates cleanly on the first response containing no `jobTitle-link` anchors" NOTE IS WRONG AND IS DELETED.** Past the end of the board this endpoint **re-serves the last page forever**; a first attempt ran **300 requests without stopping.** **Add repeat-page detection: stop when two consecutive responses yield an identical href list.** **🔴 THE STOP ROW IS NOT A CONSTANT — IT TRACKS BOARD SIZE AND MUST NEVER BE HARDCODED.** Measured values: `530` (when this was written) → `520` → `510` (2026-08-28 r2, 2026-08-29) → **`500` (2026-08-30, board 487)** — **a THIRD distinct value in three runs, and the third consecutive run in which a hardcoded figure was stale.** Derive the stop from the repeat-page condition every run; a figure from these notes is a description, never a parameter.
  - **⚠️ The COUNT STRING'S unreliability is itself PER-RUN — keep treating it as a description too.** It oscillated 509/510 within a single walk on 2026-08-29 and read a stable **487** throughout on 2026-08-30. **Neither behaviour is a property, so it can never be a health check** in either direction; use the repeat-page condition and the unioned record set.
  - **⚠️ The optional keyword sweep's yield is PER-RUN — keep running it, but do not budget on its yield.** It recovered 9 records on 2026-08-29 and added **0** on 2026-08-30 (46 keywords / 259 requests / 486 unique, a strict subset of the full walk). It is cheap redundancy, not coverage.
- **🔴 Three silent parse traps on this family — all of them return a plausible-looking empty or spliced board rather than an error:**
  1. **The anchor class carries a hash suffix.** It is `class="jobTitle-link fontcolor<hash>"`; a regex requiring `class="jobTitle-link"` **exactly** matches zero links and returns an empty board that reads as a legitimate "0 postings".
  2. **Each posting is emitted twice** (desktop + screen-reader variant), and the sr-only variant's anchor text contains the **entire tile** — title, location, business unit, req ID *and the next job's title* — so a naive inner-text grab splices two jobs together. **Fix: take only the first text node inside the anchor, then keep the shortest title per href.** Location lives in `<div id="job-{jobId}-desktop-section-location-value">`.
  3. **🔴 hrefs must be HTML-UNESCAPED, not just titles.** Titles containing `&` make the raw `href` carry `&amp;`, and the emitted URL is broken (`…Global-Insurance-&amp;-Risk-Management-Analyst…`). Run `html.unescape()` on the **href** as well as the title — `rules.md` §6 requires every reported posting to be a working link.
- Confirmed working for: Under Armour (`careers.underarmour.com`).

### Rippling ATS
- **Job-detail JD text is at `description.role` / `description.company`** (rich HTML) in the `_next/data` payload. The first top-level `"description":"..."` match is an unrelated form-field help string, **not** the JD. (Found on DNC.)
- **PowerShell `ConvertFrom-Json` throws a duplicate-key error** (`"url"` vs `"Url"`) on the Rippling `_next/data` payload. Fall back to raw-text grep. (Found on Easy Dynamics.)
- Job data is not embedded in the initial HTML (just i18n strings) — it's fetched client-side.
- Recipe: `GET https://ats.rippling.com/{companySlug}/jobs`, grep the raw HTML for `"buildId":"([^"]+)"`. Then `GET https://ats.rippling.com/_next/data/{buildId}/{companySlug}/jobs.json` — plain GET, no auth, no pagination seen so far (small company boards). Job postings are in a react-query cache entry with `"items":[{"id","name","url","department":{"name"},"locations":[{"name","workplaceType",...}]}]`.
- **✅ `buildId` is PLATFORM-WIDE, not per-company.** It identifies Rippling's deploy, not the tenant: DNC and Easy Dynamics return the identical string, confirmed independently again on 2026-08-24 r2. **Fetch it once per scan and reuse it across every Rippling board.**
- **🔴 `buildId` HAS ROTATED — it is `JB605EkvD8rzfPbDXB5H6` (was `O-U4HQp_j6fmwpBJVHHcP` for three consecutive runs). THE "far less volatile than 'changes on every deploy' implies / a stale-looking value is not a bug" NOTE IS ITSELF STALE AND IS DELETED.** **Never hardcode, cache across scans, or carry a `buildId` forward from these notes — fetch it fresh every scan (one cheap GET) and use whatever comes back.** That instruction turns out to be load-bearing: the boards still read correctly on the rotated value (Easy Dynamics 14, DNC 15) on the raw-text extractor below, but a carried-forward value would have hit a dead path. A *changed* value is normal and is not evidence of a failed fetch either.
- **🔴 DO NOT `json.loads` then re-serialise `jobs.json` before regexing it.** `json.dumps` inserts `", "` / `": "` separators, so a regex written against the **wire** format matches **zero** and returns an empty board that reads as a legitimate "0 postings". **Parse the raw response text.** Working extractor (found on Easy Dynamics 2026-08-24, `re.S`): `\{"id":"([0-9a-fA-F-]{20,})","name":"((?:[^"\\]|\\.)*)".*?"locations":\[(.*?)\],"language"`. Easy Dynamics' board is **14** on this extractor, against the 10-11 the broken path reported.
- **Strip whitespace from `name`.** At least one posting's `name` carries a **leading space** (`" Data Engineer"`), which breaks exact-title comparisons.
- Canonical job URL: `https://ats.rippling.com/{companySlug}/jobs/{id}`.
- Confirmed working for: Easy Dynamics (`companySlug=easy-dynamics-corporation`).

### Ross Stores (Custom, bespoke .NET job-search widget)
- The public search page (`jobs.rossstores.com/search/searchjobs?keyword={kw}`) doesn't embed results — they're loaded client-side via `fetch()`.
- Recipe: `GET https://jobs.rossstores.com/search/searchresultslist?keyword={kw}&page={n}` — plain GET, no auth. Returns JSON `{"jobsHtml": "...", "paginationHtml": "..."}` (both HTML fragments, escaped). Parse `jobsHtml` for `<li class="job-item" data-record-key="{guid}">...<h3 class="job-title" ...>{title}</h3>...<address>{street}</address>...City, State</dt><dd><span>{city, state}</span>...<a href="{detailPath}" class="view-details-link">`. Parse `paginationHtml` for `Showing X-Y of {total}` and `totalPages` (also embedded as a JS var `var totalPages = N;`) to know when to stop paging.
- **The `keyword=` param is fuzzy/full-text, not title-matched** — a "data engineer" query returned generic-titled results like "Engineer II/III/IV" and various Analyst/Manager titles with no "Data" or "Engineer" combination in the title itself. Title-only filtering may not be enough to judge fit here; JD text isn't in this endpoint's response, would need a separate per-job detail fetch (`/search/jobdetails/{slug}/{guid}`) to check.
- **✅ A bare `data` query alone is sufficient coverage — confirmed FOUR consecutive scans** (119 unique of 120 reported over 10 pages, then 121 over 11 pages, twice). The parse recipe above is unchanged and exact. Note the endpoint is full-text OR across all fields, so `business intelligence` returns ~7,099 — the breadth is why one broad keyword suffices.
- **🟢 ADOPT THE PROGRESSIVE REPEAT-PASS METHOD HERE — A SINGLE PASS IS NOT TRUSTWORTHY** *(established 2026-08-30)*. Single pass **123/126 = 97.6%**; one repeat pass → **126/126 = 100%**, and a second pass confirmed it stable. **⚠️ Ross returned 126/126 in a SINGLE pass on 2026-08-29, so do NOT treat a single-pass 100% here as a board property** — it was a lucky draw. This board re-orders between requests exactly as Progressive, Airbnb and Under Armour do. **Stop condition is "repeat until a pass adds nothing", never a fixed pass count** — see the WordPress/FacetWP recipe, where that rule is recorded in full.
- Confirmed working for: Ross Stores.

### Oracle Recruiting Cloud (Custom-labeled, but has a real API)
- Underlying REST API: `https://{tenant}.fa.{region}.oraclecloud.com/hcmRestApi/resources/latest/recruitingCEJobRequisitions`
- Paginated (`limit`/`offset`-style, 100 per page observed).
- Confirmed working for: American Express — tenant `egug`, region `us2` (`https://egug.fa.us2.oraclecloud.com/hcmRestApi/...`). The public-facing `careers.americanexpress.com` SPA itself stays an empty shell; go straight to the API.
- Also confirmed working for: Hilton — tenant `efet`, region `us2`, site `CX_1009`. Full working query: `https://efet.fa.us2.oraclecloud.com/hcmRestApi/resources/latest/recruitingCEJobRequisitions?onlyData=true&expand=requisitionList&finder=findReqs;siteNumber=CX_1009,facetsList=LOCATIONS;WORKPLACE_TYPES;WORK_LOCATIONS;TITLES;CATEGORIES,limit=20,offset=N,sortBy=POSTING_DATES_DESC,keyword={kw}`. Results are in `items[0].requisitionList`, with `TotalJobsCount` on `items[0]` for pagination. Canonical job URL: `https://efet.fa.us2.oraclecloud.com/hcmUI/CandidateExperience/en/sites/CX_1009/job/{Id}`. **Note:** in PowerShell, `Invoke-WebRequest -UseBasicParsing` sometimes returns `.Content` as a raw byte array instead of a string for this host (inconsistent, cause unclear) — if `ConvertFrom-Json` on `.Content` fails or returns null, decode explicitly first: `[System.Text.Encoding]::UTF8.GetString($r.Content)`.
- **🔴 `limit=100` works on the RC finder — the `limit=20` in the query above is stale.** Confirmed on both tenants; use 100 and cut the request count 5×. **`TotalJobsCount` is accurate** on Hilton and Amex alike — page against it, and treat a short page as a bug to investigate rather than a stop signal. *(Amex has now run clean at `limit=100` on consecutive scans — `TotalJobsCount` 413 then 414, unique matching exactly both times, no short pages.)*
- **🟢 `PrimaryLocationCountry` (ISO-2) IS A FIRST-CLASS FIELD — no location text-parsing needed at all, and it defuses the `, XX` trap by construction.** Re-confirmed 2026-08-27 across a full-board histogram (US 1,378 · IN 337 · GB 323 · MY 293 · JP 221 · AE 191 · TH 137 · SG 126 · SA 125 · MX 124 · QA 116 · KW 98 · TR 79 · MV 74 · EG 62 …). **It handles both directions of the `, XX` trap live:**
  - `Accounting Clerk/Analyst — Panama, Panama, Panama` resolves **`PA` = Panama**, where a state-abbreviation regex reads Pennsylvania.
  - `Accounting Clerk/Analyst — Ponce, PR, United States` resolves **`US`**, where a regex would have to special-case Puerto Rico. Same family as the Radancy `san-juan` note.
- **🔴🔴 ORACLE-RC DEDUP: SCOPE BEFORE YOU MATCH — the constant `CX_####` segment defeats the underscore matcher, not just the bare-segment matcher.** `CX_1009` (Hilton, 100% of postings) and `CX_1` (Amex, 100%) **contain underscores**, so the standard `_([A-Za-z]{0,3}[-_.]?\d[\w.\-]*)` extracts `1009` / `1` before any scoping branch runs. **On an Oracle-RC host, CLEAR the key set and index ONLY the segment following `/job/`.** Fired for real on 2026-08-27: Hilton showed **37 of 37 candidates suppressed, 0 new, no exception, `distinctkeys=8`** — and the frequency guard did not save it. After the fix, 32 of 37 were genuinely untracked. Amex's bare `1` is worse still: a one-character key collides across *other employers' boards*.
- **🔴 AMEX IS DUAL-SCHEME — fold the host aliases or half the tracker misses.** The API serves `egug.fa.us2.oraclecloud.com/…/sites/CX_1/job/{Id}` while `seen_jobs.json` stores `careers.americanexpress.com/en/sites/CX_1/job/{Id}`. A host-based special case that knows only one form misses the other. Reproduced 2026-08-27: all 3 Amex non-exact suppressions depended on it.
- **✅ The Cigna/Amex `26######` cross-employer ID collision is LIVE AND RECURS EVERY RUN, on the same req ID.** A Cigna posting key-matches stored Amex `.../CX_1/job/26009848`. **Keep the same-employer host assertion** — it was the only cross-host match across all six groups on both 2026-08-26 r2 and 2026-08-27, and it refused correctly both times.
- **🔴 Hilton — DROP THE `keyword=` PARAM. UNFILTERED FULL-BOARD PAGINATION AT `limit=100` IS PRIMARY.** *(Reversed 2026-08-25 r2. This overturns the 2026-08-06 "do NOT drop the keyword param" instruction, which stood for three weeks and was costing 41% of the board.)*
  - **Measured: 45 requests → 4,375 unique == `TotalJobsCount` 4,375, exact 100%.** Against the keyword union's **74 requests → 2,500–2,599 unique (57–59% of the board)**. So full pagination is simultaneously **cheaper and 1.7× more complete**.
  - **Why the old instruction was wrong:** the 2026-08-06 disproof computed "~200 pages" from `limit=20`. `limit=100` retired that arithmetic (see the bullet above) and **nobody re-tested the conclusion that depended on it.** The stated board size was also stale — it is **4,375, not ~4,005**.
  - **Cross-checked before switching:** page 1 of all 18 documented keywords (`data` 520, `developer` 1,787, `reporting` 1,238, `analytics` 177, `insight` 161, `database` 87, `analyst` 39, `business intelligence` 26, `data engineer` 20, `power bi` 14, `tableau` 9, …) produced **zero records absent from the full board.** The union adds nothing.
  - **Consequence: `developer` as a documented cost driver is MOOT**, and Hilton is no longer a sensible rebalancing donor — it is now one of the cheapest sites in its group at 45 requests / ~14 s.
  - *Kept for provenance only, do not act on it:* the retired union's genuine zero-return keywords were `informatics`, `databricks`, `data warehouse`, `etl`, `hadoop`, `snowflake`.
- **🔵 GENERAL LESSON — a cost argument that depends on a page-size constant DIES when that constant changes.** Hilton's keyword rule was correct when written and wrong the moment `limit=100` landed, but it read as a settled finding rather than a conditional one. **When a recipe rules out a method on request-count grounds, record the page size the arithmetic assumed**, so a later `limit` change flags the conclusion for re-testing instead of silently outliving it.

### Phenom People (Custom-labeled SSR sites)
- Job data is often server-side-rendered directly into the page HTML (not JS-only) — a plain `curl`/fetch of the job-search or job-detail page returns real content without needing to execute JS.
