- **Method upgrade (2026-08-04): don't use the Phenom front-end's `?keywords=` search at all if the backend is Workday.** It's badly fuzzy (e.g. Cigna's "data engineer" query returned 295 "hits," ~95% irrelevant, matching on "data" or "engineer" appearing anywhere in the JD). Go straight to the underlying **Workday CXS API's `jobFamilyGroup` facet filter** instead (same trick documented for J&J) — far more precise and gives an accurate company-wide total for free. Confirmed working 2026-08-04 for:
  - **🔴 Cigna — THE FACET IS RETIRED. Use full empty-`searchText` CXS pagination instead** (`POST https://cigna.wd5.myworkdayjobs.com/wday/cxs/cigna/cignacareers/jobs`). Full pagination returned **559 postings in 29 pages** with no wraparound on 2026-08-24. The `jobFamilyGroup` = "Data & Analytics" facet (ID `b7947bbbfff201d41a750216390c8516`) has **undercounted on every scan since 2026-08-11**, and the "facet ID undercounts, supplement with direct detail fetches" workaround is retired with it — the workaround was papering over a facet that should simply not be used. *(The facet ID is recorded here only so it is not re-derived from scratch as a fresh lead.)*
  - **Humana** — `POST https://humana.wd5.myworkdayjobs.com/wday/cxs/humana/Humana_External_Career_Site/jobs`, filter to combined "Analytics & Business Intelligence" + "Technology - Architecture"/"Technology - Cloud" sub-families (39 of 321 total). Distinct from `CenterWell_External_Career_Site`, which is clinical-only.
  - **CVS** — `POST https://cvshealth.wd1.myworkdayjobs.com/wday/cxs/cvshealth/CVS_Health_Careers/jobs`. **🔴 The "Data and Analytics" facet is nearly worthless for coverage on its own — the KEYWORD SWEEP carries the run.** Facet counts: "Data and Analytics" **45**, Technology **185**, against `business intelligence` *alone* at **418**. Keep the facet only as a supplement, and never report a CVS result from the facet alone.
    - **⚠️ `power bi` IS A BROAD FULL-TEXT KEYWORD ON THIS TENANT, NOT A PHRASE — it returns 2,701 on its own and expands the pool 690 → 3,808 (5.5×).** It OR-matches "power" and "bi" across JD bodies. **Same class as Marsh's `bi` and Hilton's `developer`: keep it if you want the depth, but budget for it and expect the extra volume to be noise, not coverage.** Record it as a known-broad keyword so a future scan does not read the pool jump as a site change.
    - **Build retry-until-clean into the pagination — see the CXS recipe. Keep the retry even though it has not fired for two consecutive runs**; the 2026-08-21 r2 `WinError 10060` mid-pagination abort is exactly what it exists for.
- **CVS Workday/Phenom sync gap:** some reqs 404/410 on the public `jobs.cvshealth.com` front-end but are live on the native `cvshealth.wd1.myworkdayjobs.com` URL — if a Phenom fetch comes up empty for a known-good req, try the native Workday URL before concluding it's closed. **Caveat (2026-08-04):** this is not always just a front-end sync gap — CVS's R0835947-1 ("Senior Data Engineer," Wellesley MA) was previously explained this way, but this scan it was absent from the *full* Workday CXS listing too, i.e. genuinely closed. Don't assume "sync gap" without checking the full API listing first.
- Adobe (`careers.adobe.com`) is also Phenom-based — resolved 2026-08-03, see the Workday CXS section above (real method is a plain GET on `search-results?keywords=`, not tenant-guessing an API). **Confirmed 2026-08-04: this endpoint has no working pagination** — `start=` is a no-op, coverage is capped at whatever the fuzzy top-10-per-keyword relevance ranking surfaces. Run it with a few different keywords and union the results rather than expecting one query to be exhaustive; spot-check any known match that doesn't appear in any keyword's top-10 directly via its Workday job-detail URL before flagging it closed. **Keyword list expanded to 7 (2026-08-06 r4): "data engineer", "analytics engineer", "business intelligence", "data architect", "data platform", "AI platform engineer", and now "data analyst"** — the newly-added "data analyst" keyword surfaced a genuinely new match (Senior Data Analyst, Adobe Stock) not found by the other 6, so keep it permanently in the union going forward.

### WordPress + FacetWP (Custom-labeled)
- **🔴 CORRECTION — the plain-text "Showing X-Y of Z" label is NOT where the total lives.** It is in an escaped JSON string: `"jobs_pager":"Showing 1-10 results out of total N open jobs"`. Job cards are `<a href="https://careers.airbnb.com/positions/{id}/" tabindex="0">{title}</a>`. This overrides the "accurate running total" claim below.
- **🔴 `/positions/page/1/` now 301-redirects to the base `/positions/` URL.** Fetch the base URL for page 1; `/page/2/` onward paginates as documented.
- **Fetch client matters:** Python `urllib.request` gets HTTP 404 from `careers.airbnb.com` even with a browser UA. Use `curl -sL --compressed -A "<Chrome UA>"`.
- **Coverage runs ~93-97%, is not exact, and can exceed 100%** — FacetWP re-orders records between requests, so records get skipped *and* surfaced beyond the reported total across a full pagination run (187 of 192; 192 unique against a `jobs_pager` total of 190; 183 of 195 over 20 pages). Treat the total as approximate in both directions, not a boundary, and **do not read a result inside that band as an under-fetch.**
- **🔴 THE REPEAT-PASS STOP CONDITION IS "REPEAT UNTIL A PASS ADDS NOTHING" — NOT A FIXED PASS COUNT. The documented "two repeat passes" is WRONG and is corrected** *(measured 2026-08-30 at Progressive: pass 1 **253/286 = 88.5%**, pass 2 **276 = 96.5%**, pass 3 **286/286 = 100%**)*. **Two passes would have stopped at 96.5% and reported it as complete.** Run passes until one contributes zero new IDs, then stop.
  - **This method is the standing treatment for every re-ordering board, not an Airbnb/Progressive quirk.** Confirmed load-bearing on **Airbnb** (pass 1 got 169, pass 2 found the 170th) and newly required at **Ross Stores** (97.6% → 100%) on the same run. **Under Armour** is the fourth member of the family.
  - ⚠️ **A board that returned an exact 100% in one pass last run is NOT exempt** — Ross did exactly that on 2026-08-29 and needed a repeat pass on 2026-08-30. The single-pass result is a draw, not a property.
- **🔴 Use the UNFILTERED `/positions/` query, not `?_workplace_type=live-and-work-anywhere`** (192 vs 152). `profile.md` imposes no location filter, and the unfiltered query is what surfaced the Gurugram Advanced-Analytics roles.
- **🔴 AIRBNB FAILURE MODES — TWO DISTINCT SIGNATURES, DO NOT CONFLATE THEM. Both are cleared as of 2026-08-24 r2; keep the signatures for recognition.**
  - *Historical, intermittent:* **HTTP 403.** Retry-able; the site has flipped blocked ↔ working repeatedly.
  - *2026-08-24 r1 — **HTTP 429 at the edge, DOMAIN-WIDE**, cleared by r2.* It affected `/`, `/positions/`, `/sitemap.xml` and `/wp-json/` **equally**. **Ineffective against it:** the full browser header set, a `Referer`, cookie-jar warm-up (`everest_cookie` is returned but does not help), `--http1.1`, `-4`. It persisted ≥15 min and **the very first request of the session 429'd — it was NOT volume-induced**, so backing off the request rate will not clear it.
  - **🔴 THE RESPONSE HEADERS ARE NOT THE SIGNATURE — do not diagnose a block from them.** On the fully-working r2 fetch (HTTP 200, 59KB of real content), `Server: nginx`, `X-Powered-By: WordPress VIP`, `x-airbnb-sureride` and `Akamai-Request-BC` are **all still present.** **Only the status code and the 564-byte body length distinguished the 429**; the header set is identical either way. A new `datadome` cookie is now set, which suggests the protection was **swapped rather than removed** — expect the next block to look different again.
- Airbnb (`careers.airbnb.com`) — plain GET, paginate via `https://careers.airbnb.com/positions/page/{n}/`, 10 postings/page.

### Etsy (Custom-labeled, method now confirmed 2026-08-04)
- **🔴 CORRECTION — the `data-count` attribute is PERMANENTLY UNTRUSTWORTHY, not "sometimes off".** It has now disagreed with the extracted record set on **six separate scans**, most recently (2026-08-29) reading `31` against **30** records actually extracted — note it has now disagreed in **both directions**, which kills the last argument for reading it as a floor or a ceiling. **Ignore it entirely; count the extracted records directly.** This overrides the "accurate total" claim in the bullet below — do not soften it back to a caveat.
- **🔴 THE WORKING LOCATION MATCHER IS ID-ANCHORED, AND IT HAD BEEN RE-DERIVED FROM SCRATCH ON AT LEAST THREE RUNS WITHOUT EVER BEING WRITTEN DOWN — homing it here 2026-09-02 r3.** Anchor on `<span id="location_icon_text_{id}">`, keyed to each posting's **own id**, rather than any positional or nth-match read. Measured **30/30 with 0 empties** on r3 and again on r2. **Do not re-derive a positional matcher** — it is the repeated-rediscovery cost this file exists to stop.
- **⚠️ ETSY URLs CARRY NO ID AT ALL — expect the `distinctkeys > 1` assertion to fire, and do NOT "fix" it.** Four of five stored Etsy URLs are pure slugs (`/jobs/senior-director-product-analytics-brooklyn-new-york-united-states`); only one carries a trailing UUID. This is a true characteristic of the board. **Etsy dedup is therefore necessarily URL-string-based and exposed to slug drift failing toward FALSE POSITIVES** — same family as the Progressive drift, but with no ID to fall back on. Spot-check any "new" Etsy match against the whole tracker before reporting it.
- **`?page=N` is a no-op** — pages 1-4 return the identical 30-posting set. The whole board fits in one response.
- `https://careers.etsy.com/jobs/search?page=1&workplace_type%5B%5D=remote&query=` — plain GET SSRs real content, no headless browser needed (previously undocumented/unconfirmed). Job cards are in elements with class `job-search-results-card-title` (title + href). A `data-count="{n}"` attribute on the page gives an accurate total for the current filter. Small company — only ~4 remote postings total as of 2026-08-04, none data-relevant so far.
- **Method changed 2026-08-11: the `workplace_type[]=remote` filter started returning 0 postings (`data-count="0"`).** Since profile.md doesn't require remote-filtering (the company list is pre-curated for remote-friendly employers), switched to the unfiltered query `https://careers.etsy.com/jobs/search?page=1&query=` — returned the full 29-posting company-wide listing cleanly. Use the unfiltered form as primary going forward; the remote filter may be worth re-testing periodically in case it recovers.
- **Title-extraction regex corrected 2026-08-13**: the `job-search-results-card-title` class lives on the `<h3>` wrapper, not the `<a>` tag — a regex anchored to the class directly on `<a>` returns 0 matches. Working pattern: `<h3[^>]*job-search-results-card-title[^>]*>\s*<a[^>]*href="([^"]+)"[^>]*>\s*([^<]+?)\s*</a>`.

### Findly / CWS (Custom-labeled)
- Raw HTML exposes the underlying jobs API directly for Allstate (`www.allstate.jobs`) — **but this regressed 2026-08-03** (page reverted to a pure JS shell, 0 embedded jobs). Fallback that worked instead: **Allstate is also Workday-backed** — tenant `allstate`, host wd5, site `allstate_careers`. Use the standard Workday CXS recipe above.
- Centene turned out to be Workday-backed too (2026-08-03 finding) — tenant `centene`, host wd5 (confirmed directly again 2026-08-04 r2, no fallback needed), site `centene_external`. Its public `jobs.centene.com` listing page had stopped returning embedded job data; use the CXS API directly instead.
- **CWS/Symphony Talent (Xcelerate) API pattern reverse-engineered 2026-08-06.** Some WordPress+CWS-widget sites' own visible client config (`cws_opts.api`, pointing at `jobsapi-google.m-cloud.io`) 404s no matter what param combination is tried — the actual working endpoint is the older host: `GET https://jobsapi-internal.m-cloud.io/api/job?Organization={numericOrgId}&SearchText={keyword}&Limit=50&fuzzy=false` — plain GET, no auth, no special headers. `Organization` must be the **numeric** ID pulled from `cws_opts.smartPost_org` in the page source (a GUID-looking `org` value shown elsewhere on the page does NOT work as this param). `fuzzy=false` is essential — omit it or set `true` and `SearchText` is effectively ignored, returning the full unfiltered company total instead. Response JSON: `queryResult[]` array with `ref` (req ID), `title`, `primary_city`/`primary_state`, `url` (canonical job page), `entity_status`. Confirmed working for: Travelers (`Organization=1907`), Home Depot (`Organization=1814`). Both are ultimately Workday-backed underneath (`"ats_portalid":"Workday"` appears in every record, and `seo_url`/apply-link fields resolve to `{tenant}.wd5.myworkdayjobs.com/...`) but the CWS proxy API is the practical method — no need to separately reverse-engineer the Workday tenant/site for either.
- **🔴 `fuzzy=false` is EXACT-PHRASE FULL-TEXT OVER THE WHOLE JD BODY — it is NOT a title match.** This is the single most important thing to know about this API. On Travelers, bare `data` → **362** hits, `engineer` → 51, `analyst` → 21. Consequence: keywords enumerate, titles filter — never treat a keyword as a title scope, and never trim the keyword list toward target titles.
- **`totalHits` is CONDITIONALLY accurate — trustworthy on narrow queries, junk on broad ones.**
  - **Travelers:** accurate. `queryResult.Count` has matched `totalHits` on all 8 keywords across consecutive scans — cross-check the two and page via `offset` when short. `Limit` hard-caps at 50.
  - **Home Depot:** accurate on every narrow query (`analytics` 158, `intelligence` 58, `business intelligence` 29, `tableau` 88, `data analytics` 46, `data science` 36, `data engineer` 6) but bare `SearchText=data` still reports **~25,203**, the old company-wide figure. A control (`SearchText=zzzqqqnotreal` → `totalHits=0`) confirms SearchText genuinely filters — bare `data` really does match ~25k JD bodies. **Bare `data` is unusable on Home Depot; every narrower query's `totalHits` is fine.** Results are relevance-ranked (all 13 core data titles appeared within the first 1,250 records).
- **Sweep breadth — the documented small sweeps badly undercounted both tenants:**
  - **Travelers: 8 keywords are standing** — `data engineer`, `data analyst`, `analytics engineer`, `business intelligence`, `data architect`, `data platform`, `data science`, `analytics` → ~63-64 unique, against 21 for the old 5-keyword list. `data platform` and `analytics` are the two that were added and `analytics` (62) is the largest single contributor. **`data analyst` and `analytics engineer` return 0 and are still worth keeping** — one request each, and on a full-text endpoint a zero is a real answer.
  - **Home Depot: a 16-keyword sweep pools 207 unique**, against 154 for the prior list and ~56 for the old 4-keyword one. **`snowflake` and `informatics` both return `totalHits=0` — those are real zeros, keep them.** Bare `analytics` needs pagination: `offset=51,101,151,…`, stopping cleanly at `offset=201`.
    - **⚠️ Bare `analyst` is OPTIONAL AND NOT RECOMMENDED — it takes the pool 207 → 560 and yields ZERO additional matches.** The extra ~350 are retail/store/finance bare-function "Analyst" titles, all noise under the narrow borderline bar. **Recorded so the 207-vs-560 gap is not misread as a coverage hole later** — it is a precision choice, not an under-fetch.
    - **🟡 `metrics` (`totalHits` 4,276) and `reporting` (2,270) ARE BROAD FULL-TEXT TOKENS ON THIS TENANT — same class as bare `data` and bare `analyst`** *(measured 2026-08-30)*. Together they took the pool **3,870 → 6,289**, and the **2,419 records they alone reached produced ZERO additional reportable titles.** They are part of the mandated enumeration net so they stay, but **budget ~132 extra requests for them and RECORD THE JUMP** — otherwise the 1,429 → 6,289 movement across two runs reads as hiring rather than as net breadth. Same lesson as Kohl's `visualization` and Travelers' 70→369.
    - **⚠️ The real-zero list DRIFTS — re-derive it, never carry it forward. `snowflake` is NO LONGER a real zero (returns 1) as of 2026-08-30**, leaving `informatics` and `dbt` on the list. A keyword that stops returning zero is not a defect and a keyword that starts is not a coverage hole.
- **🔴 Home Depot parse trap:** a greedy `.*"title":"…"` regex silently returns an **empty title on ~9% of CWS records**, because the JD `description` field contains escaped quotes — fall back to the URL slug when the title comes back empty. Separately, a title filter of `analyt` misses every "Analyst" title (`analy`**`s`**`t`) — **use `analy`.** Both fail toward false negatives.
  - 🔴 **THAT `analy` INSTRUCTION IS ABOUT THE CLIENT-SIDE TITLE FILTER ONLY. IT IS NOT A `SearchText` KEYWORD, AND USING IT AS ONE PRODUCES A CONFIDENT ARTIFACT ZERO** *(found 2026-09-01 at Home Depot)*. `fuzzy=false` is **exact-phrase full-text**, so a stem matches nothing: `analy` → `totalHits` **0** and `visuali` → **0**, on a board that genuinely holds thousands of Analyst titles. Sweep with the **full words** (`analysis`, `analytics`, `analyst`, `visualization`) and filter titles on the stem client-side. **This is the same artifact class as the Travelers stem zeros — and it must never be recorded alongside a genuine confirmed zero**, because a stem returning 0 on an exact-phrase endpoint says nothing whatever about the board.
- **🔴🔴 READ `url`, NEVER `seo_url` — `seo_url` IS A FALSE FRIEND AND IT PRESENTS AS A WHOLE-BOARD FALSE-POSITIVE FLOOD** *(found 2026-08-30 at Travelers)*. The canonical field is plain **`url`** — `https://careers.travelers.com/job/{numeric id}/{slug}/`, which is the form the tracker stores. **`seo_url` is the underlying Workday apply link** (`travelers.wd5.myworkdayjobs.com/…`), a completely different ID space. Preferring it reported **31 of 31 qualifying items as brand new.** Measured: the Workday `R-` id appears in **0 of the 41 stored Travelers URLs**, while the CWS numeric `id` appears in **33**.
  - **🔴 `ref` (the `R-` form) MUST NOT BE A DEDUP KEY** on this carrier — same conclusion as the bullet below, reached from the other direction.
  - 🔴 **The standing health check did NOT catch this: `distinctkeys` read a healthy 361 throughout.** Same structural-death family as J&J's plural `/jobs/` scheme and Progressive's `{numericId}-{slug}` — see the global dedup bullets, where the general lesson (**a healthy health check is not a proof**) is recorded.
- **🔴 Both tenants dedup on the NUMERIC JOB ID in the URL, NOT the CWS `ref`/Req ID** — see "Global / common rules".

### Paradox.ai (Custom-labeled, SSR `window.__PRELOAD_STATE__` JSON)
- A career-site builder (vendor behind the "Olivia" recruiting chatbot) used by several large traditional employers — confirmed 2026-08-06 for Nationwide, Stryker, Marriott, and McDonald's. Front-end signature: assets served from `cdn.sites.paradox.ai`/`paradox-prod-careersites`. Critically, the current page's job-search results are embedded directly in the raw HTML as `window.__PRELOAD_STATE__ = {...};` inside a `<script>` tag — no separate XHR/API call needed to get page 1. **Parse with a real JSON parser, not a naive regex up to `</script>`** — a trailing `window.__BUILD__ = ...` statement often follows in the same script block and can bleed into a sloppy regex match.
- Key fields inside the blob: `jobSearch.jobs[]` (each with `title`, `requisitionID`, `locations[]` — including `isRemote`/`locationParsedText` — `applyURL`, `originalURL`), and `jobSearch.totalJob`.
- **🔴 `originalURL` IS RELATIVE** on both Marriott and McDonald's — the shape is `{slug}/job/{HEX}`, with no scheme or host. Prefix it with `https://careers.marriott.com/` / `https://careers.mcdonalds.com/`. Emitting it verbatim produces unusable links, and `rules.md` §6 requires every reported posting to be a working link.
- **🔴 DEDUP on the 32-hex `uniqueID`/`originalURL`, NOT `requisitionID`** — see "Global / common rules".
- **🟢 FIRST-CLASS PER-LOCATION `country` / `countryAbbr` IN `jobSearch.jobs[].locations[]` — no text parsing, and it defuses the `, XX` trap by construction.** Confirmed on **Marriott** (a global hotel operator, so it matters most there) and on **McDonald's — populated on 213/213 records, 2026-08-27.** This is the field that kills the documented McDonald's `, DE` → Delaware false positive; use it rather than parsing the location string.
  - ⚠️ **The case is INCONSISTENT — some records emit lowercase `mx` where others emit `MX`** (both Ciudad de México). Compare case-insensitively. A `== "US"` test happens to be safe; a `!= "us"` test is not.
- **🔴 MCDONALD'S: `curl --compressed` IS MANDATORY — Python `requests` is hard-403'd by Akamai even with the full documented browser header set.** Measured 2026-08-27: **30/30 `requests` calls returned HTTP 403 / 384-byte `Access Denied`** carrying the complete Accept / Accept-Language / Sec-Fetch-* / sec-ch-ua / Upgrade-Insecure-Requests set, while the **identical header set through `curl -sS -L --compressed` returned HTTP 200 on all 209 requests, zero 403s.** **The header set is NOT the whole story — a 403 here can mean the CLIENT, not the headers and not a block.** Same family as Progressive (`requests` 403 / `curl` 200) and Airbnb (`urllib` 404 / `curl` 200).
- **🔵 "STOPWORDS / SINGLE-LETTER KEYWORDS RETURN NOTHING" IS A PARADOX PROPERTY, NOT A GENERAL ONE — do not carry it off-platform** *(scoped 2026-09-02)*. The assumption was derived here, on McDonald's, and it holds on Paradox tenants. It is **false on Wendy's** (WordPress SSR), where `keyword=e` and `keyword=a` both report the full board total. **Whenever this recipe's behaviour is cited as a reason not to probe a keyword shape elsewhere, check the platform first.** → *Wendy's row.*
- **🔴 MCDONALD'S GARBAGE CONTROL IS NON-ZERO AND THE VALUE DRIFTS — a control cannot validate this endpoint.** `keyword=zzqqxxnotreal` → `totalJob=13`, `13`, then **`15`** across three consecutive runs. It is a property of the endpoint, and because the number itself moves it cannot even be used as a fixed sentinel.
- **🔴 THE "PAGE-1 CAP" IS FULLY DISPROVED — retire the assumption everywhere.** Path-based pagination (`/jobs/page/{N}`) is confirmed working on **McDonald's, Marriott, Nationwide and Stryker**; only the `?page={N}` **query param** is a no-op. **Wendy's is the sole genuine exception** (see its own entry) and it is not a Paradox site.
  - **🔴 ADD BARE `data` TO THE STANDING MARRIOTT SWEEP — it is +736 unique (32%), and the "impractical — 191 pages" note is WRONG.** *(Measured 2026-08-25 r2.)* Documented 10-keyword sweep = **2,268 unique / 305 requests**. Bare `data` paginated to exhaustion (`totalJob` 2,142, 215 pages) = **1,692 unique**. **The two barely overlap: `data` found 736 records the sweep never saw, and the sweep found 1,312 `data` never saw. Union = 3,004**, against the 2,284 documented. Expensive, but it is the single largest coverage contributor available. **Budget Marriott at ~520 requests, not ~294.**
    - **🔵 Marriott's `keyword=` is a GENUINE FILTER, so the "weak keyword ≈ full-board pagination" diagnostic is FALSE here — keywords PARTITION rather than overlap.** Paginating one broad keyword is not a cheaper substitute for the union; it is strictly worse than the union. **Test the diagnostic per-site; do not generalise it.** *(Contrast Hilton, where it was true and no keyword is needed at all.)*
    - Per-keyword `totalJob` drift re-confirmed as noise, not a health signal: `analytics` 1,223→1,177, `data platform` 785→767, `data warehouse` 424→427, `data` 1,905→2,142.
  - **🔴 Marriott rate-limits deep pagination with a 508-byte HTTP 403 that does NOT clear on retry** *(found 2026-08-25 r2 — the recipe previously warned only about transient 502s with a 524-byte body)*. All 24 failures in the `data` run were byte-identical 403s and survived 4 attempts with backoff at 8 concurrent workers. **Fix: fewer workers on any run past ~100 pages.** Up to ~240 `data` records were unrecovered, so **3,004 is a floor, not a ceiling.**
  - **🔴 THE 403 IS A SESSION-SCOPED QUOTA, NOT A PER-REQUEST RATE LIMIT — AND THAT CHANGES THE RECOVERY** *(established 2026-08-30)*. 4 of 673 requests failed (`analytics` pages 24–27), byte-identical **520 B**. They **survived 4 in-session retries with backoff at 6 workers** — retrying harder inside the session cannot work, because the session itself is what is exhausted. ✅ **A FRESH SESSION after a 20 s cooldown recovered all 4 on the FIRST attempt.** **Standing recovery: open a new session; do not retry in-session.**
    - ⚠️ **520 B is the FOURTH distinct 403 byte count and it has now REPRODUCED on a second consecutive run — body size stays DEAD as a signature here.** A repeat is not a stabilisation; key on the status code.
  - **🔴 Marriott has NO site-side page cap — the "capped at 18 pages / coverage caveat" note was WRONG and is deleted.** That figure was a run's own `MAXPAGES` constant being misread as a site limit. `careers.marriott.com/jobs/page/{N}?keyword=` returns **distinct 10-record pages at least to page 120** (spot-checked 18/19/25/40/60/120 — all HTTP 200, all distinct `requisitionID`s). **Correct rule: paginate to `ceil(totalJob/10)`.** Doing that on `analytics` (`totalJob` 1,284 → 129 pages), `data platform` (519) and `data warehouse` (377) took Marriott **808 → 2,127 unique**, against the 511 previously documented — a **4× coverage gain for 165 extra requests, zero failures.**
- **🔴 An explicit `Accept-Encoding` header BREAKS this recipe.** Sending `-H "Accept-Encoding: gzip, deflate, br"` *alongside* `--compressed` makes curl skip decompression — the response comes back HTTP 200 with an undecodable body that reads **exactly like a WAF block**. **Drop the explicit header and let `--compressed` set it.** The rest of the browser-header fingerprint below is correct and still required. Affects the McDonald's and Progressive recipes, which share that header list.
- **Backend varies by company — check what's actually behind Paradox before assuming coverage is capped at ~10 results:**
  - Nationwide and Stryker are both Workday-backed underneath (see Workday CXS section/quick-reference above) — bypass Paradox's page-1-only SSR entirely and hit the CXS API directly for full coverage, the same upgrade already applied to Adobe/Cigna/Humana/CVS. Confirmed working for Stryker (`stryker.wd1.myworkdayjobs.com/wday/cxs/stryker/StrykerCareers/jobs`, 130 fuzzy hits vs. Paradox's top-10-per-keyword cap) and Nationwide (`nationwide.wd1.myworkdayjobs.com/wday/cxs/nationwide/Nationwide_Career/jobs`).
  - Marriott's apply backend is Oracle Recruiting Cloud (apply links resolve to `ejwl.fa.us2.oraclecloud.com`) — not yet reverse-engineered as a direct REST API; Paradox's own path-based pagination was sufficient for Marriott's small result set (20 postings for the keyword query used).
  - McDonald's backend wasn't identified (the Akamai WAF blocks even the Paradox HTML without a full browser header set, see below) — Paradox SSR was the only viable path found so far.
- **McDonald's needs a full browser header set, not just a User-Agent string.** A bare `curl -A "<realistic UA>"` still gets a hard Akamai 403 (`Access Denied`, `Server: AkamaiGHost`). Required to get through: `Accept`, `Accept-Language`, `Sec-Fetch-Dest: document`, `Sec-Fetch-Mode: navigate`, `Sec-Fetch-Site: none`, `Sec-Fetch-User: ?1`, `Upgrade-Insecure-Requests: 1`, plus `--compressed` — and **no explicit `Accept-Encoding`**, per the trap above. Once past the WAF, the same `__PRELOAD_STATE__` SSR pattern applies, including for individual job-detail pages (which also carry a clean `application/ld+json` JobPosting block). The Akamai block is intermittent: it fired once on 2026-08-21 r1 and has not fired on any run since (all requests HTTP 200) — **retry rather than concluding a regression**, and equally do not assume it is gone.
- **🔴 McDonald's — `filter[category][0]=` is HARD-BLOCKED by Akamai; use the `?keyword=` sweep.** Both `?filter[category][0]=…` (with `curl -g`) and the percent-encoded form return **HTTP 403, `Server: AkamaiGHost`**, 384-byte `Access Denied` body, `Set-Cookie: AKA_A2=A`. The category filter is dead as a method. `?keyword=` still works with the same header set, and `jobSearch.totalJob` is accurate per keyword. Each keyword terminates on a recognizable constant **76,900-byte** empty-results shell. Sweep size matters: 4 keywords → 144 unique, 6 → 155, **10 → ~152-160**, **24 → 199** (the pooled figure drifts with the board; the keyword count is the method, the number is not).
  - **🔴 McDonald's `keyword=` is OR-SEMANTICS, not a phrase filter** *(found 2026-08-25 r2)*. A multi-word keyword returns **more** than any of its components: `analytics engineer` **131** > `analytics` **104** > `data engineer` **103** > `data` **68**, with `engineer` alone 58. **Actionable consequence: a single multi-word keyword string is itself a union query**, so `keyword=data analytics engineer analyst intelligence` should replace several sweep calls. Stopwords return nothing (`a` → 0, `the` → 0, `e` → 31), so no single broad term approaches the board — the weak-keyword/full-pagination diagnostic answers **NO** here, but for this more useful reason.
    - **🟢 MEASURED 2026-08-30 — THE OR-UNION STRATEGY HAS SATURATED THIS BOARD. STOP LENGTHENING THE KEYWORD LIST.** A single union string `data analytics analyst analysis intelligence insight` returned `totalJob` **170**, more than any component; `information architect business information` returned **166**. **35 keywords / 263 requests → 208 unique, against the prior run's 26 / 195 → 209 — the pool is FLAT while the list GREW by nine.** Adding keywords here now buys requests, not coverage. **Build union strings and keep the list short.**
    - ✅ `curl --compressed` returned HTTP 200 on all 263 requests, **zero Akamai 403s**; `countryAbbr` populated **208/208**.
    - **🔴 The garbage control DRIFTED AGAIN — `totalJob` 13 → 15 → 14. It remains non-zero, so a control still CANNOT validate this endpoint** in either direction; do not use it as a sentinel.
- **⚠️ McDonald's — `careers.mcdonalds.com/jobs` with NO `keyword=` param returns HTTP 200 but carries NO `window.__PRELOAD_STATE__` at all** (found 2026-08-24). The unfiltered board total is **not** readable the way Marriott's is, and the missing blob reads like a parse failure rather than a missing param. **Always pass a keyword.**
- Confirmed working for: Nationwide, Stryker, Marriott, McDonald's (all first confirmed 2026-08-06).

---

### Indeed (separate source — see `indeed.md` for the search config)
- **Two queries, run separately and merged client-side.** Never combine into one `title:(A OR B OR C OR D OR E)` list — Indeed's `title:()` operator does not reliably OR-combine 5 terms and collapses toward whichever dominates relevance ranking.
- Params: `fromage=7` (hard date filter), `l=Chicago, IL`, `radius=25`. **`&sort=date` is NOT reliably chronological** — don't use it.
- **Page 1 only.** Any `start>0` request hits a sign-in wall. There is no workaround. The `title:()` + `fromage=7` scoping is what keeps the result set inside one page.
- **🔴 THE PAGE-1 CEILING IS 15 RECORDS — NOT the `~20-25` the docs (and `indeed.md`'s flag threshold) assume.** Measured on both queries: q1 reported `totalJobCount: 20` and yielded **15**; q2 reported **19** and yielded **15**. **Consequence: `totalJobCount` above 15 means postings are being silently dropped, so 15 — not 20-25 — is the threshold to flag at.** The remedies are all query-side: narrow the radius from 25mi, tighten the title lists, or split into more and narrower queries.
- **🔴 DETECT THE PAGE-2 WALL BY THE `page-two-signin` MARKER IN THE FINAL URL, NOT BY THE STATUS CODE** (re-tested 2026-08-24). It used to surface as a bare HTTP 403. With `curl -L` it now **follows the redirect and returns HTTP 200** on the auth page (`secure.indeed.com/auth?...branding=page-two-signin`). Zero records are extracted either way, but a status-code check now reads the wall as a successful fetch of an empty page.
- Job data is in `window.mosaic.providerData["mosaic-provider-jobcards"]` → `"results":[...]`. This is a much larger blob than the `window.mosaic.initialData` config object earlier in the page; don't confuse them.
- **🔴 DO NOT zip parallel field lists by index.** The counts do not align (2026-08-20: 9 `jobkey` hits vs 13 `displayTitle` hits) and the mismatch is invisible in the output — you get a plausible-looking list of wrong rows. **Split on the `"jobkey":"` delimiter and take the LAST occurrence of each field in the preceding chunk** (JSON keys are alphabetical, so the record's own fields precede its `jobkey`). A working awk implementation is in `indeed.md`.
- If anchoring a field regex instead, `formattedLocation` must be anchored as `"feedId":\d+,"formattedLocation":"([^"]*)"` — a bare match picks up 3-4 unrelated hits per query from an `estimatedSalaryModel` / `employerResponsiveCardModel` section.
- Canonical URL: `https://www.indeed.com/viewjob?jk={jobkey}`. **Client matters:** Python `requests` gets 403; `curl` with the same UA succeeds.
- The same posting can legitimately appear under two `jobkey` values (staffing-agency reposts). Track both; don't de-dupe by title+company+location.

### TalentBrew / Radancy (Custom-labeled family)
Covers **Progressive, Sysco, United Health, Lyra Health, Quest Diagnostics, Comcast**.
- This family tends to have a fuzzy/unreliable `keyword=` search and pagination that silently drops the keyword filter after page 1.
- **`/sitemap.xml` is the full-listing bypass** and should be the primary method where pagination is unreliable. Canonical job URLs are `/job/{city}/{slug}/{orgId}/{jobId}`. ⚠️ **The sitemap is NOT universally available on this family any more — see Quest, where all three sitemap paths return a silent HTTP 200 with zero `<loc>`.** Where it is dead, the replacement is **unfiltered HTML pagination** (`/search-jobs?p={N}`), which Lyra and Quest both run on.
- **🔴🔴 THE UNFILTERED-HTML-PAGINATION CARD MARKUP IS *NOT* IDENTICAL ACROSS THIS FAMILY — DO NOT COPY ONE TENANT'S PARSER TO ANOTHER** *(found 2026-08-29; the file previously asserted Quest used "the same card markup as Lyra", which is FALSE and is deleted)*. **Lyra's location class is `job-location`; Quest's is `job-location-info`.** A parser carrying Lyra's class against Quest **parsed 2,148 records with an EMPTY location on every single one** — and because an empty location is *ambiguous*, not an error, the ambiguous-location rule floods the report with falsely-flagged rows instead of failing loudly. **Portable pattern: `class="job-location(?:-info)?"`.**
  - **🔴 STANDING ASSERTION FOR EVERY RADANCY-FAMILY SCRAPER: COUNT THE RECORDS WITH AN EMPTY LOCATION AND TREAT A NONZERO COUNT AS A DEFECT.** Not a warning, not a flag — a defect that stops the board. This is the only cheap check that catches a class-name drift, and the failure it catches is invisible in every other signal (the page returns 200, the card count is right, `distinctkeys` is healthy). Titles and IDs parsing correctly proves nothing about the location field.
  - **🔴 CARD PARSERS MUST NOT BE LAST-WINS — MERGE BY PREFERRING THE RICHER RECORD** *(found 2026-08-30 at Quest)*. **A job id appears TWICE on a Quest page**: the real card, plus a **bare anchor with no `<h2>` and no location span**. A last-wins dict assignment overwrote **3 good records with empty ones**, and it presented as `EMPTY_LOC=3` — i.e. **wearing the class-drift defect's signature.** Key on the id but keep the record with the most populated fields.
  - **⚠️ COROLLARY — the "nonzero empty-location = defect" rule is RIGHT but does NOT discriminate CAUSE, so read the magnitude:** a **class drift** gives ~100% empty (2,148 of 2,148); a **merge bug** gives a handful (3 of 2,150). Same alarm, different repair — check the ratio before assuming which one you have.
- **Slug filters must be UNANCHORED substring matches** — an anchored `/data` prefix silently undercounted United Health 126 → 20 by missing mid-slug matches like `senior-data-engineer`. Use `data|analyt|business-intelligence|bi-developer` as a substring. Quest needs the same breadth (`data|analytic|business-intelligence`): its tracked BI match's slug is `developer-business-intelligence`, which a narrow `data-engineer|data-analyst` pattern misses entirely.
- **🔴 `warehouse` must NEVER enter a slug filter on a distributor.** Sysco is a food distributor; a broad slug filter including `warehouse` produced 146 candidates of which ~125 were Warehouse Order Selector/Clerk roles. **Anchor it as `data warehouse` or drop it.** The narrow filter (`data|analyt|business-intelligence|bi-developer`) returns **exactly 1** slug across the whole Sysco sitemap — verified twice, most recently across all **1,332** `/job/` URLs, and that single hit is an already-tracked internship — so **a Sysco "zero new data roles" result is real, not an under-fetch.**
- **Tooling:** PowerShell `[regex]::Matches` on `Get-Content -Raw` handles a ~5MB sitemap in seconds where Bash `grep -o` hung twice.
- Some tenants redirect the sitemap (Sysco `/sitemap.xml` → `/en/sitemap.xml`) — **always use `curl -L`**, or you get a 301 with a 0-byte body that reads exactly like a method regression.
- **🔴 Sysco — use `https://careers.sysco.com/en/sitemap-jobs.xml`, not `/sitemap.xml`.** `/sitemap.xml` 301s to `/en/sitemap.xml`, which returned a reproducible **HTTP 504 Gateway Time-out** (534-byte nginx body) on 2026-08-21. `sitemap-jobs.xml` is healthy and is the standing primary — 5,575 `<loc>` / **1,339 `/job/` URLs** on 2026-08-24, no 504, in line with the historical 1,330-1,345.
  - ⚠️ **Decoy: `careers.sysco.com/sitemap_index.xml` returns HTTP 200 / ~14.8KB but the body is the HTML careers page with ZERO `<loc>` elements** — same 200-means-nothing family as the SmartRecruiters token trap.
- **🔴 A TalentBrew sitemap fallback is DEDUP-BLIND when the employer's real ATS is elsewhere — see the Comcast entry.** TalentBrew numeric IDs have no correspondence to Workday `R######` reqs, so every posting reads as new.

Tested and rejected: `/jobs/?page=N` → always page 1; `/jobs/2/` → 403; `/jobs/page/2/` → 404. **Do not use the roster or keyword-path URLs** — `/search/remote_work/remote/jobs/` returns 13 postings against a ~284-294 board (5%), and `/search/data/jobs/` returns **0** cards (the keyword-path form is dead). **Card markup — four things are load-bearing:** **🔴 the `href` is ABSOLUTE, not relative** — it is `https://careers.progressive.com/jobs/{id}-{slug}/`, so a regex written against a bare `/jobs/…` path **matches zero**; **accept an optional `https://careers.progressive.com` prefix.** This fails as a silent **"0 postings," not a fetch error** — the page still returns HTTP 200 and the `Showing 1-25 of {N} results` marker still parses fine, so nothing anywhere looks wrong. Job links are `/jobs/{id}-{slug}/` **not** `/job/…` (filtering on `/job/` returns zero); the heading is `<h3 class="heading-4 space-none">` and the **`heading-4` class is required** (the page carries 5 decoy `<h3 class="space-none">` facet headings); location is bare text after `<span class="hide">Location: </span>` running to `</div>`, city and state on separate whitespace-padded lines needing whitespace collapsing. Board size reads as `Showing 1-25 of {N} results`. Coverage ~93-95% — the result set re-orders between requests (FacetWP-class, same as Airbnb), not a hard boundary. **Dedup: key on the leading `\d{5,}` prefix of the last path segment** — confirmed working against the documented slug drift. **🔴 REPEAT-PASS RECONCILIATION IS STANDING METHOD HERE (established 2026-08-27, homed 2026-08-27 r2 — it had been living only in a corrections section and was nearly lost when that section was folded down).** A single pass under-covers ~14%: measured **244/285 = 85.6%**, *below* the documented 91.5–95% band. **Two repeat passes over pages 1–12 (24 requests) took it to 282/285 = 98.9%.** Do **not** treat a sub-90% single pass as in-band — run the repeat passes and reconcile. On 2026-08-28 r2 the 403 reappeared only as a **transient mid-sweep failure at a fourth byte count, 5,888 B**, one request of 36, which the repeat pass absorbed: the block is no longer all-or-nothing and **body size is dead as a signature here**.)* **The flip-flop pattern continues — retry every scan regardless of last outcome, and never delete the repeat-pass method while it is blocked.** **🔴 THE REPEAT PASS IS CONFIRMED LOAD-BEARING AGAIN on 2026-08-29: pass 1 ALONE was 245/288 = 85.1%**, below the documented band, exactly as in the 2026-08-27 measurement — do not treat a sub-90% single pass as in-band. **🟢 Cheap board-total marker: `<meta name="description" content="Search 288 Careers available at Progressive.">`** — read the count from it and assert `unique == that number`. | `/search/jobs?page={N}` | **Working (2026-08-29, 288/288; flip-flops — retry every scan)** |

No usable location field on either page. **Extraction regex fixed 2026-08-11**: the payload double-escapes quotes, wrapping each record in literal backslash-quotes — the working pattern is `\"(title)\",\"(YYYY-MM-DD)\",\"https://www.shopify.com/careers?ashby_jid=(uuid)\"` (note the `\"` not `"`). This superseded the 2026-08-08 plain-quote pattern, which itself had regressed by 2026-08-10 r2 to needing a keyword-substring workaround. **🔴 THAT TRIPLE RECOVERS ONLY 38/47 — THE DATE MUST BE OPTIONAL.** *(The earlier "46/47, one title-less record" figure was itself an undercount and is corrected.)* **Nine records emit `title, url` with NO date between them** — the shape is `\"TITLE\",\"https://www.shopify.com/careers?ashby_jid={uuid}\"`. **Fix: make the date group optional — `\"TITLE\",(?:\"DATE\",)?\"URL\"`** — which, together with the bare-URL fallback below, gives **47/47**. ⚠️ **Caveat: the optional-date pattern mis-captures one record (`3f5d85c0`) with the title `externalLink`**, because that record sits inside the payload's field-name block; a **key-name blocklist (`externalLink`, `applyLink`, `jobId`) cleans it up.** A title-less record is skipped by the client-side title filter and fails toward a false negative with nothing in the output to show for it. ## Per-company gotchas — required IDs, parse traps and title conventions

Recovered from the scan narrative on 2026-08-20 r2 before it was moved to `scan-history.md`. **Several of these are the only record of a value the documented method needs** — without them the current recipe for that company cannot be executed at all. Fold each into the quick-reference row above as those rows get touched.

### Required values that exist nowhere else
- **Capital One — `jobFamilyGroup` facet IDs.** Technology = `a12c70bf789e105802e9e79458dc29ab`, Analysis = `a12c70bf789e105802e9caf800542991`. **⚠️ These are a supplement, not the method — the "facet pool is the sole documented method" framing is superseded.** Full empty-`searchText` pagination is what Capital One runs on: **~95 pages, terminating on a genuinely zero-new-ID page, with unique == page-1 `total` == 1,862 exactly.** A result near half that is a `total:0` stop or a page cap in the helper, not a site change — see the Workday CXS recipe. The IDs are kept so they are not re-derived from scratch.
- **CVS — "Data and Analytics" facet ID** = `e65dbadf6a50100168ed7f2a693c0001`. Technology = `e65dbadf6a50100168ed86fe4cf50001`.
- **American Public Education — working `LoadSearchResults` POST body** = `{"opportunitySearch":{"Top":25,"Skip":0}}`. **The `opportunitySearch` wrapper key is required**; a bare `{}` returns an empty shell. Cross-confirm a genuine-zero board via `GetOpportunityMatchCount`.
- **Cigna — facet ID recovered, then RETIRED.** The "Data & Analytics" `jobFamilyGroup` ID is `b7947bbbfff201d41a750216390c8516`. **It is recorded for provenance only — do not use it.** It undercounted on every scan since 2026-08-11 and was replaced by full empty-`searchText` CXS pagination on 2026-08-24 (559 postings, 29 pages, no wraparound). Keeping the ID here stops it being re-derived as a fresh lead.

### Dedup traps (all fail silently toward false negatives)

> 🔴🔴 **2026-08-31 — SIX URL-DRIFT SHAPES, 16 ALREADY-TRACKED POSTINGS THAT PURE URL DEDUP RE-REPORTED AS BRAND NEW.** Found independently by four dispatch groups. **These fail toward FALSE POSITIVES (re-reporting), which is the visible direction and the one `rules.md` §3 prefers — but at 16 items a run it is real noise.** Two were fixed in `bin/jobscan_dedup.py` the same day; **four are NOT fixed because fixing them requires keying on an extracted req ID, which `rules.md` §3 deliberately retired on 2026-08-30 so genuine reposts stay visible. That is the operator's call, and it is open.**
>
> | Shape | Company | N | Status |
> |---|---|---|---|
> | Board now appends `?gh_jid={id}` restating the last path segment; tracker holds the bare path | Scopely | 7 | ✅ **fixed** — `gh_jid` is dropped **only when its value equals the last path segment**, which is provably identity-preserving. A `gh_jid` saying anything else still keys, so this is NOT the whitelist mistake that collapsed 11 Shopify postings. |
> | Trailing `/apply` on the Workday path; tracker holds both forms for the same req | Adobe | 3 | ✅ **fixed** — dropped only when ≥2 segments remain, so it can never eat the only meaningful segment. |
> | Cross-host Greenhouse namespace: `pantheon.io/about/careers/detail?gh_jid={id}` vs `job-boards.greenhouse.io/pantheon/jobs/{id}` | Pantheon | 3 | ❌ **open — needs ID keying** |
> | iCIMS path prefix: served `/careers/jobs/{id}`, stored `/jobs/{id}` | Ulta | 1 | ❌ **open — needs ID keying** |
> | iCIMS trailing slug **plus a retitle**: served `/jobs/490486` ("Sr Data Engineer (Remote)"), stored `/jobs/490486/senior-data-engineer` ("Senior Data Engineer"). **The title drifted too, so a title-guarded match does not catch it** — the req ID has to key alone. | Ulta | 1 | ❌ **open — needs ID keying** |
> | Slug drift on a stable numeric id: `...-testing-analyst-lead` → `...-testing-**data**-analyst-lead` | Progressive | 1 | ❌ **open — needs ID keying** |
>
> **🔴 Procedure for ANY future normalization change, from `jobscan_dedup`'s own docstring and exercised properly on 2026-08-31: run `collapse_report()` against the live tracker BEFORE and AFTER, and verify every newly-appearing group is genuinely the same posting.** On this change collapse groups went **25 → 28**, all three new groups were the same Adobe req under its `/apply` and non-`/apply` forms, **zero groups were lost, and normalization stayed idempotent** (0 non-idempotent keys across 1,943 stored URLs). A transform that collapses two genuinely distinct postings is a silent suppress and must be reverted, not shipped.

- **Pfizer — dual URL scheme.** `seen_jobs.json` still stores entries under the pre-2026-08-10 scheme (`pfizer.com/about/careers/job/{id}`) while the live site serves Workday CXS URLs with a `-N` revision suffix. **Strip the `-N` and match the base numeric ID across BOTH URL forms.** This is not resolved by "having fixed it once" — it recurs every scan and produced 3 false "new core" positives when missed.
- **Humana — the req ID is a MIDDLE path segment** (`/job/{reqId}/{slug}`), not a trailing one. An `_ID`-anchored matcher misses it entirely because there is no `_` separator. Scan all path segments for an ID-shaped token — specifically, **index any bare segment matching `^[A-Za-z]{0,3}[-_.]?\d[\w.\-]*$`**, the same fix that covers Adobe's Phenom form (see "Global / common rules").
- **Empower — the `bulletFields[0]` req ID contains a literal dot** (`R.0061425`) where the URL slug, `externalPath` and `seen_jobs.json` all use the dot-free form. Strip the dot before comparing.
- **Pantheon — canonical URL drift, and a CONSTANT-WHOLE-PATH collapse.** The live site serves `pantheon.io/about/careers/detail?gh_jid={id}`; `seen_jobs.json` stores `job-boards.greenhouse.io/pantheon/jobs/{id}`. Same ID, so `gh_jid`-based dedup handles it — URL-based dedup would not. **🔴 Worse, the normalised `host+path` is IDENTICAL for all 31 Pantheon postings**, so adding it as a key collapses the company and suppresses real reqs (8056205 and 8056238 were both lost this way). **Only add a `host+path` key when the path contains a digit** — see "Global / common rules". Same shape at **Cribl** (`/job-detail/`) and **Coalition** (`/job-posting`).

### Title-matching conventions (silent misses, per-company and mutually contradictory)
> ⚠️ These two rules conflict by design — **title matching is per-company, not global.** Don't unify them.
- **eBay — use SUBSTRING matching (`data eng`).** It posts abbreviated titles ("Competitive Data Eng & Analyst", "Staff Data Eng TCGplayer") that a full-word `data engineer` regex misses.
- **Pfizer — use WORD-BOUNDARY matching (`\bdata engineer\b`).** A substring match false-positives on "Senior Associate, Meta**Data Engineer**ing".
- **Humana — searches for "business intelligence" must include the full phrase**, and Humana's own convention is "Business **Information** Architect", not "Intelligence". Broaden to `business information|information architect` or real core matches drop out (R-408582, R-409275 were both missed this way).
- **American Express — "Manager" appears abbreviated as "Mgr"** ("Sr Mgr-Data Engineering"). A spelled-out-only regex misclassifies silently.
- **United Health — title-classification split. 🔴 CORRECTED 2026-08-21: the old seniority-based half of this rule is DEAD.** It used to read *"titles containing 'Data Analytics'/'Analytics' behind a Director/Manager/VP/Consultant prefix default to **borderline** absent a JD read."* That directly contradicts `rules.md` §1 as rewritten 2026-08-20 r2 (*"Never push a posting to borderline because of its level… strip the seniority word and ask whether what remains is a data role"*), and `rules.md` wins. **Apply the §1 strip test to UHG like everywhere else:** "Senior Manager Data Engineering" → core, "Senior Manager Data Analytics / EDW Reporting" → core, "Associate Director, Data Quality and Controls" → core. Only genuine business/strategy/domain subjects go borderline ("Senior Clinical Informatics Analyst", "VP Physician Review and Market Insights").
  - **This changes only how NEW UHG postings are classified.** The ~12 already-persisted UHG entries carrying the old borderline tag stay exactly as they are — they are already-seen, so they suppress regardless, and `rules.md` §3 forbids re-reporting or re-tagging them. Do not backfill.
  - The old rule was justified as "what keeps the largest borderline pool in the project stable scan to scan." That stability is now supplied by borderline persistence (§2) instead, which is what makes the seniority shortcut both wrong and unnecessary.
- **Capital One — known keyword false positives** to exclude explicitly: "Databolt" (a product name), standalone "Advisor" titles, and "Enterprise Data"/"Data Security" used as org or product suffixes on non-data roles.
- **🔴 `\bbi\b` MATCHES "bi-weekly".** Two J&J *CAR-T Manufacturing Supervisor* reqs entered the candidate pool solely because their shift schedules read "(Thursday to Saturday **bi**-weekly)". **Anchor it as `\bbi\b(?!-)`**, or require a following boundary that is not a hyphen. The `\bBI\b` token itself stays in the regex — 13+ tracked titles use the abbreviation.
- **🔴 In pharma, "Analytical"/"Analytics" means analytical CHEMISTRY.** At J&J, `Sr Scientist, Analytics`, `Senior Principal Scientist – Scientific Integration, Analytical Development`, `Senior Principal Scientist, Bioanalytical Outsourcing` and `Principal Scientist, Imaging Analytics` are all **wet-lab** roles. **Rule: `Scientist` + `Analytic*` with no data noun is lab work, not analytics.**
- **🔴 J&J MISSPELLS "Scientist" in live titles** — `Senior Data **Sciencist** - AI & Scientific Applications` (R-092134) evaded a `data scientist` exclusion regex entirely. **Exclusion regexes should carry `scien(ti|ci)st`**, or fall back on a bare `data scien` prefix.
- **🔴 NON-ENGLISH INTERNSHIP WORDS — the internship exclusion must be MULTILINGUAL.** `Werkstudent` / `Praktikum` are German working-student and internship roles that a plain `\bintern\b` regex does not reach — two J&J ones matched the sweep. **But `\bintern\b|werkstudent|praktikum|praktikant|working student` is still not wide enough:** it misses Portuguese `estágio`, French `stagiaire` / `alternance`, German `Ausbildung` / `Pflichtpraktikum`, and plain `apprentice`. **Marsh R_361040 "Estágio em Seguros de Crédito - Analytics"** carries a data token, so it clears the borderline bar on title and would have been reported — it is an internship and is excluded. **Marsh generates this family in bulk (17 titles), so it is not a one-off.** Carry the full set: `\bintern\b|werkstudent|praktikum|praktikant|pflichtpraktikum|ausbildung|working student|estágio|estagio|stagiaire|alternance|apprentice|graduaat|bachelorstage|afstudeer`.
  - **Dutch/Flemish added 2026-08-25** — Pfizer `4961266` *"Graduaat / Bachelorstage: Optimalisatie van **data**-opzoeking…"* carries a data token, clears the borderline bar and is an internship. Re-confirmed firing 2026-08-25 r2 (it is also Belgium, so excluded twice over). **Pfizer generates this family the way Marsh generates the Portuguese one.**
  - ⚠️ **The exclusion reaches US postings too — it is a title rule, not a location one.** The Hartford opened a four-posting `Tech & Data Program Summer 2027` block on 2026-08-25 r2, including two US `Data Engineer Intern` reqs (R2626610 Columbus, R2626103-1 Hartford) that are fully title-qualifying. All excluded per `rules.md` §1. **Hartford now generates this shape in blocks of four, seasonally.**
- **Quest Diagnostics — `data entry` and `pre-analytical` are both false friends.** Four clerical "Data Entry Clerk/Operator" postings match a literal `data` filter, and 9+ "Pre-Analytical Assistant" lab-specimen roles match `analy`. **Anchor both out of Quest's slug filter.**
- **Home Depot — bare `warehouse` is a title false friend** (same family as Lyra's `dbt`): 640+ "Warehouse Associate / Clerk / Operations" retail roles match it. **Anchor it as `data warehouse`.** Same rule applies at Sysco, more severely — see the TalentBrew recipe.
- **Lyra Health — `dbt` is a false friend** (Dialectical Behavior Therapy, "Licensed DBT Psychologist"). Drop it from Lyra's filter or anchor it `\bdbt\b`.
- **⚠️ Residual regex gap: `\bdata\b` does not match glued forms like "DataOps".** Cheap fix: drop the trailing word boundary. Theoretical so far — no live title has been missed this way — but the fix costs nothing.

### Fetch quirks
- **Marriott — the Paradox `?keyword=` param needs `%20`-encoded spaces.** Combining a bare-space keyword with `filter[country][0]=` **silently drops the keyword filter entirely** and returns the unfiltered board (386 unfiltered vs. 51 correctly filtered). Marriott's keyword search also fuzzy-matches individual words, not phrases — full manual title filtering is still required. *(Pagination depth: see the Marriott row — there is no page cap.)*
- **🔴 Wendy's — `keyword=` DOES filter server-side. The old "keyword is cosmetic, fetch once and filter client-side" note was WRONG and is deleted.** `?keyword=data&category=corporate` returns 1 posting, not the whole board. The keyword sweep is what works around the page-1 cap of 10. See the Wendy's row for the `&ndash;` entity trap and the ≤2-worker rate limit.
- **Marsh — `searchText` does not honor quoted "exact phrase" queries.** A quoted `"data analyst"` returned an implausible 590, the same fuzzy OR-match as unquoted. *(This is one of the reasons the sweep was replaced by full board pagination — see the Marsh row.)*
- **American Express — the job-detail endpoint returns 0 bytes** on at least some reqs (confirmed on 26008191, 26008278, 26012055). JD verification for AmEx borderline items is currently not possible via `recruitingCEJobRequisitions/{id}`. No workaround found.
- **🔴 PetSmart — the old method conflict stays retired, but for a new reason: the feed FLIPS.** *(Both the "category facet is more reliable" position and the later "keywords= scopes correctly, sweep instead" position are dead, and so is the 2026-08-21 r2 "both methods work, either is safe" ruling.)* The feed dropped its entire corporate surface and restored it inside one month. **The durable method is not a choice of param but the garbage control: run `categories=Home%20Office` alongside `categories=zzqqxxnotreal` every scan and trust the result only when the control is zero.** See the PetSmart row and the iCIMS recipe.
- **Gopuff runs on Lever, not Greenhouse.** A Greenhouse-token probe 404s cleanly there and can be misread as a block — same family as the SmartRecruiters 200-means-nothing trap and the Cribl Ashby decoy.

### Companies with no quick-reference row yet
**DNC** is referenced by the recipes above but has no row in the table. Its details are in the Rippling recipe and in `companies.md`; add a row when next touched. *(Marsh and Ryze rows were added 2026-08-24.)*

### Known prose/tracker drift — ✅ RECONCILED 2026-08-24, re-verified 2026-08-24 r2, keep it that way

**Current tracker size (the figure prose must agree with): `seen_jobs.json` = 1,870 postings (878 core / 992 borderline); `indeed_seen.json` = 143 (126 core / 17 borderline).** As of **2026-08-27**, recomputed from the files after that run's write (+41), the Indeed write (+3) and the InStride Health roster-add baseline (+1). *(The `jobs` map also holds two legacy string values, `_comment_r4` and `_comment_2026-08-07`, so `len(jobs)` reads **1,872** — type-guard with `isinstance(v, dict)`.)* ⚠️ **The previous version of this line also had its own arithmetic wrong** — it claimed 1,828 postings but `len(jobs)` reads "1,752", which cannot both be true given two string values. **Recompute BOTH numbers from the file in the same pass; do not hand-edit one of them.**
> ⚠️ **The previous figure here (1,845 / 118) was WRONG in a specific and instructive way, flagged independently by two dispatch groups on 2026-08-25 r2.** The live file held 1,727 at the time and the 118 gap was *exactly* the then-size of `indeed_seen.json` — i.e. **the line had conflated the two trackers**, not lost entries. It also predated the 2026-08-25 Apple purge (−209) and non-US purge (−14). **Recompute this line from the file at write time; never carry a previous run's number forward or add the two trackers together.** **Update this line whenever the trackers are written**, so a later scan has one authoritative number to reconcile prose against instead of re-deriving it.

All four long-standing drift items are resolved. `companies.md`'s prose now matches `seen_jobs.json` for **Quest Diagnostics** 97939224720 (*Director of Data Fabric Platform*, core), **Ross Stores** *Senior Analyst, Business Intelligence* (core), **Foodsmart** (both *Director … Data Science* entries are core), and **Kohl's** (R467418 and R488937 *Senior Manager, Business Intelligence Engineer* both core). Each of those four rows now carries an explicit "`seen_jobs.json` is the sole authority" note.

Two findings from that reconciliation are worth keeping, because they show how drift goes wrong rather than just that it did:
- **Kohl's prose cited a borderline req ID (`R472537`) that the tracker does not hold at all**, and omitted R488937 entirely. Drift is not only stale *labels* — it invents and loses IDs. A prose ID is never evidence a posting exists.
- The drift had survived several scans because each scan re-read the prose and believed it. **`seen_jobs.json` is the ONLY authority for dedup** (`rules.md` §7); prose is a human-readable convenience and must never be consulted for a dedup or classification decision.

**Re-check this on any scan that rewrites `companies.md`.** The reconciliation is only durable if a later prose edit doesn't silently reintroduce a claim the tracker contradicts.

### Rescued from the `companies.md` run-log (2026-09-04)

`companies.md` column 4 accumulated a per-run counts log that also carried real method. The
counts were deleted when that column was removed; **these are the durable rules extracted from
it first.** Run narrative (pass sequences, percentages, per-run tier counts) was dropped
deliberately. Some entries restate a rule already given above — kept rather than risk dropping
one that only ever lived in that column.


**Allstate**

- it moves DOWN as well as up -- do not read a smaller number as a coverage regression, check it against the page-1 total.
**Geico**

- **Reading quoted history inside a newest-first status cell as current state is a repeatable misread of this file.** The rule stands unchanged: `unique == page-1 total` plus "repeat until a pass adds nothing", never a fixed pass count.
**Progressive**

- ⚠️ NEW -- unique (329) EXCEEDED `total_entries` (328), so total_entries is a LIVE MARKER on a moving board, not a constant; assert against the value read in the same pass, exactly as Airbnb's `total_rows` needed.
- The structured `country` field is the only thing keeping those 7 out of AMBIGUOUS; a caller reading the location TEXT would flag a real US posting instead of reporting it.
- curl + full browser header set, 0 403s, 0 page failures; stdout captured as BYTES and decoded utf-8 explicitly (no text=True).
- Use the ROOT-LEVEL control; a `/jobs/` control silently reads a live site as unblocked-but-meaningless.
- (1,195 B sits in the same band as r2's 1,193 B; the 2,674 B reading remains the other pole — the control's size oscillates, key on the status.) 🔴 NEW CLIENT TRAP — `subprocess.run(..., text=True)` decodes curl's stdout with the Windows locale codepage (cp1252) and mangles every UTF-8 en-dash in a title: 'Field Claims Adjuster – Auto Damage' arrived as 'Adjuster â€“ Auto Damage'.
- 🔴 **TWO further silent parse traps on the JSON API:** there is **no `total_pages` key** (only `current_page` / `per_page` / `total_entries` / `entries`), so a `total_pages`-based stop never fires at all; and there is **no `url` field** — the canonical URL must be BUILT as `/jobs/{id}-{permalink}/`.
- 🔴 3,327 B IS AN EIGHTH DISTINCT 403 BYTE BAND (3,120 / 3,379 / 3,528 / 5,696 / 5,888 / 5,924 / 5,967 on record) -- BODY SIZE STAYS DEAD AS A SIGNATURE; key on the 403 plus the title.
- The control's SIZE is not a constant either -- key on the status code.
- 🔴🔴 **THE CLIENT TRAP APPLIES TO THE JSON API TOO — THIS WAS NOT DOCUMENTED AND COST A GROUP TIME.** Python `requests` **with the full browser header set** is hard-403'd by Cloudflare (403 / **5,967 B** `Just a moment...`, a **sixth** distinct byte band); `curl --compressed` with the *identical* headers returns 200 on every request.
- 🟢 **MIGRATE TO THE JSON API: `careers.progressive.com/search/jobs.json?page={N}`** (25/page, path from `window.csns.paths`) returns `total_entries` **plus a structured location with a first-class `country` field** — it retires the href-absolute trap, the location-text parse and the meta-count scrape in one step.
- **Body size is dead as a signature here; detect on the 403 plus the `Just a moment...` title.** The block is no longer all-or-nothing and a repeat pass absorbs the transient form.* ⚠️ This site flip-flops between blocked and working, sometimes within the same day — always retry, and **never delete the repeat-pass method while it is blocked.** 🔴 The roster URL above sees only ~13 of the board; real pagination is `careers.progressive.com/search/jobs?page={N}` (25/page).
**Humana**

- 🔴 DURABLE, PREVIOUSLY UNDOCUMENTED DRIFT SHAPE: 13 of the 20 stored careers.humana.com keys use an OLD req-first public form '/us/en/job/{REQ}/{Slug}', while both the current public form and the Workday form put the req LAST ('/job/{Location}/{Slug}_{REQ}').
- The two normalize differently and the old form is NOT reconstructible caller-side, because Workday triples the dash for ' - ' ('Data-Engineer---Platform...') where the stored slug has a single dash.
- Do not go looking for a specific req id, and do not treat a previously-null id's disappearance as a fix.
- The shape is still NOT closed (13 of 20 stored `careers.humana.com` keys remain req-first and the served form is not reconstructible from the stored one) but produced no false positive.
**CVS**

- 🟢 **The r2 no-canonical-URL defect DID NOT RECUR** — every emitted record carries a full canonical URL, asserted before returning, and zero pooled records had a null `externalPath`.
- Unfiltered board RE-MEASURED at 19,457 (r2 19,640, r1 19,254) -- it keeps moving, so re-measure every run and never carry the figure forward.
- UNFILTERED BOARD RE-MEASURED AT 19,640 (r1 re-measured 19,254; the older ~19,000 figure is superseded again -- re-measure it every run, it is not stable).
- 🔴 THE `Technology` FACET REPRODUCED ITS r1 RESULT EXACTLY: 141 records, +7 the 55-token sweep never served; `Data and Analytics` (36 records) added 0.

**J&J**

- Enumeration keyed on the FULL `externalPath` rather than a parsed req id, which sidesteps the `_R-093799` (J&J) / `_R_355062` (Marsh) req-id-key class outright -- in enumeration a key collision can only LOSE records, and the full path cannot collide.
- 🟢 THE 09-01 EXTRACTOR FIX HOLDS FOR A THIRD RUN: the enumeration key is everything after the LAST underscore with the revision suffix INCLUDED, so the internal-hyphen req ids (_R-093799) do not collapse onto the key `R`.
- **Catchable only on the page-1 total.** Standing rule: the **enumeration** key is everything after the last underscore, revision suffix included — revision folding belongs in dedup, where a collision is harmless.

- METHOD CORRECTION, AND IT COST A FULL RE-FETCH: the location matcher must NOT be anchored on a class containing the word 'location' -- Airbnb's card markup has no such class.
- Working method, and what should be written into the WordPress+FacetWP recipe: split the page on the list-item marker 'li class="inner-grid', take the position anchor for id and title, then read every span inside the following col-span-3 div, discarding the bullet entity.
- 🔴 **This board flips often enough that its status must be PROBED EVERY RUN and never inherited** — nine flips is no longer an anomaly, it is the board's behaviour.
- HTTP 429 / 564 B nginx, byte-identical across 5 probes including `--http1.1` and the full browser header set, on the FIRST request of the session.** Not volume-induced.
- 🔴 **The FIRST request of the session 429'd — this is NOT volume-induced**, and neither `--http1.1` nor the full browser header set helps.
- **Keep the repeat-pass method and run passes until one adds nothing.** 🔴 **`total_rows` TRACKS A LIVE BOARD — it read 169 early in the run and 170 late.
- It is a MARKER, not a constant**, so assert against the value read in the same pass, never a carried-forward one.
- **This site has now flipped in both directions four times — retry every scan and never assume either direction holds.**
**NBC Universal**

- location.country read as a structured LOWERCASE ISO-2 and uppercased, never round-tripped through a display string, so no non-US posting leaked into the report flagged.
- 🔴 **`location.country` IS A LOWERCASE ISO-2 CODE (`us`, `gb`) — READ IT AS STRUCTURED DATA AND UPPERCASE IT; NEVER JOIN IT INTO A DISPLAY STRING.** `London, gb` reads AMBIGUOUS to a `, XX` matcher, which would push a UK posting into the report **flagged rather than excluded**.

- **Build the post-redirect `/careers/jobs/{id}` form.** This is a caller-side canonical fix, **NOT** a widening of `jobscan_dedup`.
- **Keep it as a CONTROL only**; it churns badly (9 → 15 → 28 → 45 → 46) and could never be primary.
- 🔴 **Short-token dedup trap: grepping the tracker for `ulta` returns 39 entries, overwhelmingly `cons`*ulta*`nt`/`cons`*ulta*`tion`** inside Allstate/Cigna/CVS/UHG — key on `careers.ulta.com`.
**McDonald's**

- Every union's served count equalled its own totalJob EXCEPT `modeler modeling scientist science platform`, which served 130 against totalJob 129 -- mid-sweep board drift, not a fetch defect; the direction (served > total) is worth recording because the reverse would read as an under-fetch.
- 🟢 **NEW: the job-DETAIL path HAS a discriminating garbage control even though the listing endpoint does not** — `/{slug}/job/{32-HEX}` → 200 / 26,288 B vs a same-shaped bogus path → 404 / 68,847 B.
- **Scope the standing "a control cannot validate this endpoint" note to the `/jobs` LISTING route only.** The same check reproduced the client trap (403 under a thin header set, 200 under the full set seconds later).
- The gap is req 4941 'Administrative Assistant' (uniqueID FCE4D7DAB1CB57600F0DCC58F85A062B), whose locations[0].countryAbbr is an EMPTY STRING rather than a missing key -- so a truthiness test catches it but a `'countryAbbr' in loc` test would NOT.
- Nothing was lost (the title reaches no tier, and its location string 'Chicago, 60607, US' resolves US on the trailing-US rule), but the field is NOT a complete oracle on this tenant and the assertion must stay.
**Wendy's**

- No detail fetch needed -- listing cards carry title and location directly.
- Read together, the two runs say the CONCURRENCY is the trigger, not the request count: 254 sequential requests beat 304 requests at 2 workers outright.
- Garbage control: 0 cards, NO 'Viewing' string, 55,906 B -- matching r2 and again contradicting r1's claim that the control carries the string; the CARD COUNT is the reliable zero signal, the 'Viewing' string is not stable in either direction.
- ⚠ unique == board total is NOT a completeness proof (the Ulta lesson); it is a consistency check that happened to pass.
- So titles do NOT require a detail fetch, and NEITHER the ld+json `&quot;` unescaping trap NOR the `<h1>` marketing-banner trap is on this path at all.
- The card count is the reliable zero signal either way; the 'Viewing' string's presence is not stable and should not be used as a discriminator in either direction.
- 🔴 **NEW PARSE TRAP, AND THE BUG WAS CALLER-SIDE: DO NOT HTML-UNESCAPE THE `ld+json` BLOB BEFORE `json.loads`.** The `description` carries `&quot;`, which unescapes to a bare `"` and **terminates the JSON string** — the first pass resolved **1 of 31** titles and looked exactly like a site regression.
**Marriott**

- Custom Paradox.ai keyword sweep (12 keywords: data, analytics, data platform, database, data warehouse, business intelligence, reporting, tableau, sql, python, power bi, snowflake), full pagination per keyword to ceil(totalJob/10), 2-worker-equivalent sequential fetch with session reset every 65 requests + 15s cooldown.
- 3,779 unique over the 12-keyword smallest-first sweep (3,607 on 09-03 r1); board_total is legitimately null -- there is no readable board-wide total.
- A `== "US"` equality test is a **SILENT US DROP**; test truthiness and fall back to the full-name `country` field.
- 🔴 NEW AND DURABLE -- THE MARRIOTT REPEAT PASS NEEDS **FOUR** PASSES, NOT ONE, AND THE STOP CONDITION MUST BE 'REPEAT UNTIL A PASS ADDS NOTHING' HERE TOO.
- **Order the sweep; do not compensate with retries.** 🔴🔴 **THE 520-BYTE 403 IS A SESSION-SCOPED QUOTA, NOT A PER-REQUEST RATE LIMIT — AND THAT CHANGES THE RECOVERY.** The 4 failures (`analytics` pages 24–27) were byte-identical 520 B and **survived 4 in-session retries with backoff at 6 workers** — retrying harder inside the session cannot work, because the session itself is what is exhausted.
**Alteryx**

- Product Marketing Manager, CDP & Data Connectivity` is not excluded; it simply does not clear the core or analyst bar on its own merits, so it is not routed either.
**eBay**

- Its non-candidacy was never proven and now CANNOT be -- do not record it as resolved, and do not re-open it as a live gap.] A targeted searchText returned total:1 against a control total:0 (positive existence proof) but title, externalPath and locationsText all stay null, and the public jobs.ebayinc.com search returns totalHits 0 for the req -- the same shape R0069441 showed.
- NOTE THE LIMIT OF THE FACET TRIAGE: a job FAMILY is not a title, so unlike Allstate's 'Adjusters/Adjuster Support Group' this does NOT prove the record was never a tier candidate -- it only makes it unlikely.
- All candidates resolved on the structured country.descriptor, never a city-name guess, so the Dublin trap stays defused.
- 3 slug-vs-title disagreements, all previously documented and all reproducing: R0074504; R0073094-1 (slug Competitive-Data-Engineering-Manager scores lead_manager, title 'Competitive Data Eng & Analyst' scores analyst); and R0052824, which reproduces the DELETION mechanism -- the slug carries Apprenticeship so is_excluded() is True, while the served title 'DoD SkillBridge - Honor Role' is not excluded.
- SDE, Distributed Databases' is NOT silently binned -- bin/jobscan_match prefix-matches, so `Databases` satisfies the `database` stem and the title reaches borderline.
- All 5 eBay core candidates were resolved non-US on structured country fields, never a city-name guess.
- 🟢 Location resolver scoped to post-bar candidates: **14 detail requests.** ⚠️ **BOARD IS FALLING AND IT IS REAL: 464 → 379 → 375 → 356.** Verified again — `unique == page-1 total`, fully monotonic, zero duplicate `externalPath` — so **do not chase the smaller number as a helper artefact or an under-fetch.** 🔴🔴 **PLURAL HEAD NOUN SILENT BIN LIVE HERE: `Sr.
- SDE, Distributed Databases` CLEARED THE NET AND WAS DROPPED BY THE BAR**, because `Database` is a qualifying token but `Databases` is not.
**Comcast**

- The same three requisitions reproduce exactly: R431946 (non-US Chennai, core on both stored and served title, unpersistable because non-US postings are never persisted, so it can NEVER self-heal -- 6th run), R438534 (slug-vs-title FALSE POSITIVE: slug Senior-Full-Stack-Data-Engineer scores core, served 'Senior Full Stack Development (.NET + React)' scores no tier), and R441127 (LOSING-direction boundary-crossing retitle: stored 'Compliance Analytics Analyst 3' = borderline, served 'Senior Compliance Analyst' = no tier at all).
- R438753 'Data Center Operations and Design Engineer' is a live worked example of why 'never dedup on title+location, always on the req ID' is load-bearing: the tracker holds TWO byte-identical titles under DIFFERENT req ids (R441717-1, R441727) at Hillsboro OR, while this is R438753 at Northlake IL -- a genuinely distinct requisition.
- Live: R431946 (non-US, 4th run, **unpersistable so it can never self-heal**), R439913-1, plus R438534 and R441127 which never reach the bar.
- 🔴🔴 THE wd5→wd115 DRIFT BACKLOG IS DOWN FROM 3 TO 1, AND THE ONE THAT REMAINS CAN NEVER SELF-HEAL.
- This is exactly the United Health shape ('the fix cannot reach a non-US posting'), now confirmed at a second employer.
- 🔴 NOT FOLDED -- comcast is not in DRIFT_SHAPES and a scan does not close an open shape.
- They are reported because a prior run judged them IN and the served title is unchanged -- re-judging a stored entry in the opposite direction would be the 'never re-litigate a stored entry' failure running backwards.
- **First live proof that persisting the drifted URL closes a shape that is NOT in `DRIFT_SHAPES`.** `comcast` still must not be folded.
- **Read a migration signature's own body before treating a 410 as terminal.** wd5 still returns 410 `ERR_TENANT_MIGRATED`; wd115's garbage slug gives a clean 404 / 143 B, so a zero here is now trustworthy.
- 🔴 **Two NEW probe traps:** (1) a bare **GET** (no POST body) on the CXS `/jobs` path returns **HTTP 400 for every slug**, valid or invalid — it erases the discriminator entirely, so the probe must be the POST with the standard body; (2) the front-end stub returns a **different** pair (`/Comcast_Careers` → 200, `/External` → 500) from the CXS POST pair — use the CXS pair, not the front-end one.
**Vanta**

- address.postalAddress.addressCountry read as the structured field and present on every post-bar candidate; it carries the ISO code 'US' here, not the full name, per the Hinge Health finding.
**Marsh**

- Standing method: at Marsh a null CXS record is a CLOSED req; resolve it on the public page's 410, count it against the board total, and do NOT add it to the candidate set.
- `placement` remains an **insurance-broking** term here — never match bare `\bplacement\b`.
- METHOD CHANGE WORTH HOMING: the pagination key was the WHOLE `externalPath`, not a parsed req ID.
- 🔴 **NEW FALSE FRIEND: `placement` is an INSURANCE-BROKING term here** — `Placement Analyst` (R_358750, Clearwater FL, **US**) is insurance placement, not a student placement; match programme shapes, never bare `placement`.
- ⚠️ **Location field is `City - Building`, so a `, XX` regex sends nearly the whole board to UNKNOWN** — resolve `N Locations` items with a targeted `searchText={reqId}` POST.
- ⚠️ This site exposed the **shared-keyword-pool stop-rule bug**: each keyword run must keep its own seen set or pagination truncates silently.

**InStride Health**

- ⚠️ **THE "garbage control → clean 404 / 38 B, so the zero is trustworthy" CLAIM ONCE RECORDED HERE IS WRONG AND IS CORRECTED:** `/v1/boards/{token}/jobs` returns a **byte-identical 404 / 38 B for a garbage token AND a genuinely dead board**, so it proves only that the endpoint is up.
- ⚠️ **Every posting is `Remote, US` or `Remote, {STATE}`** — no non-US exposure, and the `, XX` trap is irrelevant here because the location field is already explicit.

## Adding a new company
When a new company is added to `companies.md`, do a quick fetch/method investigation (following the per-ATS recipes above as a starting point) and add its entry to the quick-reference table here — don't leave it to be re-derived next scan.
